### [1] [Time-Dependent Integrability from Gauge Theory, I](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02648)
*Shota Komatsu,Jun-ichi Sakamoto,Anders Wallberg,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unifying framework using generalized 4D Chern-Simons theory to construct solvable, time-dependent integrable field theories; it establishes that their time dependence matches the one-loop RG flow, preserves Lax integrability and inverse scattering solvability, and extends to ultralocal/non-ultralocal systems and 2D dilaton gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide exact solvable settings for non-equilibrium physics and benchmark quantum simulations via real-time dynamics—domains where exact results are scarce.

Method: Generalizes four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory by replacing the holomorphic one-form with a spacetime-dependent one-form, enabling systematic construction of time-dependent classically integrable field theories while preserving Lax structure and inverse scattering solvability.

Result: A universal correspondence between allowed time dependence and one-loop renormalization group flow; construction applies to both ultralocal and non-ultralocal theories; yields new time-dependent sigma models and a broad class of classically integrable 2D dilaton gravity theories.

Conclusion: Generalized 4D Chern-Simons theory provides a natural, unifying framework for generating solvable time-dependent integrable systems—bridging quantum simulation benchmarks, RG flow, classical integrability, and gravitational duals.

Abstract: Solvable time-dependent systems provide important settings for studying non-equilibrium physics, where exact results are rare. They are also useful for benchmarking quantum simulations, which can directly probe real-time dynamics beyond the reach of conventional numerical approaches. In this paper, we show that the four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory offers a natural and unifying framework for constructing such systems. Focusing on classically integrable field theories, we consider a generalization of the four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory in which the usual holomorphic one-form is replaced by a more general, spacetime-dependent one-form. This yields a systematic procedure for generating time-dependent integrable field theories and establishes a universal relation: for every theory obtained in this way, the allowed time dependence coincides with the one-loop renormalization group flow. Despite the explicit time dependence, these theories retain Lax integrability and remain solvable by inverse scattering methods. Our construction applies to both ultralocal and non-ultralocal theories and extends previously known time-dependent sigma models to a much broader class of integrable systems. It also admits rewriting as dilaton gravity coupled to matter, producing a large family of classically integrable dilaton gravity theories in two dimensions. We also comment on connections to time-dependent integrable models studied recently in condensed matter physics and non-autonomous integrable systems arising from dimensionally-reduced Einstein gravity.

</details>


### [2] [The giant graviton expansion in AdS$_5\times$SE$_5$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02658)
*Alfredo González Lezcano,Leopoldo A. Pando Zayas,Augniva Ray*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies giant graviton-like D3-branes in AdS₅×SE₅ type IIB supergravity backgrounds, focusing on supersymmetric three-cycles in 5D Sasaki–Einstein (SE₅) manifolds. It formulates the dynamics via the transverse Kähler potential, identifies maximal angular momentum configurations with its divergence loci, and quantizes excitations as a Fock–Darwin problem with conical deficit—generalizing the Landau problem on S⁵. It computes a protected contribution to the superconformal index and verifies agreement with the finite-N index of the dual N=1 SCFT for AdS₅×T¹,¹.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how geometric data of Sasaki–Einstein manifolds SE₅ is encoded in the dynamics and quantization of supersymmetric giant gravitons, and to compute protected observables (e.g., superconformal index) that bridge bulk supergravity and dual SCFTs beyond the maximally symmetric AdS₅×S⁵ case.

Method: Analyzes probe D3-branes wrapping supersymmetric 3-cycles in SE₅ and rotating along the Reeb vector field; uses transverse Kähler geometry to characterize maximal angular momentum configurations; performs canonical quantization of small fluctuations yielding a Fock–Darwin Hamiltonian with conical deficit; computes the protected contribution to the superconformal index via trace over the resulting Hilbert space.

Result: Giant graviton configurations with maximal angular momentum correspond to divergences of the transverse Kähler potential; their quantized fluctuations yield a Fock–Darwin spectrum dependent on SE₅ geometry (via external potential and conical deficit); the computed protected index matches the finite-N result for the dual N=1 quiver CFT in the AdS₅×T¹,¹ case.

Conclusion: The geometry of SE₅ is holographically encoded in the quantum mechanics of giant gravitons—not only in the background metric but in the effective potential and topological defect (conical deficit) of their fluctuation spectrum; this provides a precise, computable link between Sasaki–Einstein geometry and protected SCFT data.

Abstract: We study giant graviton-like D3-branes as probe configurations in type IIB supergravity backgrounds AdS$_5\times$SE$_5$, with emphasis on the structure of the five-dimensional Sasaki-Einstein manifolds SE$_5$. These configurations wrap supersymmetric three-cycles in SE$_5$ and rotate at the speed of light along the Reeb direction. We formulate the general problem in terms of the transverse Kähler potential and show that configurations carrying maximal angular momentum can be described by loci where the transverse Kähler potential diverges in suitably chosen coordinates. We quantize a particular set of excitations, similar to those considered in the AdS$_5\times S^5$ case, and show that they are governed by a Fock-Darwin problem with a conical deficit, which generalizes the Landau problem obtained for $S^5$. Succinctly, the information about the geometry of SE$_5$ is encoded in the form of the external potential and the conical deficit. We compute a protected contribution to the superconformal index arising from quantized fluctuations of supersymmetric giant graviton configurations. For the explicit case of AdS$_5\times T^{1,1}$, we recover the finite-N protected index of the dual quiver ${\cal N}=1$ superconformal field theory in the sector captured by a non-Abelian generalization of the quantized degrees of freedom.

</details>


### [3] [Holographic Learning from Fermionic Spectra: Application to Strange Metal Phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02861)
*Hong-Zhi Xiao,Zhan-Zhi He,Zhuo-Yu Xian,Shao-Feng Wu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Neural ODE-based framework to infer bulk black hole geometry and gauge potential from boundary fermionic spectral data; validated on holographic models, it is applied to cuprate strange metals, revealing an emergent near-AdS₂×ℝ² geometry with negligible gauge potential, while highlighting limitations in temperature sensitivity and the need for additional thermodynamic constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge top-down holographic models and bottom-up experimental data in strongly correlated quantum matter—specifically, to reconstruct bulk gravitational duals (metric and gauge field) of strange metals directly from measured fermionic spectral functions without assuming a predefined action.

Method: A data-driven Neural ODE framework trained to invert the holographic dictionary: inputs are boundary fermionic spectral functions (from ARPES-calibrated extended power-law liquid model); outputs are bulk metric components (including conformal factor Ω(z)) and U(1) gauge potential Aₜ(z); validation performed on Einstein–Maxwell and Gubser–Rocha black hole backgrounds; semi-holographic interpretation used for cuprate (Pb,Bi)₂Sr₂₋ₓLaₓCuO₆₊δ.

Result: The strange metal phase at low-T and near-optimal doping is well-described by a near-AdS₂×ℝ² black hole geometry with qAₜ ∼ 10⁻⁴ eV (nearly vanishing gauge potential); massless probe fermions render reconstruction insensitive to Ω(z), implying spectral data alone cannot fix thermodynamics (e.g., specific heat); framework remains robust across doping but degrades significantly with increasing temperature—both reconstruction loss and qAₜ grow sharply above low-T regime.

Conclusion: Neural ODEs enable effective holographic reconstruction from spectral data, revealing universal near-AdS₂ geometry in cuprate strange metals; however, the insensitivity of massless fermions to the conformal factor necessitates complementary thermodynamic input, and temperature is the principal practical limitation for applicability.

Abstract: We develop a data-driven framework based on Neural ODEs that learns the bulk metric functions and the $U(1)$ gauge potential of a static, planar-symmetric black hole from boundary fermionic spectral functions. After validating the framework on the Einstein--Maxwell and Gubser--Rocha models with high accuracy, we apply it to the strange metal phase of the cuprate $\mathrm{(Pb,Bi)_2Sr_{2-x}La_xCuO_{6+δ}}$ within a semi-holographic setting, taking as input the spectral data generated from the extended power-law liquid (PLL) model calibrated by angle-resolved photoemission measurements. At low temperatures and near-optimal doping, we show that the normalized extended PLL model can be well described by an effective representative of a conformal class near $\mathrm{AdS}_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}$ black holes with a nearly vanishing gauge potential ($qA_t\sim10^{-4}\,\mathrm{eV}$). A key structural observation is that our probe fermion is massless and therefore insensitive to the conformal factor $Ω(z)$. This further implies that fixing the macroscopic thermodynamics such as the electronic specific heat would require independent input beyond the fermionic spectra. We also delineate the applicability boundaries of our framework: the learned effective model remains viable across doping at low temperatures, with the loss increasing mildly as the samples move away from the marginal Fermi liquid point toward the overdoped side; the principal limitation is temperature, where both the loss and $qA_t$ grow substantially.

</details>


### [4] [Pre-Strings Lectures on Artificial Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02905)
*James Halverson*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper presents lecture notes from the Pre-Strings 2026 school, structured across three days: Day 1 introduces neural networks through a field-theoretic lens; Day 2 introduces Neural Network Field Theory (NN-FT), linking architectures and parameter densities to physical theories (e.g., Liouville theory, string amplitudes, critical dimension); Day 3 explores AI applications in string theory—including agentic workflows, physics-informed NNs for Calabi-Yau metrics, RL for landscape/knot search, and interpretable learning for conjecture generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge deep learning and theoretical physics—particularly string theory—by developing mutual frameworks: using field-theoretic concepts to understand neural networks (Day 1), formalizing field theories via neural architectures (Day 2), and deploying modern AI techniques to advance string-theoretic research (Day 3).

Method: Lecture-based exposition integrating theoretical physics (quantum field theory, conformal field theory, string theory) with machine learning concepts (neural network expressivity, parameter statistics, dynamics, agentic AI, physics-informed NNs, reinforcement learning, interpretability). Formal constructions include defining field theories via neural architectures and parameter densities (NN-FT); applications involve supervised/unsupervised/RL methods tailored to geometric and topological problems in string theory.

Result: Establishment of NN-FT as a rigorous framework yielding concrete results: universality theorem for neural field theories; neural realization of Liouville CFT; reproduction of classic string amplitudes; identification of topological sectors and Kosterlitz-Thouless transition analogs; derivation of Ward identities, anomalies, and the bosonic string’s critical dimension d=26. Also demonstrates practical AI advances: Calabi-Yau metric learning, RL-driven landscape exploration, knot classification, and conjecture-generation via interpretable models.

Conclusion: Neural networks and string theory are deeply synergistic: field-theoretic perspectives enrich ML foundations, while AI methods accelerate discovery and interpretation in quantum gravity and geometry. The NN-FT formalism provides a unifying language, and applied AI is transforming how string-theoretic problems are formulated and solved.

Abstract: These notes are based on six lectures given over three days at the Pre-Strings 2026 school in Shanghai. Day 1 develops neural network essentials, organized around the expressivity, statistics, and dynamics of neural networks, presented with a field-theoretic lens. Day 2 develops a neural network approach to field theory (NN-FT), in which a field theory is defined by a network architecture and a density on its parameters, and surveys recent results. Examples include a universality theorem, a neural network realization of Liouville theory, famous string amplitudes, topological sectors and the Kosterlitz-Thouless transition, Ward identities and anomalies, and a new derivation of the critical dimension of the bosonic string. Day 3 turns the lens around and covers applied AI for string theory: agentic workflows that are changing how the other techniques are implemented, physics-informed neural networks and Calabi-Yau metrics, reinforcement learning and search in the string landscape and in knot theory, and interpretable supervised learning with an eye towards conjecture generation.

</details>


### [5] [Null-strings Gauged, Reloaded and Quantized, I: Canonical Quantization in the Light-Cone Gauge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02970)
*Ida M. Rasulian,M. M. Sheikh-Jabbari,H. Yavartanoo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies light-cone quantization of null-strings in D-dimensional flat spacetime, revealing a previously overlooked Carroll-Weyl gauge symmetry that reduces physical degrees of freedom to (D−3), yields a discrete spectrum, and eliminates the need for a critical dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To properly quantize null-strings by incorporating their full gauge symmetry—specifically the Carroll-Weyl symmetry—previously neglected in standard light-cone gauge treatments.

Method: Light-cone quantization with explicit implementation of the Carroll-Weyl gauge symmetry; constraint analysis; reduction of phase space; Schrödinger representation quantization on the reduced configuration space.

Result: Physical phase space reduced to (D−3) propagating degrees of freedom; discrete energy spectrum for null-strings; no critical dimension required—valid for arbitrary D; construction of ground-state and excited-state wavefunctions.

Conclusion: Null-strings admit a consistent quantum formulation without critical dimension constraints, owing to their intrinsic Carroll-Weyl symmetry, leading to a discrete spectrum and a well-defined Hilbert space built on a reduced phase space.

Abstract: We study the light-cone quantization of null-strings in $D$ dimensional flat target-space. Incorporating the essential new gauge symmetry and the associated constraint structure of the null-string, allows one to solve for one more degree of freedom (DoF) compared to the standard light-cone gauge, reducing the physical phase space to $(D-3)$ propagating DoF. Quantization is formulated directly in the Schrödinger representation, leading to a Hilbert space of wavefunctions on the reduced configuration space. The space of physical states is built on a reduced phase space associated with the corrected gauge structure. We discuss the ground-state wavefunction and a generic class of excited states. As a direct consequence of the overlooked Carroll-Weyl gauge symmetry of the null-strings, we find the remarkable and perhaps unexpected result that null-strings exhibit a discrete spectrum. Our analysis indicate that there is no critical dimension for null-strings, and $D$ can be arbitrary.

</details>


### [6] [Graph Neural Networks for the Graphical Bootstrap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03109)
*Rigers Aliaj,Gabriele Dian,Reza Doobary,Paul Heslop*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies graph neural networks—including graph transformers—to classify over 20 million graphs from planar N=4 SYM correlator computations, achieving near-perfect ROC AUC (99.996%) and enabling up to 85.5% data reduction in denominator graphs versus traditional graphical bootstrap; interpretability is explored via model embeddings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the computational bottleneck in high-order perturbative calculations of correlators in planar N=4 super-Yang–Mills theory—specifically, classifying massive sets of Feynman-like graphs arising from the graphical bootstrap method.

Method: Benchmarked multiple graph neural network architectures (including graph transformers) on a large-scale graph classification task; evaluated generalization to larger graphs; quantified data reduction efficacy by measuring redundancy elimination in denominator graphs; analyzed learned graph embeddings for interpretability.

Result: Achieved 99.996% ROC AUC on graph classification; demonstrated robust generalization to unseen larger graphs; attained up to 85.5% shrinkage of redundant denominator graph data; revealed interpretable structure in latent embeddings correlated with physical graph properties.

Conclusion: Graph neural networks—especially graph transformers—are highly effective and scalable for symbolic-graph classification in theoretical physics contexts, offering both predictive accuracy and computational acceleration, while embedding analysis suggests potential for physics-informed model introspection.

Abstract: We study a graph classification problem involving over 20 million graphs, arising from high-order perturbative computations of correlators in planar $\mathcal{N}=4$ super-Yang--Mills, a model closely related to the theory of the strong nuclear force. We benchmark graph neural networks, including graph transformers, achieving robust generalization to larger graphs with up to $99.996\%$ ROC AUC. Then, we analyze how the models can be used to gain a computational speedup compared to the traditional graphical bootstrap algorithm, through shrinking the redundant data by up to $85.5\%$ at the level of denominator graphs. Finally, we study the embeddings of the models to investigate their interpretability.

</details>


### [7] [Exact Schwarzian Metric Factor and Holographic Wilson-Loop Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03120)
*Miguel Tierz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper exactly computes the radial metric factor h(ζ) in the AdS₂ throat of an extremal Reissner–Nordström AdS₅ black brane using Schwarzian averaging, revealing a Gaussian integral representation with coth(πy) kernel; it leverages Mordell’s identity to obtain an exact Appell–Lerch q/q*-series, uncovering a nonperturbative scale e^{-π²C/ζ} missed by near-boundary expansions, and proves complete monotonicity of G₀(ζ) = h(ζ)/ζ²—ruling out spurious confining minima—while computing exact variance of the Schwarzian kernel and confirming algebraic Wilson-loop screening E(L) ∼ −κ_IR/L² characteristic of a semi-local quantum liquid.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in near-boundary approximations of the AdS₂ throat geometry induced by Schwarzian averaging, particularly whether apparent confining minima are physical or artifacts, and to identify nonperturbative scales and IR physics (e.g., Wilson-loop screening) beyond finite truncations.

Method: Exact evaluation of the radial metric factor h(ζ) via Schwarzian averaging; derivation of a Gaussian integral representation against coth(πy); application of Mordell's identity to obtain an exact Appell–Lerch q/q*-series; computation of the third parametric derivative yielding the quasimodular Eisenstein series E₂; proof of complete monotonicity of G₀(ζ) = h(ζ)/ζ² via integral analysis; exact calculation of the relative variance of the Schwarzian kernel; application to the temporal rectangular Wilson loop in the exact throat geometry.

Result: An exact, all-depth expression for h(ζ) as a Gaussian integral and its Appell–Lerch series; identification of the nonperturbative scale e^{-π²C/ζ}; proof that G₀(ζ) is completely monotone—hence no true confining minimum exists; exact relative variance quantifying averaging error; algebraic Wilson-loop screening E(L) ∼ −κ_IR/L²; numerical confirmation that the screened saddle dominates over truncated alternatives, and that the constant-force regime in fourth-order truncation is unphysical.

Conclusion: Finite near-boundary truncations of the Schwarzian-averaged AdS₂ throat produce spurious confining minima; the exact geometry is free of such features and exhibits algebraic confinement screening consistent with a semi-local quantum liquid IR. The nonperturbative scale and quasimodular structure (E₂) are essential for capturing full quantum gravitational effects in the throat.

Abstract: We evaluate exactly the radial metric factor $h(ζ)$ generated by Schwarzian averaging in the AdS$_2$ throat of an extremal Reissner--Nordström AdS$_5$ black brane. The result is a Gaussian integral against $\coth(πy)$, valid at all radial depths, which Mordell's identity turns into an exact Appell--Lerch $q/q^\ast$-series representation. The dual series identifies the nonperturbative scale $e^{-π^2 C/ζ}$ missed by any finite near-boundary truncation. The third parametric derivative required by the evaluation generates the quasimodular Eisenstein series $E_2$, absent from the classical Mordell identity. From the integral representation we prove that $\mathcal{G}_0(ζ):=h(ζ)/ζ^2$ is completely monotone and hence has no interior minimum, so any confining minimum produced by a finite near-boundary truncation is an artifact. We also compute the exact relative variance of the Schwarzian kernel, which makes the averaged-metric approximation error quantitative and shows that the absence of a confining minimum is robust across moment-based effective geometries. Applied to the temporal rectangular Wilson loop, the exact throat gives algebraic screening, $E(L)\sim -κ_{\rm IR}/L^2$, the Wilson-loop diagnostic of the semi-local quantum-liquid IR of the extremal RN brane. A numerical check in a simple matched geometry confirms that the screened saddle is the dominant string configuration, and an exact-versus-truncated force comparison shows that the apparent constant-force regime of the fourth-order truncation is not a feature of the exact geometry.

</details>


### [8] [Singularities, Entropy and the Arrow of Time, {\it or} Is CRT a Gauge Symmetry in Quantum Gravity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03199)
*T. Banks*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper investigates whether the discrete CRT transformation is a gauge symmetry in quantum gravity, concluding it is generally not, but can be interpreted as an asymptotic gauge symmetry in flat/AdS spacetimes or a spontaneously broken gauge symmetry in eternal de Sitter space—under specific conditions including an extended Chern-Simons formulation and time-independent quantum mechanics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the status of the discrete CRT transformation as a gauge symmetry in quantum gravity, given that 'quantum gravity' lacks a complete definition; the authors aim to assess CRT within well-defined gravitational frameworks (asymptotically flat, AdS, dS).

Method: Analytical investigation using asymptotic symmetry analysis, thermofield double formalism for eternal dS, and extension of the Chern-Simons formulation of 2+1D gravity; incorporates constraints from quantum measurement theory and semiclassical gravity on clock-based proper time measurements.

Result: CRT is not a fundamental gauge symmetry in general quantum gravity; it acts as an asymptotic gauge symmetry in asymptotically flat and AdS spacetimes, and—conditionally—as a spontaneously broken gauge symmetry in eternal dS when an 'Alice String' configuration is admitted in the thermofield double Hilbert space and quantum dynamics is time-independent.

Conclusion: CRT’s status as a gauge symmetry is framework-dependent and contingent on specific theoretical extensions and physical assumptions; its interpretation in dS requires nonstandard gravitational reformulation and imposes stringent limitations due to quantum measurement and semiclassical gravity constraints.

Abstract: We examine the question of whether the discrete transformation CRT is a gauge symmetry of ``Quantum Gravity". Since the phrase in quotes is not yet completely well defined, we first try to define specific frameworks in which one might ask the question. We find that the general answer is NO. In asymptotically flat and AdS spaces, CRT is an asymptotic gauge symmetry in the same sense that the continuous parts of the Poincare/AdS isometry groups and scalar internal symmetries are. In eternal dS space it can be considered a spontaneously broken gauge symmetry if a certain ``Alice String" configuration is allowed in the Thermofield double Hilbert space. This requires an extension of the conventional Chern-Simons rewriting of $2 + 1$ dimensional gravity. This interpretation only makes sense if we consider the underlying quantum mechanics to be time independent. General quantum measurement and semi-classical gravitational restrictions on measuring devices put {\it a priori} limits on the existence of detectors with clocks that can actually measure proper time along a classical dS geodesic.

</details>


### [9] [Falling through the horizon of a quantum black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03344)
*Victor Franken,Thomas G. Mertens,Bruno de S. L. Torres*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum-gravitational effects on an infalling detector near the horizon of a near-extremal black hole within quantum Jackiw-Teitelboim (JT) gravity, showing that gravitational dressing—motivated by geometric modular flow—enables local determination of horizon location and temperature, violating the equivalence principle without firewalls.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum gravity modifies local physics for infalling observers near black hole horizons, particularly whether equivalence principle violation occurs without firewall formation.

Method: Uses quantum JT gravity framework; constructs diffeomorphism-invariant local observables via gravitational dressing to the Schwarzian mode, extending exterior dressing (linked to geometric modular flow) into the black hole interior using the thermofield double state.

Result: Gravitational dressing couples near-horizon physics to the IR Schwarzian sector, enabling the infalling detector to locally measure horizon position and Hawking temperature—violating the equivalence principle—but no firewall is encountered.

Conclusion: Quantum gravitational dressing in near-extremal black holes permits horizon-localized measurements violating classical equivalence principle expectations, yet preserves smoothness—suggesting a resolution to the firewall paradox via boundary-anchored observables in holographic gravity.

Abstract: We study quantum-gravitational effects on the response of an infalling detector as it crosses the horizon of a near-extremal black hole in the framework of quantum JT gravity. These effects are incorporated via the gravitational dressing needed to define both the infalling trajectory and the local observables probed by the detector in a diffeomorphism-invariant way. In the black hole exterior, a preferred choice of dressing to the Schwarzian mode of JT gravity can be motivated in connection to geometric modular flow. We show how to extend this dressing to the black hole interior, defining local observables that are gravitationally dressed to both boundaries in the thermofield double state. The gravitational dressing connects the near-horizon region to the IR sector of the Schwarzian theory, leading to measurable effects as the horizon is approached. We find that the infalling detector is able to locally determine the location of the horizon and its temperature, violating the equivalence principle, but without encountering a firewall.

</details>


### [10] [Berry Picking: Random Wave Chaos Hierarchy for BPS Microstate Geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03434)
*Vladan Djukić,Milica Stepanović,Mihailo Čubrović*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates chaos in probe waves and geodesics across smooth supergravity backgrounds with varying supersymmetry and AdS throat length, finding wave chaos increases (toward black hole behavior) while geodesic motion becomes more regular; the dichotomy is explained via stable periodic orbits and diminishing KAM tori measure; dual CFT Rényi entropies show no universal trend, indicating BPS chaos hierarchy differs between bulk and field theory and does not smoothly extrapolate to black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how chaos manifests in smooth supergravity geometries interpolating between BPS states and black holes, and whether bulk chaos signatures correlate with field-theoretic complexity or extrapolate to black hole physics.

Method: Numerical and analytical study of wave chaos (via Berry random wave hypothesis compliance) and geodesic chaos (via Poincaré sections) in LLM geometries, supertubes, and superstrata; computation of Rényi entropies for dual CFT states in weak coupling.

Result: Wave chaos strengthens with reduced supersymmetry and longer AdS throats; geodesic motion becomes more regular; stable periodic orbits persist in long throats despite decreasing KAM tori measure; Rényi entropies lack universal dependence on supersymmetry or throat length.

Conclusion: The 'hierarchy of BPS chaos' behaves differently in the bulk (wave vs. geodesic dichotomy) and in the dual CFT (state-specific complexity), and neither exhibits simple continuity toward black hole physics.

Abstract: We estimate the strength of chaos of probe waves and probe geodesics in different smooth supergravity backgrounds of decreasing supersymmetry and/or increasing length of the AdS throat in the interior (LLM geometry, supertubes, superstrata). We find that the wave chaos becomes stronger and stronger with less supersymetry and longer throats; in other words, chaos becomes stronger as we approach black hole solutions. Geodesic motion shows the opposite trend, becoming more and more regular. Testing the wave chaos by its compliance with the Berry random wave hypothesis and the geodesic chaos by computing Poincare sections, we explain the dichotomy between wave and geodesic motion by the existence of stable periodic orbits inside long throats while the overall measure of KAM tori decreases. Computing the Renyi entropies for the dual CFT states in the weak coupling regime, we show that they do not have such universal trends and the complexity depends on the specifics of the state rather than just the amount of supersymmetry and throat length. We conclude that the hierarchy of BPS chaos works differently in the bulk and in field theory, and in either case cannot be simply extrapolated to black holes.

</details>


### [11] [Finite path integral limits work in cases where the perturbative series is not Borel summable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03439)
*Ariel Edery*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates perturbative expansions under finite path integral limits for a non-Borel summable double-well integral, showing that truncating integration bounds yields absolutely convergent series—both around the local maximum and a minimum—that recover the exact result in the infinite-limit limit; crucially, expansion about one minimum captures contributions from both minima.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether finite path integral limits enable convergent perturbative expansions for non-Borel summable systems—specifically those with non-trivial vacuum structure (e.g., double-well potentials)—where standard Borel resummation fails.

Method: Analyzes a prototypical non-Borel summable integral with double-well potential; constructs two perturbative series under finite integration limits [-L, L]: one expanded about the unstable critical point (origin), another about a stable minimum; proves absolute convergence and exact recovery in the L→∞ limit.

Result: Both perturbative series—about the local maximum and about one minimum—are absolutely convergent for finite L; in the L→∞ limit, each reproduces the exact analytical value of the full integral; notably, the expansion about a single minimum fully encodes the contribution of both degenerate vacua.

Conclusion: Finite path integral limits restore convergence and physical correctness even for non-Borel summable perturbative series arising from multi-vacuum systems, suggesting a robust alternative to Borel resummation for strongly coupled or topologically nontrivial quantum theories.

Abstract: The perturbative expansion in powers of the coupling of observables in quantum field theory and quantum mechanics is known to yield an asymptotic series. If the original physical system is well-behaved and a finite observable is expected, this can often be calculated via a Borel resummation of the asymptotic series. However, there are cases where a system is well-behaved and the series is not Borel summable. This typically occurs when the physical system has a non-trivial vacuum structure. It has recently been shown that if the perturbative series is carried out under finite path integral limits, one can obtain a convergent series that yields observables even at strong coupling. This was recently used to obtain the energy at strong coupling for the anharmonic oscillator. This is a Borel summable case so the question is whether finite path integral limits work when the series is not Borel summable. To begin answering this question we consider a simple non-Borel summable case: the series stemming from a basic integral where the function has a double-well shape and hence two minima. The integral has an exact analytical expression that the series can be compared to. Under finite integral limits that run from $-L$ to $L$, where $L$ is finite, positive and real, we develop two perturbative series in powers of the coupling: one by expanding the integral about the local maximum at the origin and the other by expanding it about one of the minima. In both cases, we obtain an absolutely convergent series and the series sums to the exact analytical expression of the original integral in the infinite $L$ limit. It is significant that a perturbative expansion about one of the minima reproduces the exact analytical expression because this implies that it captures the full effect of both minima.

</details>


### [12] [Quantum nucleation of black hole mimickers via chaos dominated tunneling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03452)
*Ulf Danielsson,Suvendu Giri,Vyshnav Mohan,Larus Thorlacius*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a quantum chaotic multichannel enhancement mechanism to dramatically amplify tunneling probability for formation of horizonless black hole mimickers—e.g., string-theoretic black shells—during gravitational collapse.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Horizonless ultracompact objects (black hole mimickers) require quantum tunneling at the Schwarzschild scale for formation, but standard tunneling rates are exponentially suppressed; hence, a physical enhancement mechanism is needed.

Method: Leverages quantum chaotic dynamics to induce multichannel enhancement of quantum transmission across tunneling barriers; applies this to nucleation of a string-theoretic black shell as a concrete example.

Result: Demonstrates that quantum chaos–driven multichannel interference can exponentially enhance tunneling probability, making formation of certain black hole mimickers dynamically viable.

Conclusion: Quantum chaotic multichannel effects provide a robust, generic mechanism for overcoming exponential suppression in horizonless object formation—supporting their plausibility as black hole alternatives in quantum gravity and beyond-Standard-Model scenarios.

Abstract: Black hole mimickers, ultracompact horizonless objects, have been proposed as alternatives to black holes in a variety of settings, including extensions of general relativity and scenarios involving matter sectors beyond the Standard Model. Their formation in gravitational collapse of matter requires quantum mechanical tunneling to occur on a length scale of the order of the Schwarzschild radius of the corresponding black hole. We propose a mechanism, based on multichannel enhancement catalyzed by quantum chaotic dynamics, that can dramatically amplify the quantum mechanical transmission across a tunneling barrier. We explore, in particular, how the nucleation of a string theoretic black shell is enhanced via this mechanism. We anticipate similar results to hold for other proposed black hole mimickers.

</details>


### [13] [Entanglement and geometric transitions in topological string theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03526)
*Gabriel Wong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper realizes the concept of bulk subsystems in quantum gravity within topological string theory, identifying open strings on entanglement branes as local holographic degrees of freedom; it constructs a subregion open string algebra, develops modular flow diagrammatics, verifies entanglement-gravity entropy matching, and connects to defect holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To define and realize bulk subsystems in quantum gravity that support local holographic degrees of freedom—specifically gravitational edge modes whose entanglement induces spacetime backreaction—within a concrete, tractable framework.

Method: Builds on topological string theory and prior work (Donnelly & Jiang) by introducing entanglement branes as dynamical entangling surfaces; constructs a subregion open string algebra; develops diagrammatic formalism for open string modular flow; computes entanglement entropy of open strings and compares with gravitational entropy of closed string backgrounds; relates geometric transitions to defect holography.

Result: Demonstrates that open strings ending on entanglement branes encode local holographic data; shows entanglement entropy of these open strings matches gravitational entropy of the dual closed string geometry; establishes a precise correspondence between geometric transitions of branes and local holographic encoding; links construction to defect holography.

Conclusion: Local holography in quantum gravity can be concretely realized via open strings on dynamical entanglement branes in topological string theory, providing a well-defined subregion structure where edge-mode entanglement drives spacetime fusion—thereby operationalizing bulk subsystems beyond AdS/CFT.

Abstract: How do we define a bulk subsystem in quantum gravity? In \cite{Wong:2025kpz}, we argued that such a subsystem must support local holographic degrees of freedom. These are gravitational edge modes, whose entanglement creates a backreaction that fuses together subregions of spacetime. In this work we give a realization of these ideas in topological string theory, building upon \cite{Donnelly:2020teo,Jiang:2020cqo}. In this theory, a subsystem for closed strings consists of open strings ending on entanglement branes, which play the role of a dynamical entangling surface. Local holography is implemented by the geometric transition of these branes. We define a subregion open string algebra and develop a diagrammatics for open string modular flow for arbitrary states and subregion. We check that the entanglement entropy of these open strings reproduces the gravitational entropy of the associated closed string background. Finally, we relate these local transitions to defect holography.

</details>


### [14] [Minimal Proper Time and Deterministic Microstates: Emergent Quantum Fields and Relativistic Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03605)
*Alessio Maiezza*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a top-down quantum field theory from a deterministic, pre-geometric causal substrate, showing how coarse-graining over microscopic event histories yields effective quantum dynamics with a running Planck constant and emergent spacetime geometry compatible with Einstein gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a microscopic, deterministic foundation for quantum field theory and relativistic spacetime by bridging pre-geometric causality and effective quantum behavior.

Method: Coarse-graining over deterministic, causally ordered microscopic event histories; identifying minimal proper-time steps as elementary updates; deriving effective Nambu-like dynamics and scale-dependent quantization.

Result: An emergent quantum field theory with ultraviolet dissipative corrections, a running Planck constant, vanishing dissipation in the infrared (recovering unitarity), and compatible emergent spacetime geometry satisfying Einstein gravity in the macroscopic limit.

Conclusion: Quantum behavior, minimal-scale structure, and relativistic spacetime share a common deterministic, pre-geometric origin rooted in causal event histories and coarse-graining.

Abstract: We develop a top-down counterpart of the minimal proper-time formulation of quantum field theory previously introduced as an effective bottom-up framework. Starting from a deterministic pre-geometric substrate of causally ordered events, we show how coarse-graining over microscopic histories leads, at low energies, to an effective Nambu-like quantum dynamics. The elementary deterministic update is identified with the minimal proper-time step, while the growth of coarse-grained equivalence classes controls both the ultraviolet dissipative correction and the scale dependence of the effective quantization strength, encoded in a running Planck constant. In this way, the proper-time cutoff kernel of the bottom-up formulation acquires a microscopic interpretation as the inverse growth of unresolved deterministic histories. In the infrared limit, the dissipative term vanishes and standard unitary quantum field theory is recovered. The same coarse-grained structure also provides a natural setting for an emergent relativistic spacetime geometry, compatible in the macroscopic limit with Einstein gravity. The resulting picture suggests a common deterministic origin for minimal-scale structure, quantum behavior, and relativistic spacetime.

</details>


### [15] [Quiver BPS Indices from Crystal Profiles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03617)
*Jiakang Bao,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives exact formulae for elliptic genera in 2D with N≥2 supersymmetry—and related indices in lower dimensions—expressed as discrete sums over crystal structures; each crystal's contribution depends only on boundary data ('molecules'), generalizing Nekrasov's partition function and revealing new combinatorics in crystal melting; it further studies the thermodynamic limit for N=(0,2) theories on toric CY4s, where a geometric projection of the Calabi–Yau emerges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize exact counting formulae (e.g., Nekrasov partition function) to broader supersymmetric settings—including elliptic genera in 2D N≥2 theories and their dimensional reductions—and to uncover deeper combinatorial and geometric structures underlying supersymmetric indices and crystal melting.

Method: Uses previously introduced crystal structures as combinatorial scaffolds; expresses elliptic genera and related indices as discrete sums over these crystals; isolates contributions via finite boundary substructures ('molecules'); applies thermodynamic limit analysis to crystal configurations arising from N=(0,2) theories on toric Calabi–Yau fourfolds.

Result: New exact formulae for elliptic genera (2D, N≥2), Witten indices (1D), and matrix-model partition functions (0D), all expressed as crystal sums with molecule-local contributions; vast generalization of Nekrasov’s Young-diagram enumeration; discovery of novel combinatorics in crystal melting; emergence of projected Calabi–Yau geometry in the thermodynamic limit for CY4-based theories.

Conclusion: Crystal structures provide a unifying combinatorial framework for supersymmetric indices across dimensions; the molecule-based localization reveals intrinsic boundary dependence; thermodynamic limits geometrize combinatorial data, linking crystal melting to emergent Calabi–Yau projections.

Abstract: We derive new exact formulae for elliptic genera in two spacetime dimensions with $\mathcal{N}\ge 2$ supercharges, as well as their one- and zero-dimensional counterparts (for Witten indices and matrix-model partition functions). Our results are written as a discrete sum over geometric/combinatorial structures of crystals introduced previously by the authors. The contribution from each crystal may be expressed in terms of the boundary data of a finite substructure of the crystal called the molecules. Our results provide vast generalisations of the celebrated Nekrasov partition function enumerated by the Young diagrams, and uncover new combinatorics underlying the crystal melting. We also analyse the thermodynamic limit of crystals arising from two-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=(0,2)$ theories associated with toric Calabi-Yau fourfolds, where a projection of the Calabi-Yau geometry emerges in the limit.

</details>


### [16] [Equal-charge projection of the $\mathcal{N}=4$ index: exact large-$N$ formula and finite-rank $U(3)$ coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03735)
*Miguel Tierz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the equal-charge limit of supersymmetric rotating AdS₅ black holes and the corresponding projected N=4 superconformal index; it proves exact large-N factorization of the index, identifies spin-dependent onset energies where degeneracies vanish at large N, reveals finite-N corrections violating classical black hole bounds, and confirms survival of the high-spin tail under exact charge projection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the microscopic counting of BPS black hole states—via the N=4 superconformal index—behaves under the physically relevant equal-charge constraint Q₁=Q₂=Q₃, and to reconcile large-N predictions with finite-N (e.g., U(3)) exact computations and giant-graviton contributions.

Method: Analytic manipulation of the multigraviton expansion of the N=4 superconformal index; projection onto equal R-charges via constant-term extraction in charge-difference fugacities; large-N asymptotics and exact U(3) enumeration; identification of onset energies j*(J_R) from factorized product-sum structure; explicit coefficient computation for specific quantum numbers (j, J_R).

Result: Exact large-N factorization of the equal-charge index into a product over k and a cube-of-partitions sum; explicit spin-dependent onset energy j*(J_R) below which large-N coefficients vanish; finite-N corrections (e.g., d₃^eqQ(87,27/2)=1) appearing far below j*(27/2), even beyond the classical U(3) black-hole bound; confirmation that the high-spin tail persists after exact equal-charge projection.

Conclusion: The high-spin tail of the BPS spectrum survives the rigorous equal-charge projection, demonstrating robustness of non-perturbative microstate structure; finite-N effects violate naive large-N onset thresholds, signaling important contributions from giant gravitons and subleading sectors not captured by semiclassical black hole geometry.

Abstract: The equal-charge branch of supersymmetric rotating AdS$_5$ black holes has $Q_1=Q_2=Q_3$. The corresponding microcanonical sector of the $\mathcal{N}=4$ superconformal index is obtained by projecting to equal charges, or equivalently by extracting the constant term in the two charge-difference fugacities. We prove that for the large-$N$ multigraviton sector the projected index factorizes exactly as \[
  \mathcal{I}^{\rm eqQ}_\infty(x,p)
  =\prod_{k\ge1}(1-p^kx^{3k})(1-p^{-k}x^{3k})
  \sum_{n\ge0}\mathsf{p}(n)^3x^{6n}, \] where $\mathsf{p}(n)$ is the partition function. This factorization gives, for every spin sector, an explicit onset energy below which the large-$N$ coefficient is zero. Exact $U(3)$ computations show that finite-rank coefficients can nevertheless appear at energies where the large-$N$ coefficient vanishes, including beyond the classical $U(3)$ black-hole bound. We also determine the full line $j=6J_R+6$. In particular, with $j^*(J_R)$ denoting this large-$N$ onset energy, \[
  d_3^{\rm eqQ}(87,\tfrac{27}{2})=1,
  \qquad
  j^*(\tfrac{27}{2})-87=1554, \] and the first giant-graviton sector already contributes one unit at this point. All coefficients are coefficients of the $(-1)^F$-graded index, not positive degeneracies. The main conclusion is that the high-spin tail survives the exact equal-charge projection.

</details>


### [17] [Conformal Renormalisation of 8D Einstein Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03679)
*Giorgos Anastasiou,Ignacio J. Araya,Nicolas Boulanger,Rodrigo Olea,Davide Rovere*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between Holographic Renormalisation (HR) in eight dimensions and a unique conformal gravity theory admitting an Einstein sector with constant negative curvature; it links HR to Topological Regularisation (TR) — which augments the Einstein-Hilbert action with the Euler term at a fixed coefficient — and shows that the 8D conformal gravity action reproduces the TR-polynomial plus boundary terms that cancel asymptotic divergences; the authors conjecture this equivalence extends to all even dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and geometrically interpret Holographic Renormalisation via intrinsic gravitational actions, specifically by connecting it to conformal gravity and Topological Regularisation in even-dimensional anti-de Sitter spacetimes.

Method: Analytic derivation using differential geometry and conformal field theory techniques: explicit construction of the 8D conformal gravity action admitting an Einstein–AdS sector; comparison of its on-shell action with the Topologically Regularised Einstein–Hilbert + Euler action; identification and cancellation of asymptotic boundary divergences via supplementary boundary terms.

Result: The unique 8D conformal gravity action reproduces the Topological Regularisation polynomial in AdS curvature and provides additional boundary terms that exactly cancel the asymptotic divergences of the action; this establishes an exact equivalence between HR and TR in 8D.

Conclusion: Holographic Renormalisation in eight dimensions is fully encoded in a specific conformal gravity theory, and the equivalence with Topological Regularisation — including divergence cancellation via boundary terms — strongly supports a general conjecture that HR in arbitrary even dimensions corresponds to conformal completion of the Einstein–Hilbert action with cosmological constant plus the Euler term at the TR-prescribed coupling.

Abstract: We show that Holographic Renormalisation (HR) in eight dimensions is encoded in the unique conformal gravity theory that admits an Einstein sector with constant negative curvature. We explicitly relate HR to Topological Regularisation (TR), where the latter prescribes to add the Euler term to the Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian density, with a precise coefficient so as to ensure that the resulting density is polynomial in the anti de Sitter (AdS) curvature. The polynomial is still asymptotically divergent. We find that the aforementioned, unique conformal gravity action in 8D, reproduces the polynomial, together with extra boundary terms that cancel the divergent terms in the action. We conjecture that, in arbitrary even dimension, HR is equivalent to conformally completing the Einstein-Hilbert action with negative cosmological constant, plus the Euler term with fixed coupling prescribed by TR.

</details>


### [18] [ERG Kernels on Multiply Connected Configuration Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03750)
*Satoshi Ohya,Taichi Tanaka*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how exact renormalization group (ERG) kernels in Euclidean field theory depend on the global topology—specifically the fundamental group—of multiply connected configuration spaces, showing that ERG kernels decompose into weighted sums over the universal cover with topologically invariant, unrenormalized weights interpretable as Aharonov-Bohm phases; it further links this to flux quantization and WZW level quantization, and proposes a topologically enriched local ERG flow equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how exact renormalization group (ERG) transformations encode global topological features—particularly for multiply connected configuration spaces—beyond standard infinitesimal ERG flow equations, which are blind to topology.

Method: Functional-integral formulation of Euclidean field theory; analysis of ERG kernels via universal covering space decomposition; use of one-dimensional representations of the fundamental group; interpretation via Aharonov-Bohm phase analogy in infinite-dimensional configuration space; derivation of a gauge-equivalent, locally formulated ERG flow equation incorporating topological data.

Result: ERG kernels for multiply connected spaces are expressed as weighted sums over the universal cover, with weights given by unrenormalized one-dimensional characters of π₁; these weights admit an Aharonov-Bohm interpretation tied to background magnetic flux; flux quantization yields Wess-Zumino-Witten level quantization; a topologically augmented local ERG flow equation is constructed.

Conclusion: Global topology—encoded via π₁-representations—fundamentally constrains ERG kernels, rendering certain topological phases invariant under renormalization; this establishes a deep connection between ERG, geometric quantization, and topological field theory, and necessitates topological refinement of standard ERG formalism.

Abstract: In the functional-integral formulation of Euclidean field theory, exact renormalization group (ERG) transformations are realized by functional-integral kernels. Unlike the ERG flow equations that describe infinitesimal ERG transformations, these ERG kernels explicitly depend on the global topology of the configuration space. This paper explores this topology dependence for multiply connected configuration spaces. We show that the ERG kernel is in general given by a weighted sum of kernels on its universal covering space, where the weight factors are determined by a one-dimensional representation of the fundamental group. These weight factors are shown never to be renormalized under the ERG. We also show that these factors can be interpreted as Aharonov-Bohm phases with respect to a background magnetic flux penetrating the infinite-dimensional configuration space. From this viewpoint, a normalization condition for ERG transformations corresponds to a flux-quantization condition, which is equivalent to the level-quantization condition for Wess-Zumino-Witten terms in nonlinear sigma models. Finally, we present an alternative gauge-equivalent form of the ERG flow equation that incorporates this topological information locally.

</details>


### [19] [Symmetries and Conservation Laws in Lie-Poisson Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04522)
*M. A. Kurkov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces Lie-Poisson electrodynamics (LPE) — a non-Abelian, nonlinear deformation of electrodynamics — and shows that, in the source-free case, its dynamics are equivalent to Maxwell theory via a non-trivial field redefinition; using this equivalence, it constructs LPE symmetries (including deformed Poincaré transformations) and conserved currents, and proposes a quantization scheme.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore consistent non-Abelian, nonlinear deformations of electrodynamics beyond standard gauge theories, motivated by geometric formulations and potential implications for quantum gravity or effective field theories with modified spacetime structure.

Method: Geometric formulation of source-free Lie-Poisson electrodynamics; construction of an explicit, invertible field redefinition mapping LPE fields to Maxwell fields under mild regularity assumptions; derivation of symmetry generators and Noether currents via pullback along the map; proposal of canonical quantization by lifting the Maxwell quantization through the field redefinition.

Result: A rigorous equivalence between vacuum LPE and Maxwell theory at the level of equations of motion and symmetries; explicit expressions for deformed Poincaré generators and associated conserved currents; a well-defined quantization prescription preserving the classical equivalence.

Conclusion: Lie-Poisson electrodynamics, despite its non-Abelian and nonlinear structure, is classically equivalent to Maxwell theory in the absence of sources; its symmetries and quantization are systematically inherited from Maxwell theory via the field redefinition, suggesting LPE is a geometrically enriched but physically equivalent reformulation rather than a genuinely new dynamical theory in the vacuum case.

Abstract: Lie-Poisson electrodynamics (LPE) is a non-Abelian and nonlinear deformation of usual electrodynamics, where the gauge algebra is defined through a Lie-algebra-type Poisson bracket on space-time. We focus on the geometric approach to LPE in the absence of charged matter. We establish a non-trivial field redefinition which, under mild technical assumptions, maps the LPE dynamics to that of Maxwell theory. Using this map, for any symmetry of the Maxwell action, we construct generators of LPE symmetries and the corresponding conserved currents. In particular, we obtain deformed Poincaré transformations. We also outline a natural quantization prescription for LPE based on our field redefinition.

</details>


### [20] [Holographic heat engines for Schwarzschild black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03749)
*Ripunjay Dwivedi,Manus R. Visser*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs reversible black hole heat engines in asymptotically flat spacetime using quasi-local gravitational thermodynamics, enclosing a Schwarzschild black hole in a finite spherical cavity and identifying the boundary thermal system as the working substance; it derives exact efficiencies for five classical engine cycles and shows the regenerated Stirling efficiency closely approaches the Carnot bound at high temperature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore black hole thermodynamics beyond global (asymptotic) descriptions by constructing physically realizable, reversible heat engines in asymptotically flat spacetime—leveraging quasi-local thermodynamics where boundary conditions and surface variables define meaningful thermodynamic quantities.

Method: Quasi-local gravitational thermodynamics is applied: a Schwarzschild black hole is enclosed in a finite spherical cavity; the boundary (cavity wall) hosts a dual thermal system whose surface pressure and area constitute a thermodynamic P–V pair; fixed coupling constants ensure no variation of fundamental constants; exact cycle efficiencies (Carnot, Otto, Diesel, Brayton, Stirling) are analytically derived and numerically compared.

Result: Exact expressions for efficiencies of all five engine cycles are obtained; the regenerated Stirling engine achieves efficiency nearly saturating the Carnot bound, converging to it in the high-temperature limit; efficiencies serve as probes of the black hole’s quasi-local equation of state.

Conclusion: Quasi-local thermodynamics enables well-defined, reversible black hole heat engines in asymptotically flat space; the near-Carnot performance of the regenerated Stirling cycle highlights the thermodynamic richness encoded in boundary data and supports the viability of operational black hole thermodynamics without requiring asymptotic structure.

Abstract: We construct reversible black hole heat engines in asymptotically flat spacetime using quasi-local gravitational thermodynamics. We enclose a Schwarzschild black hole in a finite spherical cavity and identify the working substance with the dual thermal system on the cavity boundary. The surface pressure and boundary area define a thermodynamic pressure-volume pair in the dual system, while all coupling constants of the gravitational theory remain fixed. We derive exact efficiencies for the Carnot, Otto, Diesel, Brayton, and Stirling engines and compare them numerically. These efficiencies probe the quasi-local equations of state of the black hole. The regenerated Stirling efficiency remains remarkably close to the Carnot bound and approaches it in the high-temperature limit.

</details>


### [21] [Entropy bounds, Geroch process, and the sign of deformation parameter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04905)
*Bijan Bagchi,Akshat Pandey,Parmest Roy,Sauvik Sen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper examines how Planck-scale modifications—via a generalized uncertainty principle (GUP)—affect the Bekenstein entropy bound near black hole horizons in (3+1) and (2+1) dimensions, finding that negative GUP deformation relaxes the bound universally, while positive deformation tightens it.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum gravity effects encoded in the generalized uncertainty principle modify the Bekenstein entropy bound near black hole horizons, particularly under dimensional reduction and semiclassical approximation.

Method: Uses Geroch's thought experiment of lowering a system into a black hole near the horizon; applies semiclassical gravity with GUP-modified dispersion relations to derive deformed entropy bounds in both (3+1) and (2+1) spacetime dimensions.

Result: Negative GUP parameter leads to universal relaxation of the Bekenstein bound across dimensions; positive GUP parameter tightens the bound; the effect is interpreted as arising from Planck-scale corrections to near-horizon redshift.

Conclusion: The Bekenstein entropy bound is sensitive to quantum gravitational deformations via GUP, and its dimensional universality persists under such deformations—however, its numerical value is systematically shifted depending on the sign of the GUP parameter.

Abstract: Based on Geroch's process of dropping a system into a black hole from the vicinity of the horizon, we investigate in this paper the influence of deformation on the Bekenstein entropy bound both for (3+1) and (2+1) dimensions in the context of a generalized uncertainty principle (GUP). While providing a coherent framework for providing an upper limit on the entropy across dimensions we show, in the framework of a semiclassical treatment, that while a negative GUP deformation yields a universal relaxation of the bound, a positive deformation tightens it. Our results may be interpreted as a response to Planck-scale modifications of the near-horizon redshift.

</details>


### [22] [A Smoothness Principle for Branch Selection Across Black-Hole Horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03800)
*Yoon-Seok Choun,Ki-Seok Kim*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a branch-selection principle—based on horizon-residual smoothness—to unify horizon physics (e.g., smooth horizon crossing), boundary phenomena (e.g., pole-skipping), and group-theoretic structure (SL(2,R) lowest-weight states) in AdS₂/JT gravity; it shows pole-skipping is the holographic signature of residual smoothness and applies the criterion to diagnose genuine pole-skipping in holographic superconductors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the apparent disconnect between horizon-local physics (e.g., regular singular behavior), boundary observables (e.g., pole-skipping in retarded Green functions), and symmetry-based structures (e.g., SL(2,R) representation theory) in near-extremal black holes and AdS₂ holography.

Method: Proposes a physical branch-selection rule: remove leading horizon divergence and impose C^∞ smoothness on the residual field; validates it using exact solutions in JT gravity/AdS₂; interprets results via SL(2,R) representation theory; extends analysis to a static holographic superconductor model to test robustness of pole-skipping identification.

Result: Demonstrates that smooth horizon continuation, pole-skipping ambiguity, and SL(2,R) lowest-weight structure all arise from the same residual smoothness condition; establishes pole-skipping as the holographic manifestation of horizon-residual smoothness; identifies necessary and sufficient conditions (simultaneous residual smoothness + linear independence of boundary branches) for genuine pole-skipping in non-exactly solvable models.

Conclusion: Horizon-residual smoothness is a fundamental physical principle—not an artifact—that unifies bulk horizon physics, boundary correlators, and spacetime symmetry representations; it provides a rigorous, symmetry-grounded diagnostic for distinguishing true pole-skipping from spurious branch-collapse effects in generic holographic systems.

Abstract: Many black-hole wave equations reduce to radial ordinary differential equations with regular singular points at horizons, where local solutions split into distinct branches. We propose a simple branch-selection principle to isolate the physically distinguished branch: removing the leading horizon behavior and imposing \(C^{\infty }\) smoothness on the residual field. Using an exactly solvable scalar field in \(JT/AdS_{2}\), we demonstrate that this single principle successfully organizes three seemingly distinct structures: smooth continuation across the black-hole horizon, the ambiguity of the boundary retarded Green function (pole-skipping), and the lowest-weight structure of an \(SL(2,\mathbb{R})\) representation. Rather than treating these phenomena as isolated boundary or horizon properties, our results unify them into a cohesive bulk-to-boundary dictionary, proving that the boundary pole-skipping ambiguity is the precise holographic manifestation of horizon-residual smoothness. Furthermore, the group-theoretic identification reveals that this physical branch selection is not a mere mathematical artifact, but is fundamentally governed by the underlying spacetime symmetries. Finally, we apply this framework as a diagnostic tool to a static holographic superconductor model, revealing that a genuine pole-skipping point requires both simultaneous residual smoothness and strict linear independence of the boundary branches to prevent deceptive branch-collapse artifacts.

</details>


### [23] [A Century of Group Theory in Particle Physics and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05225)
*Paul Sorba*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper surveys the development and interdisciplinary impact of Group Theory in mathematics and physics over the 20th century, emphasizing its role in particle physics, statistical physics, and potential applications in theoretical biology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To highlight the broad and growing significance of Group Theory beyond pure mathematics—particularly its foundational and unifying role across physics disciplines and emerging relevance in theoretical biology.

Method: Survey and conceptual analysis of historical developments and cross-disciplinary applications of Group Theory.

Result: Demonstrates that Group Theory is not only central to particle physics but also valuable in statistical physics and potentially applicable in theoretical biology.

Conclusion: Group Theory serves as a powerful unifying framework across multiple scientific domains, with expanding relevance beyond traditional applications.

Abstract: The development of Group Theory in Mathematics as well as its impact in Physics has been spectacular during the twentieth century, and more particularly these last fifty years. If its contribution to Particle Physics deserves a special consideration, its usefulness cannot be neglected in other domains like Statistical Physics for example, and might also be of interest in Theoretical Biology. Some of these aspects will be examined.

</details>


### [24] [From OPE Associativity to Gravitational Wave Signatures: Critical Dimensions and AdS/dS Transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03969)
*Tetiana Obikhod*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper links OPE algebraic consistency in CFTs to observable log-periodic features in the stochastic gravitational wave background via AdS/CFT and analytic continuation to de Sitter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge abstract conformal bootstrap constraints (OPE associativity) with concrete cosmological observables—specifically, signatures in the stochastic gravitational wave background—by leveraging holography and analytic continuation.

Method: Interprets conformal 6j symbols as crossing kernels to extract anomalous dimensions γ_{n,J} from OPE crossing singularities; uses AdS/CFT to map these to bulk mass shifts; applies analytic continuation AdS → dS to identify critical γ_c violating the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound; derives complex scaling dimensions Δ_dS = d/2 ± iμ and associated log-oscillatory four-point functions; computes resulting log-periodic modulation in SGWB power spectrum.

Result: Derives a log-periodic structure Δln f = 2π/μ in the SGWB spectrum, directly tied to the imaginary part μ of complex conformal dimensions arising when γ < γ_c; establishes a measurable observational signature of OPE consistency.

Conclusion: Algebraic consistency of the CFT OPE—encoded in crossing symmetry—has a direct, falsifiable imprint on the SGWB through log-periodic oscillations, enabling next-generation GW detectors like LISA to probe fundamental CFT data (e.g., μ) and thus test quantum gravity constraints via cosmology.

Abstract: We present a framework connecting the algebraic consistency of the Operator Product Expansion (OPE) in Conformal Field Theories to observable signatures in the stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB). By interpreting the conformal 6j symbols as crossing kernels, we extract the anomalous dimensions $γ_{n,J}$ of double-trace operators from the singularities of the crossing equation. Through the AdS/CFT correspondence, these algebraic shifts are holographically reinterpreted as effective mass shifts in the Anti-de Sitter bulk. We identify a critical anomalous dimension, $γ_c$, which marks the threshold where the effective mass violates the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound. Upon analytic continuation to de Sitter space ($L_{\text{AdS}} \to iL_{\text{dS}}$), we demonstrate that crossing this threshold ($γ< γ_c$) forces the dual scaling dimensions into the complex principal series, $Δ_{\text{dS}} = d/2 \pm iμ$. This complex dimensionality induces characteristic logarithmic oscillations in the four-point correlators. In the cosmological context, these oscillations manifest as a log-periodic modulation in the SGWB, characterized by a specific spacing $Δ\ln f = 2π/μ$. Detecting this log-periodic structure with next-generation interferometers such as LISA would directly measure the parameter $μ$, closing the loop between abstract OPE associativity and observational cosmology.

</details>


### [25] [The Normalised Wigner Negativity Rate as a Second-Moment Probe of Infall in AdS$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04065)
*Ritam Basu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces the rate of normalized Krylov-Wigner negativity as a diagnostic for the second moment (spreading) of boundary quantum states in AdS/CFT, linking it to radial momentum and tidal stretching of infalling geodesics; it finds an exact sinh⁴Δ(πt/β) scaling, with Δ = 1 (BF-bound saturating dimension in AdS₃) being special—yielding negativity rate ∝ ℛ ∝ 𝒞 P_ρ—and connects Krylov variance growth to negativity rate only at this critical dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the first-moment spread complexity (which captures average operator position but not wavepacket spreading) and diagnose the second moment—i.e., quantum delocalization away from classical trajectories—of boundary states dual to infalling particles in AdS.

Method: Constructs the seed-normalized Krylov-Wigner distribution (Wigner transform of descendant cloud with return amplitude removed), derives its analytic Bessel form in the macroscopic limit, computes total negativity exactly, and leverages exact negative binomial statistics of the Krylov chain plus Caputa et al.'s momentum dictionary to relate negativity rate to Krylov variance growth and radial dynamics.

Result: Negativity scales as sinh⁴Δ(πt/β); seed-normalized negativity saturates per O(√D) bound; negativity rate equals Krylov variance growth rate asymptotically iff Δ = 1; at Δ = 1, rate equals tidal stretch ℛ ∝ 𝒞 P_ρ; suggests SU(1,1) discrete series unifies Krylov number and transverse string size operators.

Conclusion: Krylov-Wigner negativity rate provides a refined, dimension-sensitive probe of quantum spreading in holography, with Δ = 1 in AdS₃ singled out as physically distinguished—where negativity dynamics directly encode horizon-approaching tidal physics—pointing to deeper algebraic (SU(1,1)) structure underlying operator growth and string geometry.

Abstract: In spread complexity, the average position of an operator along its Krylov chain, recovers the right radial momentum of an infalling particle in AdS, yet it is a measure of the first moment, irrespective of the spread of the wavepacket away from its classical trajectory. The rate of a normalized Krylov-Wigner negativity can be proposed as a diagnostic of the second moment of the boundary state that captures this spreading. Starting with the \emph{seed-normalized} Krylov-Wigner distribution -- that is, the Wigner transform of the descendant cloud, with the decaying return amplitude divided out -- we obtain an analytic Bessel form in the macroscopic limit and compute its total negativity explicitly. Retaining the Bessel variable all the way through, we find that the negativity goes as $\sinh^{4Δ}(πt/β)$, while the raw, seed-normalized state negativity saturates, as dictated by the $O(\sqrt{D})$ bound. Using the exact negative binomial statistics of the Krylov chain and the momentum dictionary of Caputa et al.~\cite{Caputa:2024}, the rate of the negativity scales as the growth rate of the Krylov variance at late time asymptotics \emph{if and only if} $Δ=1$, the Breitenlohner-Freedman saturating dimension in $AdS_3$. This dimensionality is special in that the negativity rate is the product of the proper radial position and momentum, $\mathcal{R} \propto \mathcal{C} P_ρ$, i.e., the rate of the tidal stretch of nearby geodesic falling into the horizon. We comment on the direction for future research, in particular the interpretation of the transverse string size operator in terms of the Krylov number operator through the common $SU(1,1)$ discrete series.

</details>


### [26] [Infrared Divergences as Itinerant Vacua](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04115)
*Masahiro Morikawa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets infrared divergences (IRDs) not as pathologies but as signatures of an 'itinerant vacuum'—a quantum vacuum continuously transitioning across inequivalent states as a classical order parameter evolves; using an exact CTP effective action decomposition and Hubbard–Stratonovich transformation, it converts IRD-induced imaginary terms into classical stochastic noise and memory kernels, unifying phenomena across soft QED, de Sitter scalars, soft gravitons, and non-equilibrium phase transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reinterpret infrared divergences constructively—as physical manifestations of vacuum dynamics—rather than merely as technical nuisances requiring cancellation or regularization.

Method: Exact real-time closed-time-path (CTP) effective action decomposition; Hubbard–Stratonovich transformation of the infrared-divergent imaginary part of the influence functional into a classical stochastic source; geometric formulation via Hilbert-space bundle over the vacuum manifold.

Result: IRDs generate observable classical effects—including 1/f noise, gravitational memory, non-Gaussian fluctuations, and primordial power spectra—via stochastic dynamics arising from vacuum transitions; unified description across four physical domains.

Conclusion: Infrared divergences signal an 'itinerant vacuum' undergoing continuous transitions between orthogonal vacua; their divergent imaginary part encodes both deterministic memory and quantum uncertainty, yielding emergent classical stochasticity without invoking decoherence or coarse-graining.

Abstract: Infrared divergences (IRDs) are usually treated as pathologies to be cancelled, regularized, or hidden in dressed asymptotic states. This paper develops a complementary and constructive viewpoint: an IRD is the signature of an \emph{itinerant vacuum} -- a quantum vacuum that wanders continuously through a family of inequivalent states as a classical order parameter evolves. Each value of the order parameter carries its own coherent vacuum, so moving the order parameter means traversing a succession of orthogonal vacua. The IRD is the field-theoretic cost of this wandering, and the $1/f$ noise, gravitational memory, and non-Gaussian fluctuations that emerge from it are its observable classical remnants.
  The technical core is an exact separation in the real-time closed-time-path (CTP) effective action. The infrared-divergent imaginary part of the influence functional must not be left as a divergent coefficient in a deterministic equation of motion; it is instead converted, by a Hubbard--Stratonovich identity, into a classical stochastic source. This step is an algebraic identity of the generating functional and requires no prior coarse graining or decoherence assumption: the retarded kernel encodes the memory of past vacuum transitions, while the noise kernel encodes the quantum uncertainty of the next one. We apply this construction to four parallel arenas -- soft QED, scalar fields in de Sitter space, soft gravitons, and non-equilibrium phase transitions -- and show that the same itinerant-vacuum mechanism underlies $1/f$ current noise, the primordial power spectrum, gravitational memory, and order-parameter dynamics. A geometric formulation in terms of a Hilbert-space bundle over the vacuum manifold is outlined as an outlook.

</details>


### [27] [Finite-$N$ Operator Algebras and the Hilbert Space of Bilocal Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04143)
*Byoungjoon Ahn,Robert de Mello Koch,Vatsal Garg,Antal Jevicki,Junggi Yoon*

Main category: hep-th
