TL;DR: This paper provides an operator-algebraic and representation-theoretic framework for finite-N bilocal holography, identifying an invariant dual-pair algebra structure on the Fock space and showing that the singlet sector corresponds to a unique irreducible representation of a 'master algebra'; trace identities become representation-theoretic identities, and characters yield finite-N traces and partition functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a rigorous finite-N Hilbert space formulation of bilocal holography—extending prior work—and to uncover the underlying algebraic and representation-theoretic structure governing the singlet sector beyond large-N approximations.

Method: Operator-algebraic analysis of commuting color and bilocal Lie algebra actions on Fock space; projection onto the singlet sector; classification of irreducible representations of the resulting invariant 'master algebra'; computation of Casimirs and characters for orthogonal, symplectic, and unitary cases.

Result: Identification of an invariant dual-pair operator algebra; demonstration that the singlet sector hosts a single irreducible representation of the master algebra; derivation of finite-N trace relations as representation-theoretic identities; explicit Casimir values and character-based formulas for traces and partition functions across O(N), Sp(N), and U(N) cases.

Conclusion: Finite-N bilocal holography admits a precise mathematical description via invariant Lie algebra representations, offering a novel, exact characterization of the singlet Hilbert space—free from large-N approximations—and unifying trace constraints within representation theory.

Abstract: We give an operator-algebraic and representation-theoretic description of the Hilbert spaces finite-$N$ bilocal holography. This work is a sequel to the finite-$N$ Hilbert space construction of arXiv:2602.20788 [hep-th]. The central result is the establishment of an invariant dual-pair operator algebra: before imposing the singlet constraint the Fock space carries commuting actions of the color group and of a bilocal Lie algebra, while the projection to the singlet sector selects a single irreducible representation of the invariant Lie algebra, which we call a master algebra. The finite-$N$ trace relations, beginning with the quadratic identities studied here, are shown to become representation-theoretic identities of the selected irreducible representation. We summarize the orthogonal, symplectic and unitary cases, identify the corresponding finite-$N$ constraints, compute the singlet Casimirs, and explain how finite traces and partition functions are obtained through characters of the resulting irreducible representations. This provides a novel, previously unknown mathematical description of the singlet space.

</details>


### [28] [Solving for the integrable boundary states of the ABJM spin chain from $KT$-relations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04203)
*Nan Bai,Hui Yang,Mao-Zhong Shao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper solves integrability constraints for boundary states in the alternating SU(4) spin chain from ABJM theory, deriving state/operator equations for n-site translational invariant states and analyzing chiral/achiral cases up to n=4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and classify integrable boundary states in the alternating SU(4) spin chain motivated by their role in ABJM theory, particularly those respecting n-site translational invariance.

Method: Uses the KT-relation to derive and solve integrability constraints; reduces them to state equations (for odd n) or operator equations (for even n) involving the elementary n-site block and the K-matrix K(u); treats 1-site case with general operator-valued pairs (including internal space), and n=2,3,4 cases with c-number solutions.

Result: Explicit integrability conditions for n-site translationally invariant boundary states; classification of chiral and achiral solutions for n ≤ 4; distinction between operator-valued (n = 1) and c-number (n ≥ 2) solution spaces.

Conclusion: The KT-relation provides an effective framework to systematically construct integrable boundary states in the alternating SU(4) spin chain; structural differences between odd/even n and between chiral/achiral cases are clarified, with full solutions obtained up to n = 4.

Abstract: We study integrable boundary states of the alternating SU(4) spin chain arising in ABJM theory. Starting from the $KT$-relation, we directly solve the integrability constraints for states with $n$-site translational invariance. For odd and even $n$, these constraints are reduced respectively to state equations and operator equations for the elementary $n$-site block and the matrix $K(u)$. We analyze chiral and achiral cases for $n\leq4$. In the 1-site case we allow general operator-valued integrable pairs with an internal space, while in the remaining cases we focus on $c$-number solutions.

</details>


### [29] [Electromagnetic Scoot for Dyons Revisited](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04246)
*Rasim Yılmaz,Onur Ayberk Çakmak*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the analysis of the electromagnetic 'scoot' effect—where scattering charges acquire a boost-like angular momentum shift—to systems with both electric and magnetic charges, contrasting it with the purely electric case within multiparticle state representations; the effect appears at 1PM order on constant-time slices but vanishes there on hyperboloidal slices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how electromagnetic angular momentum shifts (the 'scoot' effect) generalize when magnetic charges are included, and to assess frame- and slice-dependence of conservation law evaluations in scattering processes.

Method: Post-Minkowskian (1PM) expansion applied to scattering of dyonic (electric + magnetic) charges; comparison of angular momentum conservation on constant-time versus hyperboloidal Cauchy slices; analysis within multiparticle quantum state representations.

Result: The scoot effect persists at 1PM for dyonic scattering on constant-time slices, with modifications due to magnetic charge contributions; it similarly vanishes at 1PM on hyperboloidal slices, confirming slice dependence; representation-theoretic analysis shows consistency with Poincaré covariance for multiparticle states.

Conclusion: The electromagnetic scoot is not intrinsic to the interaction alone but depends critically on the choice of spacelike hypersurface; inclusion of magnetic charges preserves the qualitative slice dependence while enriching the angular momentum structure, reinforcing the need for careful treatment of asymptotic symmetries and boundary conditions in scattering amplitudes.

Abstract: In the scattering of two electric charges, the particles acquire a shift in their net boost-like angular momentum, balanced by an opposite field contribution. This electromagnetic scoot effect appears at first order in post-Minkowskian expansion (1PM) order when conservation laws are evaluated on constant-time slices, but disappears at this order on hyperboloidal slices. Here, we extend this analysis to scattering involving both electric and magnetic charges and compare the results with the purely electric case in the context of multiparticle state representations.

</details>


### [30] [Entanglement of excited states after measurements in conformal field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04268)
*Hui-Huang Chen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a CFT framework to compute post-measurement Rényi entropies for low-energy excited states after projective measurement on a spatial interval, mapping the geometry to a disk and expressing results as boundary-CFT correlation functions; it applies the formalism to the compact free boson, deriving hafnian expressions for chiral current excitations and revealing phase-sensitive interference in conjugate vertex operator superpositions, with numerical validation proposed via free-fermion and multi-Slater determinant methods in the critical XX chain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum entanglement of low-energy excited states is modified by projective measurements in (1+1)D CFTs, particularly how post-selection on a slit with conformal boundary conditions affects Rényi entropy.

Method: Conformal mapping of the replicated post-measurement geometry (slit + operator insertions) to a disk; computation of Rényi entropy via normalized boundary CFT correlation functions; explicit evaluation for compact free boson CFT using current algebra and vertex operator techniques; proposal of free-fermion and multi-Slater determinant numerical methods for the critical XX chain.

Result: Post-measurement Rényi entropy expressed as boundary-CFT correlators; chiral current excitations yield current hafnians; coherent superpositions of J/\bar J and conjugate vertex operators show distinct phase dependence—phase-insensitive on infinite cylinder but phase-sensitive for finite slit; predictions amenable to exact numerical tests in XX chain.

Conclusion: Projectively measuring a spatial interval in 1+1D CFT induces nontrivial entanglement structure in excited states, governed by boundary CFT data; phase coherence manifests only in finite geometries, highlighting the interplay between topology, boundary conditions, and quantum superposition in post-selected entanglement.

Abstract: We study the entanglement of low-energy excited states after a fixed-outcome projective measurement on a spatial interval in a (1+1)-dimensional conformal field theory (CFT). The post-selected measurement outcome is represented by a slit carrying a conformal boundary condition, while excited states are introduced by operator insertions in the Euclidean path integral. After mapping the replicated slit geometry to a disk, the excited-state contribution to the post-measurement Rényi entropy is expressed as a normalized boundary-CFT correlation function.
  We apply this framework to the compact free boson CFT. For the chiral current excitation, the relevant ratios are given by current hafnians. We also study coherent superpositions of $J$ and $\bar J$, and of conjugate compact vertex operators. In the conjugate-vertex case, the ordinary-cylinder second Rényi ratio is independent of the relative phase, whereas the finite-slit post-measurement ratio contains phase-sensitive interference terms. Finally, we describe free-fermion and multi-Slater determinant methods for testing these predictions in the critical XX chain.

</details>


### [31] [Stress stability criterion of the isospinning $\mathbb{C}P^2$ solitons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04413)
*Sergei Antsipovich,Yakov Shnir*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the energy-momentum tensor, shear forces, and pressure distributions of stationary rotating topological solitons in a (2+1)-dimensional CP² sigma model with stabilizing potential, finding parameter-dependent classical instability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand mechanical properties (shear forces, pressure) and stability of rotating topological solitons in a CP² sigma model, which serve as toy models for exotic quantum Hall states or skyrmionic textures.

Method: Analytical and numerical evaluation of the energy-momentum tensor components; derivation and analysis of mechanical stress distributions (pressure and shear forces); application of virial-type and local stability criteria.

Result: Nontrivial spatial distributions of pressure and shear forces are obtained; stability analysis reveals parameter regimes where solitons violate mechanical equilibrium or energy bounds, indicating classical instability.

Conclusion: Rotating CP² solitons exhibit mechanical structure sensitive to model parameters, and classical stability is not guaranteed—instability emerges beyond critical values of coupling or angular momentum.

Abstract: We study the energy-momentum tensor of stationary rotating topological solitons in a (2 + 1)-dimensional $\mathbb{C}P^2$ nonlinear sigma model with a stabilizing potential term. We evaluate the distributions of the corresponding shear forces and pressure and study the stability criteria for these solutions. Our results suggest that these solitons become classically unstable for some range of values of the parameters of the system.

</details>


### [32] [Neumann scalars in AdS: partition functions and phases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04417)
*Biki Bishwakarma,Astha Kakkar,Swarnendu Sarkar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes one-loop partition functions for scalars in AdS$_{d+1}$ with Neumann boundary conditions, using analytic continuation from Dirichlet to Neumann conformal dimensions and contour deformation in the eigenvalue integral; it identifies and contrasts thermodynamic phases with Dirichlet case, validating them via long-range correlator behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum phase structure of scalar field theories in AdS with Neumann boundary conditions—complementing prior Dirichlet studies—and to clarify how boundary conditions affect thermodynamics and holographic duals.

Method: Analytic continuation of the conformal dimension from Δ₊ (Dirichlet) to Δ₋ (Neumann); contour deformation in the λ-integral over Laplacian eigenvalues; comparison of thermal partition functions and long-distance two-point correlators.

Result: Neumann-boundary scalar theories exhibit distinct thermodynamic phases compared to Dirichlet ones; phase structure is confirmed through both partition function analysis and asymptotic behavior of correlators; contour deformation enables consistent analytic continuation beyond the standard real-line integral.

Conclusion: Boundary condition choice (Neumann vs. Dirichlet) fundamentally alters the phase diagram and spectral structure of scalar QFTs in AdS; the proposed contour-deformation method provides a robust framework for computing partition functions for non-Dirichlet boundary conditions.

Abstract: We present an analysis of one-loop partition functions for scalars in AdS$_{d+1}$ obeying the Neumann boundary condition and explore phases of scalar field theories in several dimensions at zero and finite temperature. The partition function computation involves an analytic continuation from $Δ_+$ corresponding to the Dirichlet boundary condition to $Δ_-$ corresponding to Neumann boundary condition. We show that this can be implemented by deformations of the contour for integral over eigenvalue ($λ$) of the Laplace operator as compared to the integral over ${\mathbb R}$ in [arXiv:2201.09043] for the Dirichlet boundary condition. We further contrast these phases with those appearing for the case of scalars obeying the Dirichlet boundary condition and corroborate the occurrence of these phases by studying the long range behaviour of correlators.

</details>


### [33] [Topological effects in the polarization of the Fulling-Rindler vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04483)
*A. A. Saharian,G. V. Mirzoyan,V. S. Torosyan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how compactifying spatial dimensions in Rindler spacetime modifies vacuum expectation values (VEVs) of a charged scalar field in the Fulling-Rindler vacuum, revealing off-diagonal energy-momentum components induced by magnetic fluxes and analyzing asymptotics near the horizon and low-acceleration limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how topological features—specifically toroidal compactification with quasi-periodic boundary conditions (and associated magnetic fluxes)—affect quantum vacuum structure in accelerated frames (Rindler spacetime), extending prior work on trivial topology.

Method: Analytical computation of VEVs of field squared and energy-momentum tensor for a charged scalar field in Fulling-Rindler vacuum; employs mode-sum regularization, zeta-function techniques, and asymptotic analysis near the Rindler horizon and in low-acceleration limit; distinguishes contributions from Minkowski vacuum and topological corrections.

Result: Compactification induces off-diagonal components in the vacuum energy-momentum tensor when magnetic flux (non-zero phase) is present; near the Rindler horizon, diagonal VEVs match those in trivial-topology Rindler spacetime, while off-diagonal components vanish there; at low acceleration, VEV differences decay exponentially except for massless fields with zero flux, where power-law decay occurs.

Conclusion: Topological compactification enriches Rindler vacuum structure via flux-dependent off-diagonal stress-energy, with universal near-horizon behavior but distinctive low-acceleration scaling; results apply to cylindrical and toroidal black hole horizons.

Abstract: We investigate how the compactification of some spatial dimensions in Rindler spacetime affects the vacuum expectation values (VEVs) of the field squared and the energy-momentum tensor for a charged scalar field prepared in the Fulling-Rindler vacuum state. A toral compactification is considered with quasi-periodicity conditions on the field operator along the compact dimensions. The phases of these conditions are interpreted in terms of the magnetic flux enclosed by the compact dimensions. For a general number of spatial dimensions, the components of the VEVs are explicitly separated, corresponding to the expectation values in the Minkowski vacuum. As a limiting case, the VEVs are retrieved in Rindler spacetime with trivial topology. We demonstrate that, for non-zero phases in the periodicity conditions, the vacuum energy-momentum tensor exhibits off-diagonal components with indices in the compact subspace. Near the Rindler horizon, the leading terms in the asymptotic expansions of the field squared and the diagonal components of the energy-momentum tensor coincide with the corresponding VEVs in Rindler spacetime with trivial topology. The off-diagonal components of the energy-momentum tensor vanish on the Rindler horizon. For small accelerations, the difference between the VEVs in the Fulling-Rindler and Minkowski vacua is exponentially small. The exception to this is a massless field with zero phases in the periodicity conditions. In this special case, the difference decays according to a power law as a function of acceleration. As an application, we consider the VEVs near the horizon of cylindrical black holes and topological black holes with toroidal horizons.

</details>


### [34] [Kaluza-Klein tower thresholds and scheme dependence of the species scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04602)
*Yuri Michinobu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper clarifies the relation between two common definitions of the species scale in quantum gravity—perturbative breakdown energy and higher-derivative operator suppression scale—by analyzing cutoff-scheme dependence of one-loop graviton corrections from particle towers; it distinguishes universal logarithmic contributions from regulator-dependent local terms, using Kaluza-Klein towers as a concrete example.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in the definition of the species scale in quantum gravity, specifically the apparent discrepancy between its characterization via perturbative breakdown (one-loop graviton correction) and via suppression scale of higher-derivative operators in the effective action.

Method: Systematic analysis of cutoff-scheme dependence of one-loop contributions from particle towers (especially Kaluza-Klein) to the graviton two-point function; classification of regulator-dependent local vs. universal logarithmic terms within proper-time regulator class; EFT matching interpretation of threshold effects.

Result: The leading tower-enhanced local correction is regulator dependent and encodes EFT matching data, whereas the subleading logarithmic correction is universal; full four-derivative one-loop corrections from KK towers are computed, cleanly separating these two types of contributions.

Conclusion: The species scale cannot be unambiguously identified at the coefficient level between the perturbative-breakdown and higher-derivative definitions due to inherent regulator dependence of local terms; only the logarithmic part provides scheme-independent physical information, supporting a refined EFT understanding of species scaling.

Abstract: We revisit the species scale in quantum gravity from the viewpoint of effective field theory (EFT). Two characterizations are commonly used: one defines it as the energy at which the perturbative description of quantum gravity breaks down, as inferred from the one-loop correction to the graviton propagator; the other identifies it with the suppression scale of higher-derivative operators in the gravitational effective action. We clarify the relation between these characterizations by analyzing the cutoff-scheme dependence of the one-loop tower contribution. For a large number of species, the leading tower-enhanced local correction is regulator dependent and should be interpreted as EFT matching data, while the subleading logarithmic contribution is universal within the class of proper-time cutoff profiles considered here. As a concrete application, we compute the full four-derivative one-loop corrections from Kaluza-Klein towers. Our results separate regulator-stable logarithmic data from scheme-dependent local threshold contributions, providing a controlled EFT interpretation of the relation between perturbative-breakdown and higher-derivative definitions of the species scale and explaining why their coefficient-level identification is not regulator independent.

</details>


### [35] [Multi-Particle Contributions to the Celestial Algebra in the ${\cal N}=8$ Supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04611)
*Changhyun Ahn*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends Calkins-Pate's multi-particle OPE framework to N=8 supergravity, deriving 95 (anti)commutators of celestial soft currents and establishing nontrivial amplitude relations via contour integrals; it also verifies results using triple-collinear limits and proposes generalized N-particle OPEs with linear-mode (anti)commutators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the recently developed multi-particle operator product expansion formalism for celestial holography to full N=8 supergravity, thereby uncovering new algebraic structures and consistency checks in the celestial CFT dual.

Method: Applies Calkins-Pate's construction of multi-particle OPEs to all N=8 supergravity fields (graviton, gravitinos, graviphotons, graviphotinos, scalars); computes (anti)commutators via contour integration of OPEs; cross-checks via triple-collinear limits in 4D bulk; proposes general N-particle OPE ansatz and associated mode (anti)commutators.

Result: Derives 95 explicit (anti)commutators for single-particle contributions to the celestial soft current algebra on the 2D boundary; establishes nontrivial relations among celestial amplitudes from the 95 multi-particle OPEs; confirms consistency via triple-collinear limits; proposes a general N-particle OPE structure with linear-mode (anti)commutators.

Conclusion: The multi-particle OPE framework successfully extends to maximal supergravity, revealing rich algebraic structure in the celestial soft sector and providing concrete constraints and relations among celestial amplitudes—supporting the conjectured holographic duality between 4D asymptotically flat quantum gravity and a 2D celestial CFT.

Abstract: Recently, Calkins and Pate calculated the multi-particle operator product expansions (OPEs) of single-particle celestial operators with two-particle celestial operators in pure Einstein gravity. We apply their construction to the graviton, gravitinos, graviphotons, graviphotinos and scalars in ${\cal N}=8$ supergravity. By performing various contour integrals on these multi-particle OPEs, we obtain ninety-five (anti)commutators for the single-particle contributions from the celestial soft current algebra (derived from the single-particle OPEs) in a two-dimensional boundary. Moreover, these ninety-five multi-particle OPEs provide nontrivial relations between the corresponding celestial amplitudes. The triple-collinear limits between these bosonic and fermionic particles from the four-dimensional bulk are also described as a double check. Finally, we implicitly propose both the multi-particle OPEs of single-particle celestial operators with $(N-1)$-particle celestial operators and the corresponding (anti)commutators for the modes of celestial operators that have linear terms (or single-particle exchange terms due to the sum of $N$-particle factorization channels from amplitudes) on the right-hand sides.

</details>


### [36] [New insights on mutual information in the island approach to the Page curve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04706)
*Anirban Roy Chowdhury,Souvik Paul,Sunandan Gangopadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes mutual information dynamics in eternal black hole systems post-Page time, revealing a conservation law of geometric entanglement: vanishing $I(B_+:B_-)$ at scrambling time coincides with divergent $I(\mathcal{I}:R)$, indicating maximal entanglement; it further derives the previously unknown tripartite mutual information $I(\mathcal{I}:R_+:R_-)$ on the Cauchy slice.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover fundamental principles governing entanglement structure in black hole evaporation, particularly how geometric entanglement is conserved across subsystems after Page time, and to fill the gap in calculating tripartite mutual information in holographic settings.

Method: Analytical derivation using quantum information theory applied to holographic duals of eternal black holes; builds upon prior results for bipartite mutual information and extends them to tripartite configurations on a Cauchy slice.

Result: Demonstrates anticorrelated behavior between $I(B_+:B_-)$ (vanishing) and $I(\mathcal{I}:R)$ (diverging) at scrambling time—evidence of geometric entanglement conservation—and provides the first explicit calculation of $I(\mathcal{I}:R_+:R_-)$ using bipartite data.

Conclusion: Geometric entanglement is conserved across complementary subsystems in the after-Page-time regime, manifested as trade-offs in mutual information; the derived tripartite mutual information establishes a new quantitative tool for probing multipartite entanglement structure in AdS/CFT and black hole information puzzles.

Abstract: In this article, we have presented one of the very important observations, regarding the behavior of mutual information of two different sets of subsystems in the after Page time scenario. This provides us with some deep insights about the conservation of geometrical entanglement. In our earlier works, we have shown how the saturation of mutual information between two specific subsystems plays a vital role in obtaining the correct Page curve for the eternal black hole. In those works, we have shown that the mutual information between $B_+$ and $B_-$, that is, $I(B_{+}: B_{-})$, vanishes at scrambling time, which leads to the correct Page curve. That means that at scrambling time, there is no correlation between $B_+$ and $B_-$. Remarkably, it is observed that at this particular value of observer's time, the mutual information between $\mathcal{I}$ and $R$, that is, $I(\mathcal{I}:R)$, becomes infinity. This indicates that the regions $\mathcal{I}$ and $R$ become maximally entangled. This provides us with a notion of conservation of geometric correlation between different regions on the Cauchy slice. In this work, we have also provided a way to calculate the tripartite mutual information of regions $\mathcal{I}$,$R_+$ and $R_-$, that is, $I(\mathcal{I}:R_+:R_-)$ on the Cauchy slice using the earlier results involving the bipartite regions. This is a new result which was missing in the earlier literature.

</details>


### [37] [Revisiting the Wess-Zumino-Witten Term in Nuclear and Quark Matter under Magnetic Fields and Rotation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04929)
*Markus A. G. Amano,Minoru Eto,Muneto Nitta,Shin Sasaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives anomalous WZW terms for neutral mesons in dense QCD under magnetic fields and rotation, revealing novel topological couplings proportional to B·∇φ and Ω·∇φ for π⁰, η, and η′.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand chiral anomaly effects in dense QCD matter—particularly in astrophysical environments like rotating neutron stars—under external electromagnetic fields and vorticity.

Method: Systematic construction of anomalous Wess-Zumino-Witten terms using background gauge fields for electromagnetism, baryon number, and isospin; explicit derivation for N_f = 2 and N_f = 3 chiral effective theories.

Result: New topological couplings emerge: B·∇φ and Ω·∇φ (φ = π⁰, η, η′), encoding magnetic and rotational responses of neutral pseudoscalar mesons; these support chiral soliton lattice formation in rotating, magnetized dense matter.

Conclusion: The derived anomalous terms provide a field-theoretic basis for chiral imbalances and topological structures in extreme QCD media, with implications for neutron star phenomenology and heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: We study anomalous Wess-Zumino-Witten terms associated with the chiral anomaly in dense QCD matter under magnetic fields and rotation. By introducing electromagnetic, baryon number, and isospin background gauge fields, we write down the topological couplings of neutral mesons for the $N_f=2$ and $N_f=3$ cases. The resulting terms contain characteristic contributions proportional to $\vec{B} \cdot \vec{\nabla} φ$ and $\vecΩ \cdot \vec{\nabla} φ$, where $φ$ denotes $π^0$, $η$, or $η'$. These results are relevant to chiral soliton lattices in dense rotating matter.

</details>


### [38] [The causal set reduction formula](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04980)
*Stav Zalel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives a reduction formula for matrix elements on causal sets, establishes Schwinger-Dyson–like correlator relations, and expresses matrix elements equivalently as a path integral or two distinct correlator sums—bypassing differential equations of motion; extends to flat spacetime yielding an alternative LSZ formulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a background-independent, discrete (causal set) framework for quantum field theory observables without relying on continuum differential equations of motion.

Conclusion: Provides a novel, equation-of-motion–free formalism for scattering amplitudes in causal set QFT, with consistent continuum limit, suggesting broader applicability to background-independent quantum gravity models.

Abstract: We derive a reduction formula for matrix elements on a causal set background. We derive an infinite tower of relations between correlators, akin to the Schwinger-Dyson equations of the continuum. Combining these two results we are able to express matrix elements in three different forms: as a path integral and as two distinct sums of correlators. We sketch the form that our method - which circumvents explicit use of differential equation of motion operators - takes in flat continuum spacetime where it provides an alternative expression for the standard LSZ result.

</details>


### [39] [From Galilei to Euclidean Carroll and the Alice Particle: The Times They Are a-Changin'](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05115)
*Eric A. Bergshoeff,Luca Romano,Jan Rosseel,Elena Simón Félix,Sara Zeko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper unifies Galilean and Carrollian limits via 'Euclidean p-brane Carroll limits', introduces the 'Alice algebra' and 'Alice particle' as a Carrollian counterpart to the Bargmann algebra/particle, derives their actions from relativistic limits and null reductions, and connects the Alice particle to a D0*-brane in type IIA* string theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify Galilean and Carrollian limits by reinterpreting p-brane Galilei limits with Euclidean worldvolumes, thereby revealing Carrollian structure when time becomes transversal; to explore resulting symmetries, particle dynamics, and string-theoretic embeddings.

Method: Mathematical analysis of non-relativistic limits: performing generalized (p-brane) Galilei limits with Euclidean worldvolume metrics to obtain Carrollian structures; constructing centrally extended spacetime algebras (Alice algebra); deriving particle actions via limit and null reduction techniques; analyzing dynamics in (A)dS backgrounds; linking to string theory via decoupling limits of D-branes.

Result: Introduction of Euclidean p-brane Carroll limits; derivation of the Alice algebra as a central extension of Carroll symmetries for p=0; formulation of Alice particle action dual to Bargmann particle; stability of Bargmann/Alice particles in AdS/dS respectively; realization of both actions via null reduction from massless particles in spacetimes with one/two times; identification of Alice particle as decoupling limit of D0*-brane in type IIA* theory.

Conclusion: Galilean and Carrollian limits are two sides of a unified geometric framework rooted in p-brane limits with Lorentzian vs. Euclidean worldvolumes; the Alice particle and its algebra provide a consistent Carrollian counterpart to non-relativistic physics, with implications for symmetry classification, particle dynamics, and string theory duality. 

Abstract: In generalized, also known as $p$-brane Galilei limits, the speed of light $c$ becomes infinite in the directions transverse to a $(p+1)$-dimensional Lorentzian worldvolume. In this paper, we explain that allowing the worldvolume to be Euclidean, and thus time to be transversal, $p$-brane Galilei limits turn into Carrollian ones, that we refer to as "Euclidean $p$-brane Carroll limits", in which $c$ goes to zero in the $p+1$ worldvolume directions. This leads to a unified approach to taking Galilean and Carrollian limits, whose consequences we explore for $p=0$. We show that the spacetime symmetries that arise from the Euclidean 0-brane Carroll limit can be centrally extended to what we will call the Alice algebra, similar to how the Bargmann algebra centrally extends the Galilei symmetries. This gives rise to the novel notion of an Alice particle, and we obtain the Bargmann and Alice particle actions from a unified limit of the action of a relativistic massive particle or tachyon, suitably coupled to a one-form gauge potential. In the presence of a cosmological constant, we find that the Bargmann and Alice particles undergo stable motion for negative and positive cosmological constant, respectively. Finally, we show that the Bargmann and Alice particle actions can be obtained from null reduction of a massless particle action in a relativistic spacetime with one and two times. Our results indicate that in 10 dimensions, the Alice particle is a decoupling limit of a D$0{}^*$-brane in Hull's type IIA${}^*$ theory, similar to how the Bargmann particle is related to a D$0$-brane in type IIA string theory.

</details>


### [40] [Chern Character for Discrete Spectrum Partition Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05136)
*Shunrui Li,Yang Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a geometric correspondence between quantum thermal partition functions and the Chern character of a 'virtual physical sheaf' over spacetime, using $U(1)$-equivariant Hamiltonian dynamics on $\mathbb{CP}^n$, Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch theory, and trace-class extensions to infinite dimensions.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Result: The finite-temperature partition function is shown to equal the spacetime integral of the Chern character of the virtual physical sheaf; thermal traces are proven topologically invariant under compactification on arbitrary manifolds.

Conclusion: Partition functions admit a rigorous geometric interpretation via characteristic classes, bridging operator-algebraic quantum mechanics and algebraic topology—enabling new tools for QFT and spectral geometry.

Abstract: We establish a rigorous geometric correspondence between thermal partition functions of discrete-spectrum quantum systems with bounded ground energy and the Chern character of "virtual physical sheaf" over spacetime. By interpreting Hamiltonian dynamics as a $U(1)$-equivariant flow on the quantum phase space $\mathbb{CP}^n$ and pushforward to spacetime, we show that the finite-temperature partition function emerges as the integral of the Chern character of "virtual physical sheaf" over spacetime. The construction extends naturally to infinite dimensions through trace class guaranteed by Weyl's aspmtotic law. Using the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch formalism, we prove pushforward invariance of the Chern character under thermal compactification on arbitrary manifolds, providing a topological foundation for thermal traces in quantum field theory. This framework unifies spectral theory with characteristic class theory, offering a geometric interpretation of partition functions based on operator-algebraic approach.

</details>


### [41] [Effective Field Theory of Noncritical M-theory from Bosonization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05143)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct an effective spacetime gravity description of noncritical M-theory (a 2+1d Fermi liquid framework for noncritical string vacua) by generalizing bosonization via the coadjoint orbit method, bridging collective field theory, integrability, and semiclassical correspondence.

Method: Extends the Delacretaz-Du-Mehta-Son coadjoint orbit bosonization formalism to double-scaled non-relativistic Fermi liquids with central inverted harmonic oscillator potential; employs gauge fixing to reduce the 2+1d theory to a family of interacting chiral bosons; leverages integrability to compute fermion density correlation functions order-by-order in perturbation theory.

Result: Reproduces Das-Jevicki collective field theory in 1+1d; derives a novel effective field theory for Hořava–Keeler’s 2+1d noncritical M-theory; shows it reduces to coupled chiral bosons; computes density correlators and verifies their agreement with Fermi liquid predictions in the semiclassical limit.

Conclusion: The coadjoint orbit approach successfully unifies bosonization across dimensions and provides a tractable, integrable effective description of noncritical M-theory; this constitutes progress toward a gravity dual and deepens understanding of 2d noncritical strings and 3d quantum gravity.

Abstract: We extend the coadjoint orbit approach to bosonization systematized by Delacretaz-Du-Mehta-Son to double-scaled non-relativistic Fermi liquids with central inverted harmonic oscillator potential. In 1+1d, this reproduces the Das-Jevicki collective field theory describing quasiparticle excitations of the $c=1$ matrix model. In 2+1d, this yields an effective field theory for noncritical M-theory, namely the 2+1d Fermi liquid proposed by Hořava and Keeler as a unified framework for characterizing noncritical string vacua. With a suitable gauge choice this theory reduces to a continuous family of 1+1d chiral bosons resembling Das-Jevicki collective fields coupled by additional interactions. Utilizing the underlying integrability of noncritical M-theory, we compute select fermion density correlation functions order-by-order in perturbation theory, and provide evidence that they agree with their Fermi liquid counterparts in a suitable semiclassical limit. This represents a step toward an effective spacetime gravity description of noncritical M-theory, which we expect to shed light on both the landscape of 2d noncritical strings and 3d quantum gravity in general.

</details>


### [42] [Thermal two-point functions in SYK and complex-time singularities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05258)
*Ilija Burić,Chi-Ming Chang,Ivan Gusev,Elizabeth Helfenberger,Andrei Parnachev,Mukund Rangamani*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the finite-temperature two-point function of the large-N SYK model at intermediate couplings, analyzing its complex-time analytic structure; it identifies leading and next-to-leading singularities governing short-time dynamics and potential holographic geodesic interpretations, persisting down to zero temperature, and discusses emergent geometry via kinematic space.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Result: The leading singularity lies on the imaginary axis, defining an 'effective temperature' controlling short-time operator complexity; the next-to-leading singularity lies outside the thermal strip and persists to T = 0, suggesting a bouncing null geodesic interpretation in the emergent black hole geometry.

Conclusion: Complex-time singularities of the SYK correlator encode universal features of quantum chaos and emergent geometry—even away from strong coupling or the IR limit—and are robust down to zero temperature, supporting a kinematic-space-based geometric interpretation.

Abstract: We analyze the finite-temperature two-point function of the large-$N$ SYK model at intermediate couplings away from the infrared fixed point. Specifically, we examine its analytic structure in the complex time plane, tracking the complex-time singularities over a range of temperatures. The location of the leading singularity lies on the imaginary axis. It controls the short-time dynamics of operator complexity, defining an `effective temperature' for the correlator. The next-to-leading singularity lies outside the thermal strip set by the above effective temperature. It has been argued that this could be interpreted in terms of bouncing null geodesics in the emergent black hole geometry. Both these singularities persist all the way down to zero temperature. We discuss our observations and motivate the related emergent geometry using a kinematic space perspective.

</details>


### [43] [Polynomial Initial-State Jumps and Christoffel Transforms in Krylov Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05294)
*Abhishek Chowdhury,Ajit Prasad Mahapatra*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops an exact analytical framework for computing state Krylov (spread) complexity under polynomial filtering of an initial quantum state, linking spectral measures, orthogonal polynomials, and Lanczos recursion—applicable to both state and operator complexity without re-running Lanczos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the dependence of Krylov complexity on the initial state by providing a closed-form, relative calculus that avoids recomputing Lanczos iterations for each new filtered state.

Method: Uses polynomial filtering $\ket{\psi_Q} = Q(H)\ket{K_0}/\sqrt{N_Q}$, maps spectral measure modification $|Q(E)|^2 d\mu(E)/N_Q$ to finite-band orthogonal polynomial recurrences, derives exact amplitude sums and projected Christoffel–Darboux kernels, and extends to operator Krylov complexity via Liouvillian $\mathcal{L}$ and nested commutators.

Result: Exact finite-sum expressions for Krylov amplitudes and cumulative spread complexity; generalized Christoffel–Darboux kernels handling confluent roots, complex coefficients, support loss, and terminal quotients; validated across Jacobi families (Charlier, Krawtchouk, Chebyshev) and Hermite scaling limit.

Conclusion: A unified, exact relative calculus for polynomially shifted Krylov dynamics is established—enabling efficient generation of complexity profiles from a single solved cyclic problem, with natural extension to operator complexity.

Abstract: State Krylov, or spread, complexity is a property of a pair $(H,\ket{K_0})$ rather than of the Hamiltonian alone. Thus, changing the initial state at fixed $H$ generally changes the Lanczos coefficients and the ordered Krylov basis. We solve this relative initial-state problem for normalized polynomial filters, $\ket{ψ_Q}=Q(H)\ket{K_0}/\sqrt{N_Q}$ with $N_Q=\langle K_0|Q(H)^\dagger Q(H)|K_0\rangle$. The filtered spectral measure is the positive polynomial modification $|Q(E)|^2\mathrm dμ(E)/N_Q$, and orthogonality turns this measure change into a finite-band transfer from reference Fourier-orthogonal-polynomial moments to shifted Krylov amplitudes. We derive exact finite sums for individual amplitudes and projected Christoffel-Darboux kernels for cumulative probabilities and spread complexity. The formulae cover confluent roots, complex seed coefficients, support loss, and terminal quotients in finite dimensions. We evaluate the construction in three canonical Jacobi families, the Heisenberg--Weyl/Charlier oscillator, the compact $SU(2)$/Krawtchouk spin, and the constant-coefficient tight-binding/Chebyshev chain, with a Hermite central-limit scaling of Charlier as a continuous-spectrum check of this Christoffel jump machinery. Finite seed families are organized by a matrix-valued parent measure whose scalar compressions recover the individual shifted problems. The fixed-inner-product construction carries over to operator Krylov complexity after the replacement \(H\mapsto\mathcal L\) and \(\ket{K_0}\mapsto O\); polynomial seeds then become nested-commutator descendants \(Q(\mathcal L)O\). The result is an exact relative calculus in which a solved cyclic problem generates a family of polynomially related initial-state dynamics without repeating Lanczos in the original Hilbert space.

</details>


### [44] [Cosmological Correlators in KLF and the Double-Exchange](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05327)
*Nathan Belrhali,Arthur Poisson,Sébastien Renaux-Petel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces the Kontorovich-Lebedev-Fourier (KLF) space formalism to compute tree-level cosmological correlators in de Sitter space, replacing nested time integrals with frequency integrals over rational propagators and vertex functions; it derives analytic properties (singularities, residues, asymptotics) of vertex functions via Lauricella series, enabling direct diagrammatic evaluation—demonstrated on the double-exchange diagram—yielding compact double-series expressions in hypergeometric functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a more efficient, analytically transparent method for computing tree-level cosmological correlators in de Sitter space, bypassing cumbersome in-in time integrals and differential equation approaches, and enabling clear physical separation of background and collider signal contributions.

Method: Uses the Kontorovich-Lebedev-Fourier (KLF) space formalism to convert in-in time integrals into frequency integrals; derives analytic properties (poles, residues, large-frequency asymptotics) of general n-field vertex functions via their integral and Lauricella series representations; applies contour integration and residue calculus in frequency space to directly evaluate tree-level diagrams.

Result: Full analytic characterization of generic vertex functions—including singularity structure and asymptotics—and a complete, closed-form computation of the double-exchange diagram expressed as a double series over hypergeometric functions, cleanly separating background and cosmological collider contributions.

Conclusion: The KLF space formalism provides a powerful, diagrammatic, and analytically tractable framework for tree-level cosmological correlators in de Sitter space, significantly simplifying computations and enhancing physical interpretability compared to standard time-domain or differential-equation methods.

Abstract: In this work, we present the procedure to find series representations of tree-level cosmological correlators using the Kontorovich-Lebedev-Fourier (KLF) space formalism. This framework allows us to trade the in-in nested time integrals for frequency integrals over rational propagators and vertex functions, which encode interactions among quantum fields on a de Sitter background. Because these functions are the key objects to understand in order to perform a diagrammatic computation, we derive their relevant analytic properties by using both their integral representation and series representation in terms of Lauricella functions. For a vertex involving any number of fields, we obtain the location of singularities, the corresponding residues and the large-frequency asymptotic behaviour. Gathering these properties at each frequency integration allows us to compute a tree-level correlator directly, without relying on the differential equations it satisfies. To illustrate this procedure, we provide a complete treatment of the double-exchange diagram. The computation naturally distinguishes the different physical contributions, whether to the background or to the cosmological collider signal. The newly derived result is expressed at most in terms of a double series over hypergeometric functions, which simplifies the analytical expression of the correlator.

</details>


### [45] [Does QFT make sense in non-integer dimensions?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05338)
*Diego Delmastro,Yan-Yan Li,Dikshant Rathore*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates analytic continuation of quantum field theories (QFTs) to complex fractional spacetime dimensions, revealing branch cuts in theories like QCD and QED, with associated discontinuities in operator scaling dimensions and OPEs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the analytic structure of QFTs under dimensional continuation, especially how conformal data (e.g., scaling dimensions, OPE coefficients) behave as functions of complex dimension d.

Method: Analytic continuation of QFTs to complex d; examination of operator spectrum, OPE structure, and scaling dimension dependence on d in gauge theories including QCD and QED.

Result: Demonstrates that standard gauge theories exhibit branch cuts in the complex d-plane, implying non-analyticity and discontinuous behavior of CFT data (e.g., anomalous dimensions, OPE coefficients) across certain d-values.

Conclusion: Fractional-dimensional continuation of realistic QFTs is obstructed by branch cuts, challenging naive dimensional regularization assumptions and highlighting intrinsic non-analyticity in d for physical observables.

Abstract: We revisit the old problem of analytically continuing QFTs to fractional dimension $d\in\mathbb C$. We observe that common theories like QCD and QED have branch cuts in the complex $d$ plane. In particular, many operators in their low-energy CFT have OPEs and scaling dimensions that jump as a function of $d$.

</details>


### [46] [Charge-Sector Construction of the Type-IIB Axion--Dilaton Wormhole Partition Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05385)
*Soo-Jong Rey*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs the Type-IIB axion-dilaton wormhole partition function from charge-sector data, expressing it as a Fourier series in the theta variable with coefficients derived from saddle-point calculations and constrained by symmetry, positivity, and analyticity conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and characterize the wormhole partition function in Type-IIB string theory using axion charge (flux) sector data, enabling control over its theta dependence and consistency with quantum gravity constraints.

Method: Uses long-distance saddle-point approximation in a fixed axion charge (form-field flux) sector to derive a two-end operator term with coefficient matrix C^{ij}_ν; applies reduction data b to obtain scalar coefficients W_ν[b]; expresses the partition function Z_wh(θ;b) as a Fourier sum ∑_ν W_ν[b] e^{iνθ}; analyzes constraints including discrete-symmetry covariance, moment positivity, Cauchy–Schwarz bounds, and asymptotic (dilute Bessel/Skellam) behavior.

Result: Derives explicit form of Z_wh(θ;b) as a Fourier transform of charge-sector scalars W_ν[b]; establishes rigorous constraints on W_ν[b]—including phase properties, absolute bounds, positivity of moments, and analyticity in complex θ—thereby delimiting consistent low-energy effective descriptions of wormholes in quantum gravity.

Conclusion: The axion-dilaton wormhole partition function is fully determined by charge-sector data and subject to stringent consistency conditions arising from quantum gravity principles; its theta dependence encodes topological and statistical properties of flux sectors, with implications for the Swampland and ensemble averaging.

Abstract: I construct the Type-IIB axion--dilaton wormhole partition function from charge-sector data. In a chosen axion charge, equivalently form-field flux sector, the long-distance saddle calculation supplies a two-end operator term with coefficient matrix \(C^{ij}_ν\). The labels \(i,j\) label end-insertion operators; the labels \(A,B\) label parent universes. Reduction data \(b\) convert this matrix into scalar coefficients \(W_ν[b]\). The wormhole partition function in the theta variable is \(Z_{\rm wh}(θ;b)=\sum_νW_ν[b]\e^{iνθ}\). I analyze properties and constraints this coefficients satisfy: discrete-symmetry covariance, phase, absolute bounds, moment positivity, Cauchy--Schwarz inequalities for the unreduced coefficient matrix, complex-\(θ\) domains, charge-lattice tails, and the dilute Bessel/Skellam limit. The \(θ\)-dependence of the wormhole partition function is the Fourier transform of the charge-sector scalar coefficients.

</details>


### [47] [Liénard--Wiechert fields in AdS and flat-space antipodal matching from geodesic-centered Coulombic data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05395)
*Sarthak Duary*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides a geometric derivation of Liénard–Wiechert fields in both flat spacetime and global anti-de Sitter (AdS) space, unifying the origin of antipodal matching via geodesic-adapted coordinates and embedding-space invariants; it constructs a closed-form AdS analogue of the Coulombic Liénard–Wiechert field, proves its exact antipodal covariance, and shows its null-fringe limit recovers flat-space antipodal matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the geometric origin of antipodal matching in asymptotic symmetries—particularly between past and future null infinity—and to extend the classical Liénard–Wiechert solution consistently to global AdS, where uniform motion is replaced by geodesic motion, thereby bridging flat-space radiation theory and AdS boundary dynamics.

Method: Uses geodesic-centered coordinates adapted to the timelike worldline of a charge; constructs static Coulomb solutions in those frames; transforms to global (e.g., Poincaré or global AdS) coordinates using embedding-space invariants; computes gauge-invariant field components (e.g., $F_{\rho\tau}$); analyzes asymptotic behavior at null infinity ($\mathscr{I}^\pm$) and the null-fringe limit; employs image-charge interpretation for conceptual unification.

Result: Derives a closed-form, gauge-invariant AdS Liénard–Wiechert field with explicit $F_{\rho\tau}$ component; proves exact antipodal covariance of the bulk field in global AdS; shows that its null-fringe limit reproduces the standard flat-space antipodal matching condition between $\mathscr{I}^+_{-}$ and $\mathscr{I}^-_{+}$; provides a unified geometric picture linking Coulomb seeds, antipodal symmetry, and the AdS-to-flat limit.

Conclusion: Antipodal matching arises fundamentally from coordinate transformations away from geodesic-centered frames—not from radiation per se—and persists as an exact symmetry in global AdS. The Liénard–Wiechert field admits a robust geometric generalization to AdS, reinforcing deep connections between asymptotic symmetries, embedding geometry, and holographic correspondence.

Abstract: We give a geometric derivation of Li'enard--Wiechert fields in flat space and in global AdS, emphasizing the origin of antipodal matching. In flat space, the field of a uniformly moving charge is obtained by using coordinates centered on the source timelike geodesic, where the charge is at rest and the solution is the ordinary Coulomb field. The nontrivial angular dependence of the leading data at null infinity arises only after rewriting this static solution in a non-centered frame, making the antipodal matching condition transparent. We extend the same construction to global AdS, where uniform motion is replaced by motion along a timelike geodesic. Starting from the static Maxwell solution at the AdS center, we use geodesic-adapted coordinates and embedding-space invariants to reconstruct the field in arbitrary global coordinates. This yields a closed-form AdS analogue of the Coulombic Li'enard--Wiechert field, including the gauge-invariant component $F_{ρτ}$. We show that the bulk field satisfies exact antipodal covariance, and that its null-fringe limit reproduces the standard flat-space antipodal matching relation between the Coulombic data at $\mathscr I^+*-$ and $\mathscr I^-*+$. We also describe an image-charge interpretation of the Coulomb seed, giving a unified picture of Coulombic fields, antipodal matching, and the AdS-to-flat-space limit.

</details>
