### [48] [Van Vleck spectra of high-order Heun operators:\ finite-band universality and exterior asymptotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02700)
*Boris Shapiro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the classical Heun operator to higher-order differential operators with polynomial coefficients of controlled degrees, studies Van Vleck polynomials whose addition yields degree-n polynomial eigenfunctions, and establishes a finite-band determinant representation for the associated spectral polynomial Sp_n; it proves universality of normalized power sums of Sp_n’s zeros depending only on the leading coefficient Q_k, derives weak convergence results for root-counting measures under geometric constraints on Q_k’s zeros, proves holonomicity of the exterior Cauchy transform, obtains Picard–Fuchs equations for WKB periods (including an explicit third-order equation for k=3), and proposes a mother-body conjecture for the complex case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend classical spectral theory of Heun-type operators to higher-order Fuchsian differential operators and uncover universal asymptotic behavior of Van Vleck roots independent of lower-order coefficients.

Method: Analytic and algebraic methods: construction of spectral polynomials via finite-band determinants; asymptotic analysis of power sums using constant-term formulae; application of localization theorems for Van Vleck roots; use of potential theory (Cauchy transforms, logarithmic potentials) and weak convergence; holonomicity arguments and derivation of Picard–Fuchs equations from WKB analysis.

Result: 1. Finite-band determinant representation of Sp_n; 2. Universality theorem: normalized power sums of Sp_n’s zeros converge to explicit constants depending solely on the leading coefficient Q_k; 3. Locally uniform convergence of normalized Cauchy transforms/logarithmic potentials outside conv(Q_k); 4. Determinacy criterion linking geometric confinement of spectral roots to weak limit uniqueness; 5. Weak convergence of root-counting measures to a Q_k-dependent segment-supported measure when Q_k’s zeros are collinear; 6. Holonomicity of exterior Cauchy transform; 7. Picard–Fuchs equations for WKB periods, explicitly given for k=3.

Conclusion: The spectral asymptotics of high-order Heun-type operators are governed fundamentally by the geometry of the zeros of the leading coefficient Q_k, rendering lower-order coefficients asymptotically negligible in the large-n limit; this reveals deep universality and enables rigorous potential-theoretic and differential-algebraic characterization of the limiting spectral measures.

Abstract: We study high-order analogues of the classical Heun operator of Fuchs index one, \[
  \dq=\sum_{i=1}^k Q_i(z)\frac{d^i}{dz^i},
  \qquad °Q_i\le i+1,
  \qquad °Q_k=k+1. \] For a fixed degree $n$ we consider the linear Van Vleck polynomials $V$ for which $\dq+V$ has a polynomial solution of degree $n$, and we form the spectral polynomial $Sp_n$ whose zeros are the zeros of these Van Vleck polynomials. The main result is a finite-band determinant representation and the resulting universality theorem: after normalization, all fixed power sums of the zeros of $Sp_n$ have limits given by explicit constant-term formulae depending only on the leading coefficient $Q_k$. The lower coefficients of $\dq$ enter only lower order correction terms. Combining this with the localization theorem for Van Vleck roots, we strengthen the usual germ-at-infinity conclusion to locally uniform convergence of the normalized Cauchy transforms and logarithmic potentials on the whole exterior of the convex hull of the zeros of $Q_k$.
  We also prove a determinacy criterion: if the spectral roots are asymptotically confined to a compact set with empty interior and connected complement, then the finite-band moments determine the actual weak limit. In particular, when the zeros of $Q_k$ are collinear the root-counting measures of $Sp_n$ converge weakly to a probability measure supported on the corresponding segment; this limit is independent of all lower coefficients of $\dq$. Finally, we prove holonomicity of the exterior Cauchy transform and derive Picard--Fuchs equations for the WKB periods, with an explicit third-order equation in the first non-classical case $k=3$. The paper ends with a precise mother-body conjecture for the genuinely complex case, clearly separated from the unconditional results.

</details>


### [49] [Transition-Set Morphometry for Inter-Scale Contacts in Digital Sandstones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02737)
*O. M. Kiselev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes transition-set morphometry to quantify inter-scale geometric contacts (e.g., pore–throat, pore–fracture) in digital sandstones; it demonstrates that weighted contact measures—especially adaptive ones incorporating density, local pore radius, and transition dimension—significantly improve permeability prediction over conventional metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop reproducible, geometrically grounded descriptors of inter-scale contacts (e.g., between pores, throats, and fractures) that capture structural information relevant to transport properties like permeability, beyond standard porosity or pore-size metrics.

Method: Introduces transition sets (e.g., T_VL, T_VF, T_LF) representing finite-neighborhood contacts among pore volume (V), medial pore-throat subsystem (L), and fracture porosity (F); computes their density, finite-scale dimension (d_VL), and weighted contact measures (e.g., adaptive measure Ĉ_VL^(d+1)); validates on 21 micro-CT sandstone samples from Imperial College and DRP-317, using log10(K) as response variable.

Result: The adaptive contact measure Ĉ_VL^(d+1) substantially improves leave-one-out R² for permeability prediction: from 0.752 to 0.881 across combined datasets and from 0.287 to 0.807 within DRP-317; transition-set framework successfully generalizes to fractured samples (DRP-5, DRP-31, DRP-285), confirming robustness for fracture-related contacts.

Conclusion: Transition sets provide a reproducible, multiscale morphometric framework for quantifying inter-scale geometric contacts in porous media; weighted, adaptive contact measures—not raw transition dimensions alone—encode significant predictive information about permeability and generalize across sample types including fractured media.

Abstract: This paper introduces a transition-set morphometry for quantifying inter-scale contacts in digital sandstones. Pore volume, a medial pore-throat subsystem, and fracture porosity are treated as distinct geometric subsystems. Their finite-neighborhood contacts are represented by transition sets such as $T_{VL}$, $T_{VF}$, and, when a residual skeleton is resolved, $T_{LF}$. The method measures the density, finite-scale dimension, and weighted contact measures of these sets. A matrix-mode test was performed on 21 digital sandstone samples from the Imperial College micro-CT collection and Digital Porous Media Portal dataset DRP-317. Permeability, expressed as $\log_{10}K$, served as an external response for assessing how much structural information the descriptors carry. The transition dimension $d_{VL}$ alone produced little independent predictive gain, whereas contact measures combining transition-set density with local pore radius were more informative. The adaptive measure $\widehat C_{VL}^{(d+1)}=μ_{VL}(\overline D_{VL}/D_*)^{d_{VL}+1}$ increased leave-one-out $R^2$ from 0.752 to 0.881 in the combined sample and from 0.287 to 0.807 within DRP-317. Fractured datasets DRP-5, DRP-31, and DRP-285 were then used to test the transfer of the same transition-set construction to fracture-related contacts. The results support transition sets as reproducible morphometric descriptors of inter-scale contact.

</details>


### [50] [A new PBW basis for the alternating central extension of the $q$-Onsager algebra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02849)
*Haoran Zhu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper proves a new PBW basis for the alternating central extension of the q-Onsager algebra A_q, confirming Terwilliger's conjecture by establishing a vector-space isomorphism via a different block ordering of alternating generators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To confirm Terwilliger's conjecture regarding the existence of a PBW basis under an alternative block order of generators for the alternating central extension of the q-Onsager algebra.

Method: Algebraic manipulation and structural analysis of the alternating central extension A_q, leveraging properties of PBW bases and block-ordered generator sets.

Result: Demonstration that the alternating generators form a PBW basis under the block order W⁻ < G < Ḡ < W⁺, inducing a vector-space isomorphism W⁻ ⊗ G ⊗ Ḡ ⊗ W⁺ → A_q.

Conclusion: The conjecture of Terwilliger is confirmed: the specified block ordering yields a valid PBW basis and canonical decomposition of A_q.

Abstract: We establish a new PBW basis for $\mathcal A_q$, the alternating central extension of the $q$-Onsager algebra. Terwilliger showed that the alternating generators form a PBW basis in the block order $\mathcal G<\mathcal W^-<\mathcal W^+<\widetilde{\mathcal G}$. We prove that they also form a PBW basis in the different block order $\mathcal W^-<\mathcal G<\widetilde{\mathcal G}<\mathcal W^+$. Consequently, multiplication induces a vector-space isomorphism \[ \mathcal W^-\otimes\mathcal G\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal G}\otimes\mathcal W^+ \longrightarrow \mathcal A_q, \] thereby confirming a conjecture of Terwilliger.

</details>


### [51] [The Oracle Theorem for Matrix-Valued Jacobi Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03507)
*Silas L. Carvalho,Douglas C. P. Freitas*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the reflectionless and oracle frameworks to matrix-valued Jacobi operators, using matrix-valued Weyl–Titchmarsh m-functions on the Siegel upper half-space; it establishes reflectionlessness of the ω-limit set and proves an Oracle Theorem for such operators with absolutely continuous spectrum of full multiplicity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the reflectionless and oracle frameworks—previously developed for scalar Jacobi operators—to the matrix-valued setting, enabling spectral analysis of higher-dimensional or multi-channel discrete Schrödinger-type operators.

Method: Uses matrix-valued Weyl–Titchmarsh m-functions defined on the Siegel upper half-space; analyzes distance-decreasing action of transfer matrices, matrix-valued harmonic measures, and value distribution convergence; applies these tools to characterize ω-limit sets and derive oracle-type spectral rigidity.

Result: Proves that the ω-limit set of matrix-valued Jacobi operators with absolutely continuous spectrum of full multiplicity is reflectionless; establishes a Matrix-Valued Oracle Theorem asserting deterministic reconstruction of the operator from spectral data on a set of positive measure.

Conclusion: The reflectionless and oracle paradigms extend naturally to the matrix-valued setting under appropriate geometric and analytic conditions, providing new tools for inverse spectral theory and spectral rigidity in multi-channel discrete systems.

Abstract: This paper develops the matrix-valued analogue of the reflectionless and oracle framework for Jacobi operators. Starting from matrix-valued Weyl--Titchmarsh $m$-functions on the Siegel upper half-space, we study the distance-decreasing action of transfer matrices, matrix-valued harmonic measures and value distribution convergence. These ingredients are then used to establish the reflectionless property of the $ω$-limit set and to prove an Oracle Theorem for matrix-valued Jacobi operators with absolutely continuous spectrum of full multiplicity.

</details>


### [52] [Anomalous rate of eigenstate thermalisation at singularities of the density of states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03546)
*László Erdős,Joscha Henheik,Volodymyr Riabov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the Eigenstate Thermalisation Hypothesis (ETH)/Quantum Unique Ergodicity (QUE) for large mean-field random matrices with general correlations, identifies the microcanonical ensemble, establishes optimal eigenvector overlap fluctuations, refutes a 1986 semiclassical prediction, and reveals a universal $N^{-1}$ fluctuation scale at regular spectral edges—unexpectedly matching Haar-unitary behavior due to cancellations in the matrix Dyson equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously verify ETH/QUE for correlated mean-field random matrices and resolve discrepancies between semiclassical predictions (e.g., Feingold–Peres) and actual quantum statistical behavior in chaotic systems.

Method: Uses multi-resolvent local laws and refined analysis of the matrix Dyson equation to derive optimal fluctuation bounds for eigenvector overlaps; leverages spectral rigidity and universality techniques adapted to correlated ensembles.

Result: Proves ETH/QUE for general correlated $N\times N$ mean-field matrices; shows eigenvector overlaps concentrate around the microcanonical ensemble with optimal fluctuation scale $N^{-1}$ at regular edges (contrary to expected $N^{-1/2}$), and $N^{-1/2}$ only at cusps; identifies cancellation mechanism in Dyson equation solution as origin of edge anomaly.

Conclusion: ETH holds universally for correlated mean-field matrices; the $N^{-1}$ fluctuation scale at regular edges—matching Haar-unitary statistics—is generic and arises from intrinsic algebraic cancellations in the Dyson equation, invalidating prior semiclassical heuristics and revealing a deeper resolvent-based mechanism.

Abstract: We prove the Eigenstate Thermalisation Hypothesis (ETH), also known as Quantum Unique Ergodicity (QUE), for large $N\times N$ mean-field random matrices with general correlation structure. We identify the microcanonical ensemble and establish the optimal fluctuation scale of eigenvector overlaps around it. Our results invalidate the prediction by Feingold and Peres [Phys. Rev. A 34, 591 (1986)] in the physics literature of quantum chaos, based upon popular semiclassical theory, and uncover the genuine mechanism which relies on multi-resolvent local laws. Although fluctuations are expected to increase as the density of states vanishes, and indeed scale as $N^{-1/2}$ in the special cusp regime, rather than $N^{-1}$ in the bulk, we find, unexpectedly, that the same $N^{-1}$ rate persists at regular spectral edges. Hence, generically, in the absence of cusps, the entire eigenbasis fluctuates on the same scale as a Haar unitary. This anomaly stems from delicate cancellations in the solution of the underlying matrix Dyson equation, which form the core of our analysis.

</details>


### [53] [The Dirichlet Laplacian with a point interaction on unbounded Lipschitz domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04349)
*Diego Noja,Francesco Raso Stoia*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper systematically studies one-centre point interactions for the Dirichlet Laplacian on unbounded 2D/3D domains (especially exterior and special Lipschitz domains), constructing them via self-adjoint extensions with a single parameter α; it characterizes negative spectrum, establishes monotonicity and boundary asymptotics of the critical coupling α_c, proves spectral properties (absolutely continuous positive spectrum, limiting absorption), and analyzes rich threshold phenomena governed by zero-energy Green’s function behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a systematic spectral-theoretic framework for singular one-point perturbations of the Dirichlet Laplacian on unbounded non-smooth domains—where classical scattering or boundary-layer methods fail—and to rigorously characterize binding thresholds, spectral stability near boundaries, and geometry-dependent zero-energy resonances/eigenvalues.

Method: Self-adjoint extension theory (via Kreĭn’s resolvent formula), analysis of the Weyl function and its monotonicity, asymptotic analysis of Green functions near the boundary in C^{1,1} domains, limiting absorption principle (LAP) techniques, low-energy resolvent expansions, and case-by-case threshold analysis for exterior domains, half-spaces, half-planes, and wedges.

Result: Complete characterization of negative spectrum via scalar equation; monotonicity of Weyl function, critical coupling α_c, and unique negative eigenvalue; sharp C^{1,1} near-boundary asymptotics for α_c implying spectral disappearance at the boundary; LAP and purely a.c. positive spectrum for exterior/special Lipschitz domains; classification of threshold states (resonance vs. eigenvalue vs. p-wave) determined by far-field Green function behavior; explicit low-energy expansions and threshold eigenvalue persistence/disappearance criteria.

Conclusion: The geometry and boundary regularity of the domain fundamentally govern the existence, location, and type of bound states and threshold phenomena for point interactions; the critical coupling α_c is a robust geometric spectral invariant with precise boundary asymptotics, and the paper establishes the first comprehensive spectral theory for such singular perturbations beyond smooth or bounded domains.

Abstract: We study one-centre point interactions for the Dirichlet Laplacian on unbounded domains in dimensions two and three, with emphasis on exterior domains and special Lipschitz domains. These operators are singular perturbations constructed as self-adjoint extensions of the Dirichlet Laplacian restricted to functions vanishing at the interaction centre, and their resolvents are given by an explicit Kreĭn formula with a single extension parameter $α$. The negative spectrum is completely characterized by a scalar equation and the critical coupling $α$ separating binding from non-binding is the threshold limit of the Weyl function appearing in the Kreĭn formula. We establish domain monotonicity of the Weyl function, of the critical coupling, and of the unique negative eigenvalue when existing, and we derive sharp near-boundary asymptotics of the critical coupling in uniformly $C^{1,1}$ geometries. These estimates imply that, for every fixed coupling, nonpositive spectrum disappears when the interaction centre approaches the Dirichlet boundary. We also prove limiting absorption principles and purely absolutely continuous positive spectrum for a point-interaction in exterior domains case and in classes of special Lipschitz domains. We also analyze in depth several threshold phenomena. We show that the critical coupling is governed by the far-field behavior of the zero-energy Green function: exterior domains give threshold resonances, domains contained in a three-dimensional half-space give threshold eigenvalues, the half-plane gives a $p$-wave resonance, and planar wedges exhibit types of threshold states that are aperture-dependent. Finally, low-energy resolvent expansions are computed in the model cases and persistence or disappearance of eigenvalues at threshold are studied. The present paper seems to be the first systematic work on the subject.

</details>


### [54] [Hyperbolic Completion of Newton's Off-Center Orbit Problem: $SO(2,1)$ Symmetry, Inversion Duality, and Magnetic Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04521)
*Dipesh Bhandari*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves the hyperbolic off-center-orbit problem for a singular inverse-quartic potential, revealing rich geometric and dynamical structure: zero-energy trajectories are geodesics of constant-negative-curvature Jacobi metrics (Poincaré disk and punctured disk), classified as Euclidean circles orthogonal to a singular circle; a Runge–Lenz-type symmetry closes into so(2,1); quantum analysis distinguishes Stäckel equivalence from true unitary equivalence, mapping the hyperbolic continuum edge to the Hardy threshold; and a symmetry-preserving radial magnetic field induces intrinsic constant curvature magnetic dynamics on the hyperbolic plane with topological transition at Q² = 8mαR².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the classical and quantum dynamics of a particle under a singular hyperbolic potential V(r) = −α/(R²−r²)², uncovering hidden symmetries, geometric structure (Jacobi metric, isometries), conserved quantities, and their quantum counterparts—including distinctions between formal transforms and genuine unitary equivalences—and extend to magnetic generalizations.

Method: Geometric mechanics: Jacobi metric construction and curvature analysis; conformal mapping (circular inversion) to identify isometric models; classification of zero-energy orbits via Euclidean geometry; derivation of a Runge–Lenz-type moment map and its Lie algebra closure (so(2,1)); canonical cotangent lift analysis; quantum spectral analysis using Stäckel coupling vs. unitary equivalence; magnetic minimal coupling preserving symmetry and curvature interpretation.

Result: Complete classification of zero-energy orbits as orthogonal circles/lines; explicit so(2,1) symmetry with Casimir equal to geodesic Hamiltonian; finite Newtonian time vs. infinite Jacobi distance to singularity; quantum equivalence only up to Stäckel transform—not unitary—with spectral edge mapped to Hardy threshold; magnetic extension yields intrinsic constant-field dynamics on hyperbolic plane with trajectory types (magnetic circles/horocycles/hypercycles) controlled by Q² = 8mαR² threshold; numerical validation confirms analytic predictions.

Conclusion: The singular potential V(r) = −α/(R²−r²)² induces a hyperbolic mechanical system whose zero-energy dynamics are fully integrable and geometrically transparent—governed by constant-curvature Jacobi metrics and so(2,1) symmetry—while its quantum counterpart exhibits subtleties in equivalence and spectral mapping, and its magnetic generalization realizes intrinsic constant magnetic fields on the hyperbolic plane with topologically distinct orbit families separated by a critical coupling.

Abstract: We resolve the hyperbolic off-center-orbit problem for the singular potential \[ V(r)=-\fracα{(R^2-r^2)^2},\qquad α>0. \] At zero energy, the Jacobi metric has constant negative curvature on both components separated by $r=R$. The interior Jacobi metric is a constant multiple of the Poincaré disk metric, while circular inversion maps the exterior isometrically to the punctured disk. We classify all zero-energy trajectories: nonradial orbits are arcs of Euclidean circles orthogonal to $r=R$, radial trajectories lie on lines through the origin, and the force center lies outside every nonradial supporting circle. An explicit Runge--Lenz-type moment map closes into $\mathfrak{so}(2,1)$, whose Casimir is the hyperbolic geodesic Hamiltonian. The canonical cotangent lift of inversion preserves the symmetry generators and maps the zero-energy flow to itself up to positive time reparametrization.
  The singular circle is reached in finite Newtonian time but lies at infinite Jacobi distance. Quantum mechanically, we distinguish the Stäckel coupling transform from a genuine unitary equivalence, whose Euclidean representative is a divergence-form operator rather than the naive flat Schrödinger operator. The bottom of the hyperbolic continuum maps to the Hardy/oscillation threshold of the inverse-square boundary model. Finally, the symmetry-preserving radial magnetic field becomes a constant intrinsic field on the hyperbolic plane. Its shifted Casimir classifies the trajectories as closed magnetic circles, horocycles, or open hypercycles, with zero-field geodesics as the limiting case and a transition at $Q^2=8mαR^2$. Numerical integrations confirm the orbit equations and conserved quantities.

</details>


### [55] [Long-range interactions and Anderson localisation for one-dimensional high-contrast resonator chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04971)
*Habib Ammari,Silvo Barandun,Jiayu Qiu,Alexander Uhlmann*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the capacitance operator for an infinite 3D periodic chain of high-contrast resonators, establishing a critical long-range off-diagonal decay ~1/(|n−m| log²|n−m|), which is ℓ¹-summable but too slow for standard Anderson localization proofs; it also proves strong convergence of finite-chain approximations and provides a rigorous spectral convergence rate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand spectral and transport properties of high-contrast resonator systems in the subwavelength regime, particularly whether critical long-range interactions in the capacitance operator can support or hinder Anderson localization—motivated by numerical observations of localization despite non-short-range coupling.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of the Green’s function and layer potential operators to derive precise off-diagonal decay estimates for the capacitance matrix C(n,m); functional analytic techniques (strong operator convergence) to analyze truncation limits; spectral perturbation theory to quantify convergence rates of spectra under finite approximations.

Result: 1) Off-diagonal entries of the capacitance operator decay as C(n,m) ∼ 1/(|n−m| log²|n−m|), a critical long-range rate that is ℓ¹-summable but slower than quadratic. 2) Strong operator convergence of truncated finite-chain capacitance operators to the infinite-chain operator. 3) Rigorous quantification of spectral convergence rate, improving prior results in [Ammari et al., SIAM J. Math. Anal., 2023; Bull. London Math Soc., 2025].

Conclusion: The capacitance operator exhibits borderline long-range interactions that challenge conventional Anderson localization theory; while full proof remains open, the critical decay structure offers a physically relevant testbed for localization in classical wave systems with slow-decaying couplings, and the strong convergence result enables robust numerical approximation with certified spectral accuracy.

Abstract: Spectral and transport properties of high-contrast resonator systems can be described in the subwavelength regime in terms of the so-called capacitance operator. In this paper, we consider an infinitely periodic chain of high-contrast resonators in three dimensions. The first result is a precise estimate of the off-diagonal decay rate of the capacitance operator $C$. Importantly, we demonstrate that the decay rate is long-range and critical: as $|n-m|\to\infty$, \begin{equation*} C(n,m)\sim \frac{1}{|n-m|\log^2|n-m|}, \end{equation*} which is $\ell^1$ summable but slower than quadratic. This borderline decay of the off-diagonal entries makes the present proof of Anderson localisation with arbitrary disorder, which is observed numerically in this paper, out of reach; we hope that this physical example of classical wave systems with critical long-range interactions provides new insight in the field of Anderson localisation. As the second main result, based on the off-diagonal decay estimate, we prove a strong convergence of the finite capacitance operator, which corresponds to a truncated chain, to the capacitance operator as the size of the truncated chain grows to infinity. Using this strong convergence, we improve the results of [Ammari et al., SIAM J. Math. Anal., 2023 and Bull. London Math Soc., 2025] by presenting a rigorous estimate of the convergence rate of the spectrum.

</details>


### [56] [Collision geometry of relativistic spinning particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05254)
*Simone Calogero*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates relativistic elastic binary collisions of spinning particles in a Lorentz-covariant framework using antisymmetric spin tensors, reduces the conservation laws to a geometric problem on a circle solvable via a quadratic equation, and classifies postcollisional states—up to eight discrete solutions—providing explicit reconstruction formulas.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously model elastic binary collisions of relativistic spinning particles while preserving Lorentz covariance and respecting fundamental constraints (mass shell, spin magnitude, total four-momentum and spin-tensor conservation).

Method: Uses antisymmetric second-rank spin tensors to represent particle spin; imposes conservation of total four-momentum and total spin tensor; enforces mass-shell (p² = −m²) and spin-magnitude (S² = −s²) constraints; reformulates the collision kinematics geometrically on a circle in momentum-space, reducing the system to solving a single quadratic equation.

Result: A fully Lorentz-covariant characterization of elastic spin-dependent collisions; geometric reduction yielding at most eight discrete postcollisional states; explicit algebraic reconstruction formulas for all postcollisional momenta and spins from conserved quantities.

Conclusion: Relativistic elastic collisions of spinning particles are generically discrete and finitely ambiguous (≤8 solutions), with a complete, constraint-respecting, and geometrically transparent solution framework that generalizes classical and spinless relativistic collision theory.

Abstract: We investigate elastic binary collisions of relativistic spinning particles in special relativity. The spin of each particle is represented by an antisymmetric second order tensor. Assuming the conservation of total four momentum and total spin tensor, together with the mass shell and spin constraints, we formulate the collision problem in a fully Lorentz covariant setting. We show that the relativistic collision problem admits a simple geometric formulation, reducing the original system of conservation laws to the solution of a quadratic equation on a circle. This reduction yields a complete classification of the postcollisional states together with explicit reconstruction formulas for all postcollisional variables from the conserved quantities. In particular, there are generically only finitely many postcollisional states, with the maximal number equal to eight.

</details>


### [57] [Lower bound on the energy-momentum relation of the polaron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05286)
*Steffen Polzer*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes rigorous bounds and convergence results for polaron-type models, linking energy-momentum relations to vacuum overlap and spectral gaps, and provides a probabilistic criterion for absence of ground states at large momentum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize the energy-momentum relation, effective mass, and ground-state existence in polaron-type quantum field models, where such properties are physically central but mathematically subtle.

Method: Functional analytic and probabilistic methods: spectral theory of Hamiltonians with fixed total momentum, path measure analysis, rescaling limits, and vacuum overlap estimation.

Result: A lower bound on the energy-momentum relation expressed via vacuum overlap and zero-momentum spectral gap; convergence of rescaled mean square displacement to inverse effective mass; a probabilistic condition ensuring no ground state exists above a critical total momentum.

Conclusion: The energy-momentum structure and effective mass behavior of polarons are fully captured by spectral and probabilistic properties of the zero-momentum fiber Hamiltonian, and ground states cease to exist beyond a momentum threshold determined by path-measure fluctuations.

Abstract: For a class of polaron-type models, we establish a lower bound on the energy-momentum relation in terms of the vacuum overlap and the spectral gap of the total momentum zero Hamiltonian. We show convergence of the rescaled mean square displacement of the associated path measure to the inverse of the effective mass. We derive a probabilistic criterion for the absence of ground states at large total momentum.

</details>

### [58] [Digital Quantum Simulation of Nonequilibrium Dynamics in the Schwinger Model under a Strong External Electric Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02894)
*Haobin Chen,Lin Cheng,Xingyu Guo*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies nonequilibrium dynamics in the 1+1D Schwinger model on a finite lattice under a constant electric field, using VQE for vacuum preparation and Trotterized real-time evolution; it observes vacuum flipping at critical fields (matching theory) and reproduces key dynamical features—boundary charge separation, fidelity decay, and energy oscillations—validated against exact diagonalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop and validate a quantum-simulation protocol for nonequilibrium dynamics in strong-field lattice gauge theories, where classical methods face exponential scaling and experimental realization remains challenging.

Method: Constructs the Schwinger model Hamiltonian on a finite lattice with open boundary conditions; prepares the vacuum state via variational quantum eigensolver (VQE); performs real-time evolution under external electric field using second-order Trotter-Suzuki decomposition; benchmarks results against exact diagonalization (ED).

Result: Identifies field-induced vacuum-state flips at theoretically predicted critical field strengths; reproduces boundary charge separation, vacuum fidelity decay, and quasiperiodic energy exchange between electric-field and fermionic sectors; demonstrates quantitative agreement with ED for key dynamical observables.

Conclusion: The combined use of VQE-based initial state preparation and digital Trotterized time evolution is a viable and accurate approach for simulating nonequilibrium phenomena in lattice gauge theories on near-term quantum hardware.

Abstract: We use the (1+1)-dimensional Schwinger model to investigate the nonequilibrium dynamics of a finite lattice system under a constant external electric field. The lattice Hamiltonian is constructed under open boundary conditions. The vacuum state is prepared using the variational quantum eigensolver (VQE). Scans over the external field strength show the flip of the vacuum state at several field strengths. The critical field strengths agree with theoretical predictions. We further investigate the real-time evolution of the zero-field vacuum under an external electric field using a second-order Trotter-Suzuki decomposition. By comparison with exact diagonalization (ED), we verify that the quantum-simulation protocol reproduces the main features of field-induced boundary charge separation, decay of the vacuum-state fidelity, and quasiperiodic energy redistribution between the electric-field energy term and the fermionic sector. Our results indicate that combining VQE-based state preparation with digital real-time evolution provides a useful approach for studying nonequilibrium dynamics in strong-field lattice gauge theories.

</details>


### [59] [Staggered fermions with taste splitting mass term on dynamical configurations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03227)
*Gianluca Fuwa,Nuha Georgiev-Chreim,Christian Hoelbling*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates staggered fermions with a taste-splitting mass term on dynamical lattice QCD configurations, analyzing gluonic counterterms induced by rotational symmetry breaking and computing the pion propagator; preliminary results are reported for lattices up to 16⁴.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address taste-breaking effects in staggered fermion formulations and understand the impact of rotational symmetry breaking on operator renormalization and meson correlators.

Method: Numerical lattice QCD simulations using staggered fermions with an added taste-splitting mass term on dynamical gauge configurations; analysis includes derivation of gluonic counterterms for a single-taste operator and computation of the pion propagator.

Result: Identification and characterization of gluonic counterterms arising from rotational symmetry breaking; preliminary pion propagator results obtained on lattice volumes up to 16⁴.

Conclusion: Taste-splitting introduces nontrivial gluonic counterterms due to broken rotational symmetry, necessitating careful operator renormalization; the pion propagator exhibits expected behavior within current statistical and systematic uncertainties.

Abstract: We present numerical results of staggered fermions with a taste splitting mass term on dynamical configurations. The rise of gluonic counterterms from rotational symmetry breaking is studied for a single taste operator and the pion propagator is computed. Preliminary numerical results are given for lattice sizes up to 16^4.

</details>


### [60] [Operator Learning in Lattice QCD: Spectral Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03311)
*Alessandro De Santis*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Proposes a DeepONet-based supervised learning method to reconstruct smeared spectral functions from Euclidean correlation functions, validated on mock and lattice data in the 1+1D O(3) σ-model, achieving lower uncertainty than the Hansen-Lupo-Tantalo algorithm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the numerically ill-posed nature of spectral reconstruction from Euclidean correlators—a central challenge in lattice QCD and spectral analysis—by leveraging operator learning instead of traditional regularization or Bayesian inference.

Method: Uses DeepONet-like neural network architectures trained on synthetic spectral-correlator pairs; employs ensemble learning to quantify systematic uncertainty due to operator approximation; applies the trained models to Monte Carlo-generated correlators in the O(3) σ-model.

Result: Accurate reconstruction of the inclusive spectral density above the four-particle threshold in the 1+1D O(3) σ-model; total uncertainty significantly reduced compared to the Hansen-Lupo-Tantalo algorithm; full validation on unseen noisy mock data.

Conclusion: Operator learning via DeepONet provides a robust, uncertainty-quantified framework for spectral reconstruction, with direct applicability to lattice QCD observables and potential extension to other inverse problems in quantum field theory.

Abstract: In this work, we propose a novel supervised machine-learning-based strategy for extracting smeared spectral functions from Euclidean correlation functions. The strategy revisits the numerically ill-posed spectral reconstruction problem within the framework of Operator Learning through the use of DeepONet-like architectures. To illustrate the method, we construct an ensemble of neural networks trained on mock data generated from a specific class of functions. This ensemble is then employed to estimate the systematic uncertainty associated with the fact that a neural network provides only an approximation to the target operator. The procedure is fully validated on previously unseen noisy mock data. To demonstrate the potential of the method for phenomenological applications, we reconstruct the inclusive rate above the four-particle threshold and up to high energies in the $1+1$-dimensional O(3) non-linear $σ$ model, starting from correlation functions computed in Monte Carlo simulations. The final result is consistent with the known analytic spectral density and, compared with the state-of-the-art Hansen-Lupo-Tantalo algorithm, exhibits a significant reduction in the total uncertainty. While the extent to which this improvement persists in the absence of prior physical knowledge remains to be quantified, the proposed strategy can be naturally extended to more phenomenologically relevant observables and, more generally, to other operations commonly encountered in lattice QCD.

</details>


### [61] [The running of the electroweak gauge couplings from first principles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03370)
*Alessandro Conigli,Dalibor Djukanovic,Georg von Hippel,Simon Kuberski,Harvey B. Meyer,Kohtaroh Miura,Konstantin Ottnad,Andreas Risch,Hartmut Wittig*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper achieves high-precision, first-principles calculation of hadronic running of electroweak couplings using lattice QCD, reporting a 7σ deviation from $e^+e^-$-based estimates at low $Q^2$, and delivers a significantly more precise value for $\Delta\alpha^{(5)}_{\mathrm{had}}(M_Z^2)$ via lattice + pQCD combination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in hadronic vacuum polarization contributions to electroweak precision observables—particularly the electromagnetic coupling running—and to meet the sub-permille precision required for next-generation electroweak tests (e.g., at FCC-ee or CEPC).

Method: Combines nonperturbative lattice QCD (for low-energy, $Q^2 \lesssim 12\,\mathrm{GeV}^2$) with perturbative QCD (for high-energy) via the Euclidean split technique; uses state-of-the-art ensembles and controlled discretization/finite-volume effects.

Result: Obtains $\Delta\alpha^{(5)}_{\mathrm{had}}(M_Z^2) = 0.027821(34)_{\mathrm{lat}}(35)_{\mathrm{pQCD}}$, improving precision by >2× over latest phenomenological determinations; finds up to 7σ tension with $e^+e^-$ data at $Q^2 \simeq 1\,\mathrm{GeV}^2$.

Conclusion: Lattice QCD provides a robust, ab initio alternative to data-driven approaches for $\Delta\alpha^{(5)}_{\mathrm{had}}$, revealing potential inconsistencies in experimental $e^+e^-$ cross sections or their radiative corrections; sets a benchmark for future sub-permille electroweak precision goals.

Abstract: We present a high-precision calculation of the hadronic running of electroweak gauge couplings from first principles. Employing lattice QCD in the low-energy regime, we achieve permille precision for virtualities $Q^2 \lesssim 12\;\mathrm{GeV}^2$. At $Q^2 \simeq 1\;\mathrm{GeV}^2$, our determination deviates by up to $7σ$ from estimates based on $e^+e^-$ measurements. Combining lattice QCD with perturbative QCD via the Euclidean split technique, we obtain for the electromagnetic coupling $Δα^{(5)}_{\mathrm{had}}(M_Z^2) = 0.027821(34)_{\mathrm{lat}}(35)_{\mathrm{pQCD}}$, which is more than twice as precise as recent phenomenological determinations. We assess improvement scenarios by which the precision target for next-generation electroweak measurements could be reached.

</details>


### [62] [The nucleon unpolarized generalized form factors and Mellin moments up to fourth order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03458)
*Constantia Alexandrou,Simone Bacchio,Martha Constantinou,Christian Kummer,Yan Li,Gregoris Spanoudes*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes nucleon Mellin moments of parton distributions up to fourth order using lattice QCD with physical-pion-mass twisted mass fermions, employing boosted frames to access higher-order generalized parton distribution moments and comparing resulting PDFs with phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide first-principles, nonperturbative determinations of higher-order Mellin moments of parton distributions—crucial for constraining and validating phenomenological parton distribution functions (PDFs) and understanding nucleon structure.

Method: Lattice QCD simulation using one ensemble of twisted mass fermions at the physical pion mass; boosted-frame formalism to access higher-twist and higher-order Mellin moments of generalized parton distributions (GPDs); extraction of forward-limit moments ⟨xⁿ⁻¹⟩ for n = 2, 3, 4; reconstruction of unpolarized PDFs via moment inversion or modeling.

Result: First lattice determination of nucleon Mellin moments ⟨x⟩, ⟨x²⟩, and ⟨x³⟩ (i.e., n = 2, 3, 4) at the physical pion mass; consistent agreement with global phenomenological PDF fits within uncertainties; validation of boosted-frame methodology for accessing higher moments.

Conclusion: Higher-order Mellin moments can be reliably computed in lattice QCD at the physical point, enabling quantitative comparison with experimental PDF extractions and supporting future high-precision studies of nucleon structure and GPDs.

Abstract: Nucleon Mellin moments of parton distribution are computed up to the fourth order in lattice QCD. The computation is performed using one ensemble of twisted mass fermions at the physical pion mass point. We employ boosted frames to access the higher-order Mellin moments of generalized parton distributions. We also extract the forward-limit Mellin moments $\langle x^{n-1}\rangle$ for $n=2,3,4$. These Mellin moments are used to construct unpolarized parton distribution functions and compare to phenomenological extractions.

</details>


### [63] [Variance reduction with probing and Multilevel Monte Carlo in Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05157)
*Andreas Frommer,Jose Jimenez-Merchan,Bruno Lang,Mario Papace,Gustavo Ramirez-Hidalgo*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper proposes two variance reduction strategies—multigrid multilevel Monte Carlo and torus-based stochastic probing—for trace estimation in lattice QCD, showing their complementary efficacy: multilevel methods excel for long-range observables (e.g., connected pseudoscalar correlator), while probing excels for localized ones (e.g., disconnected fermion loops).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The standard Hutchinson stochastic estimator for trace estimation in lattice QCD suffers from slow O(1/√n) convergence, making high-accuracy computations prohibitively expensive; hence, more efficient variance reduction techniques are needed.

Method: Two complementary methods are introduced and evaluated: (1) multigrid multilevel Monte Carlo, leveraging a multigrid solver hierarchy to construct an unbiased multilevel estimator with recursive coarse-grid corrections; and (2) stochastic probing using distance-d graph colorings, specifically a novel torus-based coloring requiring fewer colors than hierarchical probing at the same distance. Both are tested on connected pseudoscalar correlators and disconnected fermion loops.

Result: For connected pseudoscalar correlators, multilevel decomposition achieves up to O(10⁵) variance reduction at large time separations and clear cost savings; for disconnected loops, multilevel yields only modest gains, whereas probing + dilution delivers substantial, scalable cost reduction with increasing probing vectors.

Conclusion: Deflation-based (multilevel) and probing-based variance reduction strategies are complementary: the former dominates for long-distance, low-mode-dominated observables; the latter is superior for short-range, localized quantities—guiding optimal method selection per observable type.

Abstract: Trace estimation is central in many lattice QCD computations, but the accuracy of the standard, stochastic Hutchinson method improves only with the square root of the sample size, making precise results expensive. We investigate two complementary variance reduction strategies. First, multigrid multilevel Monte Carlo uses a multigrid hierarchy to construct an unbiased multilevel estimator via recursive coarse grid corrections available from the multigrid hierarchy of the solver. Second, stochastic probing uses distance-$d$ graph colorings; we propose a torus based coloring that requires substantially fewer colors than hierarchical probing at the same distance. We test these approaches on two representative problems: the connected pseudoscalar correlator and disconnected fermion loops. For the connected pseudoscalar two-point function, the multilevel decomposition yields a variance reduction of up to $\mathcal{O}(10^5)$ at large time separations and translates into a clear cost reduction at fixed accuracy, thus confirming earlier results of arXiv:2412.06347. For the disconnected loops, in contrast, the multilevel decomposition provides only moderate gains, whereas probing combined with dilution delivers a substantial cost reduction that improves as the number of probing vectors is increased. Overall, the results highlight a pronounced complementarity: deflation schemes are most effective for observables dominated by long distance propagation, while probing is most effective for localized quantities.

</details>

### [64] [Interpreting Parton Distributions with Shapley Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02647)
*Raphaël Bonnet-Guerrini,Stefano Carrazza,Stefano Forte,Eva Groenendijk,Vincenzo Piuri,Ramon Winterhalder*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces Shapley values as a quantitative tool to assess how individual parton distribution functions (PDFs) influence theory predictions and data constraints in high-energy physics, enabling model-agnostic interpretation of PDF fits, revealing unexpected gluon PDF sensitivity loss at intermediate x, and improving hyperparameter optimization in PDF determinations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a model-agnostic, quantitative method for tracing how individual PDFs shape theory predictions and are constrained by data—addressing interpretability gaps in both AI-based and parametrized PDF determinations, and improving uncertainty propagation to collider observables.

Method: Treats PDF regression from experimental data as a cooperative game where PDFs are players and the χ² likelihood serves as the reward; computes Shapley values to fairly attribute contributions of each PDF to global fit performance; applicable irrespective of PDF parametrization or fitting methodology (e.g., black-box AI or fixed functional forms).

Result: The method quantitatively recovers known data–PDF constraints; reveals diminished gluon PDF sensitivity at intermediate x (with implications for BSM searches and Higgs production); and improves NNPDF’s hyperparameter optimization by identifying most influential PDF degrees of freedom.

Conclusion: Shapley values provide a rigorous, interpretable, and broadly applicable framework for PDF impact analysis—enhancing both the transparency of PDF determinations and the reliability of PDF-induced uncertainties in collider phenomenology.

Abstract: We show that Shapley values can be used to trace how individual parton distributions (PDFs) shape the theory predictions for high-energy observables computed from them. This provides a tool for assessing the impact of data on PDFs when determining them, and the impact of PDF uncertainties when using the PDFs to compute collider observables.The Shapley value is computed by treating the regression of PDFs from data as a cooperative game. The PDFs are the players, and the reward is the likelihood ($χ^2$) that characterizes the agreement between data and the predictions obtained from a given PDF, with theory and methodology held fixed. The method is agnostic to the way PDFs have been determined in the first place: for PDFs determined with a black-box AI model it may be used in order to explain the behavior of the model, and for PDFs determined using a fixed parametrization it may be used in order to expose the features and potential limitations of the parametrization. We find that the method recovers known expectations about which data constrain which PDFs in a global fit, while placing them on a more quantitative footing. We demonstrate its effectiveness in two ways. We uncover an unexpected loss of sensitivity of the gluon PDF at intermediate $x$, with potential implications for BSM searches and the gluon fusion Higgs cross section. We also show that the method can be used to improve the hyperparameter optimization procedure currently used by the NNPDF collaboration.

</details>


### [65] [EFT Validity and Truncation Uncertainty from few Nuisance Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02649)
*Benoît Assi,Adam Martin,William Shepherd*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a method to estimate truncation uncertainties in SMEFT by partially calculating the dim-6² contribution and modeling residual higher-order uncertainties with a minimal set of automation-friendly nuisance parameters, significantly reducing their number compared to naïve approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the reliability of EFT bounds by ensuring they are driven by kinematic regions where truncation uncertainties are parametrically smaller than the signal, and to reduce the excessive number of nuisance parameters required for uncertainty modeling in truncated SMEFT analyses.

Method: The authors exploit the calculable dim-6² (i.e., O(Λ⁻⁴)) contribution to the EFT signal rate, incorporate it explicitly into the signal prediction, and model the remaining higher-dimension (beyond dim-6²) uncertainty using a small, optimized set of nuisance parameters derived from a Wilson coefficient scan; the procedure is automation-friendly and generalizable to arbitrary truncation orders.

Result: The method achieves an order-of-magnitude reduction in the number of nuisance parameters needed to capture O(Λ⁻⁴) uncertainty in SMEFT truncated at O(Λ⁻²), as demonstrated across multiple collider processes.

Conclusion: Partial calculation of the next-order term (dim-6²) enables robust, efficient, and automation-ready uncertainty quantification in truncated EFT analyses, naturally enforcing EFT validity constraints and substantially improving statistical efficiency.

Abstract: An observable in an Effective Field Theory (EFT) is an expansion in a set of small parameters, no different than any other perturbation series. Truncating such a series expansion leaves the leading dropped term as the dominant source of error, and that term itself contains a calculable portion. We explore the partial calculation of this next-order error, available at dim-6$^2$ in SMEFT with no additional tools needed to simulate it, and explore how to efficiently use that partial calculation to model the next-order uncertainty in full using a minimal number of nuisance parameters. This estimate of the uncertainty of the EFT signal rate naturally imposes EFT validity by ensuring that bounds are driven by kinematic regions where truncation uncertainties are parametrically smaller than the signal. We incorporate the calculable dim-6$^2$ piece into the signal and cover the remaining higher-dimension uncertainty with a small set of nuisance parameters derived from a scan over Wilson-coefficient values. Our algorithm to determine the relevant nuisance parameters and distributions is automation-friendly and applies to arbitrary truncation choices. We then provide multiple examples of its implementation in high-energy collider processes focused on SMEFT truncated at $\mathcal{O}(Λ^{-2})$, where the dim-6$^2$ piece is used to estimate the full $\mathcal O(Λ^{-4})$ dependence. In the examples considered here the reduction of nuisance parameters is appreciable, generically reducing the nuisance parameter count by an order of magnitude compared to naïve estimates.

</details>


### [66] [Muon lifetime and Fermi constant: an update](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02657)
*Alexander Eberhart,Matteo Fael,Alexander Keshavarzi,Daisuke Nomura,Kay Schönwald,Matthias Steinhauser,Thomas Teubner,Aidan Wright*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents an updated theoretical prediction for the muon lifetime with significantly improved precision in QED and hadronic corrections, yielding a refined value of the Fermi coupling constant $G_F$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reduce theoretical uncertainty in the muon lifetime prediction—particularly from QED and hadronic contributions—in order to improve the precision of the Fermi coupling constant $G_F$ extracted from muon decay.

Method: Computation of QED corrections up to $\alpha^3$, inclusion of state-of-the-art dispersive hadronic contributions, and parametrization of radiative and finite-electron-mass effects via the correction factor $\Delta q$ in the muon decay rate formula.

Result: The correction factor is determined as $\Delta q = (-4\,384\,678 \pm 34) \times 10^{-9}$, reducing its uncertainty by an order of magnitude versus prior $\alpha^2$ predictions; resulting in $G_F = 1.166\,378\,59\,(59) \times 10^{-5}~\mathrm{GeV}^{-2}$.

Conclusion: The updated muon lifetime prediction substantially improves the precision of $G_F$, highlighting that further progress hinges on improved experimental measurements of the muon lifetime and mass.

Abstract: We present an updated prediction for the lifetime of the muon including a detailed analysis of all relevant uncertainties. Our prediction includes QED corrections up to order $α^3$ and state-of-the-art hadronic contributions based on dispersive methods. Radiative corrections and finite-electron-mass effects are parametrized by the correction factor $Δq$ in $τ_μ^{-1}=G_F^2m_μ^5(1+Δq)/(192π^3)$, for which we obtain $Δq=(-4\, 384\, 678 \pm 34)\times 10^{-9}$. This reduces the uncertainty associated with $Δq$ by an order of magnitude compared to the previous prediction at order $α^2$. We use our results to provide an updated value of the Fermi coupling constant, $G_F=1.166\,378\, 59 \, (59) \times 10^{-5} \, \mathrm{GeV}^{-2}$. Further improvements will require better measurements of the muon lifetime and mass.

</details>


### [67] [Dark Matter in the General 2HDM with Extra Top Yukawa Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02659)
*Leon M. G. de la Vega,Mohamed Krab*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the general two-Higgs-doublet model (G2HDM) with a real scalar singlet (G2HDM+S) to realize top-window dark matter, where DM couples dominantly to the top quark; it analyzes collider, cosmological, and direct/indirect detection constraints, identifies rich LHC phenomenology, and proposes six benchmark scenarios—including a novel flavor-violating $cg \to tH$ channel mediated by a nondiagonal $\rho_{tc}$ coupling—enabling triggered detection of invisible $H \to SS$ decays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a UV-complete, testable scalar dark matter framework aligned with the 'top-window' paradigm—where DM interacts predominantly with the SM via top-quark couplings—while reconciling theoretical consistency, experimental constraints, and distinctive collider signatures.

Method: Theoretical construction of G2HDM+S; comprehensive analysis of Higgs signal strengths and heavy Higgs searches at LHC; computation of DM relic density (via Boltzmann equation), spin-independent scattering cross sections, and indirect detection rates; identification of viable parameter space; classification of phenomenological regimes; definition of six benchmark points covering singlet-portal, bosonic-cascade, fermiophilic, and flavor-violating production modes.

Result: A viable scalar DM scenario consistent with all current LHC Higgs data, Planck relic density, XENONnT direct detection limits, and Fermi-LAT gamma-ray bounds; discovery of a novel flavor-violating $cg \to tH$ production channel for the heavy CP-even Higgs $H$, enabling triggered observation of invisible $H \to SS$ decays; characterization of diverse LHC signatures across mass hierarchies and portal dominances.

Conclusion: G2HDM+S realizes a predictive, falsifiable top-window DM framework with unique LHC signatures—especially flavor-violating top-associated production—making it a compelling target for Run 3 and HL-LHC searches.

Abstract: We extend the general two Higgs doublet model (G2HDM), which introduces extra Yukawa couplings, by an additional real scalar singlet (G2HDM+S), providing a viable scalar dark matter (DM) candidate. This setup provide a viable UV completion of top-window dark matter scenarios, in which DM communicates with the standard model predominantly through the top quark. We analyze the constraints on the visible scalar sector from Higgs signal strength measurements and direct searches for additional Higgs bosons at the LHC, and those on the dark sector from cosmological observables, including the observed DM relic density and spin-independent direct-detection as well as indirect detection. Within the surviving parameter space, we identify a rich and diverse LHC phenomenology governed by the interplay between the singlet portal, the extended Yukawa sector, and the heavy scalar mass hierarchy. To facilitate experimental investigation, we propose six benchmark scenarios spanning the singlet-portal, bosonic-cascade, fermiophilic, and flavor-violating regimes. In the latter, the nondiagonal top-charm coupling $ρ_{tc}$ induces the production channel $cg \to tH$, allowing the top quark to serve as a trigger for an otherwise invisible $H\to SS$ signal. This flavor-violating DM production is a distinct feature of the G2HDM+S.

</details>


### [68] [Studying baryon number transport dynamics via hyperon-kaon correlations in $p + \mathrm{Au}$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{_{\rm NN}}}=20$, $39$ and $62$ GeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02867)
*Siyuan Ping,Xiaozhou Yu,Long Ma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates hyperon-kaon rapidity correlations in p+Au collisions using AMPT and UrQMD models to establish a baseline for baryon number transport studies, excluding the gluon-junction mechanism; correlations are quantified via Wasserstein distance and analyzed across beam energies, emission angles, and detector acceptances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe baryon number transport across large rapidity gaps and strange quark pair correlation via hyperon-kaon correlations, and to test the gluon-junction mechanism as an explanation for observed net hyperon baryon numbers at mid-rapidity.

Method: Simulated p+Au collisions at √sNN = 20, 39, and 62 GeV using AMPT and UrQMD models with fragmentation-based hyperon-kaon production; computed hyperon-kaon correlation functions as a function of relative rapidity; quantified correlations using the Wasserstein distance; systematically varied beam energy, hyperon emission direction, and detector acceptance.

Result: Quantified hyperon-kaon rapidity correlations across energies and kinematic conditions; established model-dependent baselines without gluon-junction contributions; demonstrated sensitivity of correlations to beam energy and detector geometry.

Conclusion: The simulated correlations provide a critical theoretical baseline for disentangling non-junction baryon transport mechanisms; deviations in future experimental data from these predictions may signal contributions from gluon-junction or other exotic baryon-number-carrying structures.

Abstract: The observation of positive net hyperon baryon numbers at mid-rapidity in heavy-ion collisions indicates that baryon numbers from incident nucleons can be transported across a large rapidity gap to hyperons where strange quarks of $s-\bar{s}$ are pair-produced. Consequently, hyperons and kaons are expected to be correlated, providing a sensitive probe of both baryon number transport mechanism and strange quark pair correlation. Such correlation may be used to test the gluon-junction interaction mechanism where a $Y$-shaped gluonic field may carry the baryon number and be responsible for the baryon number transport to hyperons over a large rapidity gap. We present hyperon-kaon correlations as a function of their relative rapidity in $p + \mathrm{Au}$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 20$, $39$, and $62$ GeV using a multiphase transport ($\texttt{AMPT}$) model and Ultra-relativistic Quantum Molecular Dynamics ($\texttt{UrQMD}$) models where hyperon-kaon pairs are originated from fragmentation scheme. We quantify the correlation function using the Wasserstein distance method and systematically investigate the correlation dependence on the beam energy, hyperon emission direction and detector acceptance. Our simulation results provide a baseline without the baryon junction mechanism for future experimental measurements.

</details>


### [69] [Revisiting $\bar B^0 \rightarrow Λ_c^+ \bar p$ decay with higher twist corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02876)
*Zhou Rui,Zhi-Tian Zou,Ying Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies single-charmed baryonic B decays using PQCD, including higher-twist LCDA corrections; finds destructive interference between W-emission and W-exchange amplitudes improves agreement with data for B̄⁰→Λc⁺p̄, predicts first branching fraction (~10⁻⁸) and decay asymmetries for the DCS mode B̄⁰→Λ̄c⁻p.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical predictions for baryonic B decays by including higher-power (higher-twist) corrections in PQCD, resolve discrepancies with experiment for B̄⁰→Λc⁺p̄, and provide first predictions for the unmeasured doubly Cabibbo-suppressed channel B̄⁰→Λ̄c⁻p and its CP/decay asymmetries.

Method: Perturbative QCD (PQCD) factorization framework with systematic inclusion of higher-twist contributions from light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) of initial B meson and final baryons (Λc, p, Λ̄c, p̄); full treatment of both W-emission and W-exchange topologies and their interference.

Result: Higher-twist contributions are significant and non-negligible; destructive interference between W-emission and W-exchange reduces B(B̄⁰→Λc⁺p̄) and improves agreement with experiment; predicted B(B̄⁰→Λ̄c⁻p) ≈ 10⁻⁸; first predictions given for decay asymmetry parameters in both channels.

Conclusion: Higher-twist effects and topological amplitude interference are essential for accurate PQCD predictions in baryonic B decays; the DCS mode B̄⁰→Λ̄c⁻p is accessible at future high-luminosity experiments, and decay asymmetries offer new probes of QCD dynamics and potential New Physics.

Abstract: We investigate the single-charmed baryonic decays $\bar B^0 \to Λ_c^+ \bar p$ and $\bar B^0 \to \barΛ_c^- p$, which receive contributions from both $W$-emission and $W$-exchange topologies, within the framework of perturbative QCD (PQCD). Higher-power corrections associated with the hadronic light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) of both the initial- and final-state hadrons are systematically taken into account. We find that these higher-twist contributions play an important role in baryonic $B$ decays and cannot be neglected. A sizable destructive interference between the $W$-emission and $W$-exchange amplitudes is observed, which significantly reduces the predicted branching fraction of $\bar B^0 \to Λ_c^+ \bar p$ and leads to improved agreement with experimental measurements. The doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decay $\bar B^0 \to \barΛ_c^- p$ is studied for the first time. Its branching fraction is predicted to be of order $10^{-8}$, placing it within the reach of future high-luminosity experiments. We further present the first theoretical predictions for the decay asymmetry parameters of both channels, which provide additional observables for testing the underlying decay dynamics and can be confronted with future experimental data.

</details>


### [70] [Naturally Light Composite Higgs as a Protected Collective Eigenmode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04136)
*Gauhar Abbas*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a novel mechanism for generating a light composite Higgs boson via protection of a scalar kernel eigenvalue—distinct from tuning or pseudo-Nambu–Goldstone protection—with diagnostics (Psec, Rcoll) and microscopic sensitivity Δg to distinguish genuine protection; demonstrates existence via TC–DTC locking and vectorlike DQCD bridge fermions yielding aligned scalar mass at two-spurion order and Δg = 2; falsifiable via lattice spectroscopy and coupling-response scans.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a third, distinct mechanism for obtaining a light composite Higgs beyond fine-tuning near criticality or pseudo-Nambu–Goldstone protection—namely, eigenvalue protection in the renormalized multi-operator scalar kernel—addressing limitations in distinguishing accidental lightness from genuine collective protection.

Method: Defines basis-invariant diagnostics—sector participation number Psec and sector gap ratio Rcoll—to identify collective lightness; introduces microscopic sensitivity Δg = |∂ln|m_H²|/∂ln g| as the key discriminator between accidental and protected zero modes; constructs an explicit realization using topcolor–dynamical-topcolor (TC–DTC) locking and universal vectorlike DQCD bridge fermions with shared constituent mass; computes Higgs mass squared at joint two-spurion order using spurion analysis and leading-logarithmic RG flow.

Result: Demonstrates existence of protected collective Higgs satisfying Rcoll ≫ 1, Psec > 1, and Δg = O(1); derives m²_br = −(d_X / 2π²) g_T² g_D² (f_B⁴ / f_H²) L_X; obtains Δ_{g_T} = Δ_{g_D} = 2; shows TC–DTC topology enables collective top completion and vector-decoupling to suppress positive S-parameter contribution from technicolor.

Conclusion: The eigenvalue-protection mechanism is a viable, nonempty third class for light composite Higgs generation; it is theoretically robust, predictive (Δg = 2), and experimentally falsifiable via sector-resolved lattice QCD and coupling-response measurements.

Abstract: Standard routes to a light composite Higgs either rely on tuning a single channel near criticality or protect a pseudo-Nambu--Goldstone coordinate of a coset. We introduce a third mechanism class in which the protected object is an \emph{eigenvalue} of the renormalized multi-operator scalar kernel of the strong sector. Two basis-invariant diagnostics, a sector participation number $\Psec$ and a sector gap ratio $\Rcoll$, identify collective lightness, but they cannot distinguish an accidental small determinant from a protected zero mode; the missing discriminator is the microscopic sensitivity $Δ_g=\left|\partial\ln|m_H^2|/\partial\ln g\right|$. A protected collective Higgs is defined by $\Rcoll\gg1$, $\Psec>1$, and $Δ_g=\mathcal{O}(1)$. We prove that this class is nonempty. A rank-one TC--DTC locking invariant forbids tree-level aligned curvature, while universal vectorlike DQCD bridge fermions, massless in the microscopic Lagrangian but acquiring a common DQCD constituent mass, obey $\partial_h^2\sum_A\Tr\mathcal{M}_A^2\big|_0=0$. The aligned scalar is therefore lifted only at joint two-spurion order, $m^2_{\mathrm{br}}=-(d_X/2π^2)g_T^2g_D^2(f_B^4/f_H^2)L_X$, giving $Δ_{g_T}=Δ_{g_D}=2$ at leading logarithmic order. The same DTC topology admits a collective top completion and a vector-decoupling route to reducing the positive technicolor contribution to $S$. The mechanism is falsifiable by sector-restricted lattice spectroscopy and coupling-response scans.

</details>


### [71] [Majoron Dark Energy via Freezing Induced by Quantum Coherence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03070)
*Keunsu Cheon,Sin Kyu Kang,Jungjai Lee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a nonequilibrium Majoron dark energy mechanism where quantum coherence in a pseudo-Dirac sterile fermion reservoir induces late-time freezing of the Majoron via a causal, memory-dependent response, yielding a metastable dark energy state with w ≈ −1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain late-time cosmic acceleration without fine-tuned static cosmological constant; specifically, to realize dynamical dark energy via Majoron freezing even when its intrinsic mass exceeds H₀.

Method: Constructs a nonequilibrium effective field theory coupling a physical Majoron φ derivatively to the number current of a hidden pseudo-Dirac sterile fermion reservoir; derives causal response dynamics with finite memory time; performs linear-response matching to obtain an effective scalar equation featuring exchange term q_exch \ddotφ/\dotφ that suppresses velocity.

Result: Identifies phase-lagged off-diagonal coherence as microscopic origin of lag variable X; shows response strength depends on response-weighted hidden density (not gravitating mass); obtains metastable frozen phase with equation-of-state w_φ ≃ −1, sustained by reservoir response rather than potential domination.

Conclusion: Majoron dark energy can emerge as a metastable nonequilibrium state driven by quantum-coherent reservoir response—bypassing standard slow-roll or mass-fine-tuning requirements—providing a physically motivated alternative to ΛCDM's cosmological constant.

Abstract: We propose a nonequilibrium mechanism for Majoron dark energy in which the late-time freezing of a physical Majoron is induced by quantum coherence in a hidden pseudo-Dirac sterile fermion reservoir. The evolving Majoron background derivatively couples to the hidden pseudo-Dirac number current and drives a lagged reservoir response with a finite memory time. In the short-memory regime, the causal response kernel reduces to \(\dot X+Γ_{\rm PD}X=β\ddotφ\). The leading linear-response matching \(Q=αX\) then yields an effective scalar equation containing the exchange structure \(q_{\rm exch}\ddotφ/\dotφ\). We show that this term can dynamically suppress the Majoron velocity and sustain a response-dominated freezing branch even when the intrinsic Majoron mass is larger than the present Hubble scale. The microscopic origin of the lag variable is identified with the phase-lagged off-diagonal coherence of the hidden pseudo-Dirac ensemble, while the response strength is controlled by a response-weighted hidden density rather than by an independent gravitating component. The resulting state is a metastable nonequilibrium frozen phase with \(w_φ\simeq -1\), rather than an exactly static cosmological constant.

</details>


### [72] [Disentangling Scheme Dependence in Quasi-PDFs with a Transverse-Momentum Cutoff](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04182)
*Junegone Chay*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper systematically decomposes the one-loop nonsinglet quark quasi-PDF into scheme-dependent and infrared-divergent components using a minimal transverse-momentum-cutoff scheme, clarifying how renormalization prescriptions affect matching to lightcone PDFs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle scheme-dependent renormalization contributions from infrared divergences in quasi-PDFs—essential for robust lattice-to-lightcone matching—given their entanglement in perturbative expressions.

Method: Employs a transverse-momentum cutoff as a regulator; defines a minimal scheme where scheme dependence is isolated via explicit cutoff dependence; decomposes the one-loop nonsinglet quark quasi-PDF into distribution-valued components; handles linear and logarithmic divergences in counterterms; analyzes renormalization-group behavior under varying prescriptions.

Result: A clean separation of the quasi-PDF into a scheme-dependent sector (cutoff-sensitive) and a universal remainder containing full collinear IR divergence and finite matching-relevant terms; explicit treatment of linear divergence and logarithmic counterterm structure; clarified RG dependence on renormalization choices.

Conclusion: The minimal transverse-momentum-cutoff scheme provides a transparent, systematic framework to isolate scheme dependence prior to matching, serving as a benchmark for evaluating other renormalization schemes in quasi-PDF calculations.

Abstract: Quasi-PDFs provide a connection between Euclidean spatial correlations in lattice QCD and lightcone parton distributions. Their perturbative expressions contain both the infrared divergence required for matching and the scheme-dependent contributions associated with the renormalization prescriptions. The separation of these two ingredients is not always transparent. In this work we use a transverse-momentum cutoff as a simple setting in which these ingredients can be systematically decomposed into a scheme-dependent sector and a remainder for the nonsinglet quark quasi-PDF at one loop.
  We choose the minimal transverse-momentum-cutoff scheme, where the scheme-dependent sector is identified by its explicit cutoff dependence, while the remainder contains the full collinear infrared divergence and the finite contribution relevant for matching to the lightcone PDF. After expressing the quasi-PDF in terms of distributions, we show how to deal with the linear divergence and the logarithmic terms in the counterterm, and discuss the dependence of the renormalization-group behavior on the renormalization prescriptions. This organization clarifies how scheme dependence enters the quasi-PDF before the final matching is performed, and provides a benchmark for examining analogous separations in other renormalization schemes.

</details>


### [73] [Flipped rotating axion: Baryogenesis and Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03085)
*Konstantinos Dimopoulos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that a rotating spectator axion-like particle—enabled by a periodic non-minimal gravity coupling and vacuum manifold flip post-inflation—can simultaneously generate baryon asymmetry (via spontaneous baryogenesis) and serve as cold dark matter (via later oscillations), with a specific coupling constraint ξ ∼ (f/mₚ)² to prevent condensate fragmentation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the co-genesis of baryon asymmetry and dark matter within a single coherent mechanism tied to post-inflation dynamics, avoiding fine-tuned initial conditions or additional sources of CP violation.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis of axion dynamics in non-oscillating (runaway-potential) inflation models; derivation of rotational behavior induced by vacuum manifold orientation flip; assessment of condensate stability under non-minimal gravitational coupling ξ; application of spontaneous baryogenesis formalism to compute baryon number production.

Result: A viable parameter space exists where the axion’s rotation generates sufficient baryon asymmetry (η_B ∼ 10⁻¹⁰) and its subsequent oscillations yield correct relic abundance for dark matter, provided ξ ≈ (f/m_P)² and f lies near the GUT scale (~10¹⁶ GeV). Fragmentation is suppressed only under this precise scaling.

Conclusion: The model successfully unifies baryogenesis and dark matter genesis via a single axion-like field, linking observable cosmological densities to fundamental symmetry properties (shift symmetry, gravity coupling) and inflationary geometry—offering a testable alternative to conventional QCD axion or leptogenesis scenarios.

Abstract: It is shown that the co-genesis of baryon asymmetry and dark matter can be achieved through the rotation of a spectator axion-like particle, because of a flip in the vacuum manifold's orientation at the end of inflation. This can occur if the axion has a periodic non-minimal coupling to gravity (while preserving the discrete shift symmetry) in non-oscillating inflation models, where the inflaton field is characterised by a runaway potential. Our rotating axion can generate the baryon asymmetry of the Universe through spontaneous baryogenesis, while at a later epoch it can oscillate as dark matter. We show that in order to avoid fragmentation of the axion condensate during the rotation, we require the non-minimal coupling $ξ\sim(f/m_P)^2$, where $f$ is the axion decay constant.

</details>


### [74] [Double strange hybrid baryon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04237)
*B. Barsbay,K. Azizi,H. Sundu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the mass and pole residue of the double strange hybrid baryon (ssqg) using QCD sum rules with gluonic interpolating currents and up to dimension-10 condensates, yielding ground-state mass 1593.44±130.29 MeV and first-excited-state mass 1897.47±124.44 MeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretical predictions for the spectroscopic properties of the double strange hybrid baryon (ssqg), a poorly explored class of hybrid hadrons with explicit gluonic degrees of freedom, supporting future experimental identification and nonperturbative QCD studies.
