Method: QCD sum rule analysis employing an interpolating current with explicit gluon field; two-point correlation function evaluated using two independent Lorentz structures (slashed-q and identity); operator product expansion including vacuum condensates up to dimension ten; mass and residue extracted by averaging results from both structures.

Result: Ground state: mass = 1593.44 ± 130.29 MeV, residue = (2.57 ± 0.40) × 10⁻³ GeV⁵; first excited state: mass = 1897.47 ± 124.44 MeV, residue = (2.88 ± 0.62) × 10⁻³ GeV⁵.

Conclusion: The study delivers quantitative QCD-based predictions for the double strange hybrid baryon spectrum, establishing benchmarks for experimental searches and advancing understanding of gluon-mediated baryonic structure.

Abstract: We investigate the spectroscopic properties of the double strange hybrid baryon with quark content $ssqg$ within the framework of QCD sum rule. Using an interpolating current with explicit gluonic degrees of freedom, the two-point correlation function is analyzed in terms of two independent Lorentz structures, $\slashed{q}$ and $I$. The operator product expansion is carried out by including vacuum condensates up to dimension ten, and the corresponding sum rules are derived for both structures. By taking the average of the results obtained from the two Lorentz structures, we extract the masses and pole residues of the ground and first excited states. For the ground state, we obtain a mass of $\widetilde{M } = (1593.44\pm 130.29)~ \mathrm{MeV}$ and a residue of $\widetilde{λ} = (2.57\pm 0.40) \times10^{-3} ~\mathrm{GeV}^5$. For the first excited state, the corresponding values are $M = (1897.47\pm 124.44)~ \mathrm{MeV}$ and $λ= (2.88\pm 0.62) \times10^{-3} ~\mathrm{GeV}^5$. The obtained results provide theoretical predictions for the double strange hybrid baryon spectrum and may be useful for future experimental searches as well as further nonperturbative studies of hybrid hadrons.

</details>


### [75] [George, I and the curvaton](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03101)
*Konstantinos Dimopoulos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This is a personal retrospective on collaborative research with George Lazarides, focusing on the development and application of the curvaton hypothesis in early-universe cosmology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document and reflect on joint scientific contributions to the curvaton hypothesis—a mechanism for generating cosmic density perturbations distinct from standard inflationary quantum fluctuations.

Method: Theoretical cosmological modeling, including perturbation theory, scalar field dynamics, and statistical analysis of primordial curvature perturbations.

Result: Advancement and refinement of the curvaton scenario, enabling its use as a viable alternative or complement to inflation for explaining CMB anisotropies and large-scale structure.

Conclusion: The collaboration significantly contributed to establishing the curvaton as a robust framework within early-universe physics, influencing subsequent theoretical and observational work.

Abstract: George Lazarides was a pivotal collaborator and friend to me. We worked together on several projects, developing and exploiting the curvaton hypothesis, which was new at the time. This is a brief overview of our joint research.

</details>


### [76] [Charting doubly strange hidden-charm pentaquarks: An electromagnetic mapping of spin-$\frac{1}{2}$ and $\frac{3}{2}$ states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04355)
*Ulaş Özdem*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes electromagnetic multipole moments (magnetic dipole, electric quadrupole, magnetic octupole) of doubly strange hidden-charm pentaquarks ($S = -2$) using QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSR), employing multiple diquark-diquark-antiquark interpolating currents to assess model dependence; results show significant current-dependent spread in magnetic dipole moments, charm dominance in most configurations, axial-vector diquark-driven isospin sensitivity, and provide benchmarks for experiment and lattice QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To perform the first systematic LCSR investigation of electromagnetic multipole structure in the doubly strange ($S = -2$) hidden-charm pentaquark sector, and to quantify theoretical uncertainty arising from interpolating current choice—particularly probing internal color-spin correlations via independent diquark-diquark-antiquark currents.

Method: QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSR); use of multiple independent interpolating currents in diquark-diquark-antiquark form; computation of magnetic dipole ($\mu$), electric quadrupole ($Q$), and magnetic octupole ($\mathcal{O}$) moments for $J^P = 1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ states; quark-level decomposition and isospin sensitivity analysis via axial-vector diquark structure.

Result: Magnetic dipole moments range from $-2.15\,\mu_N$ to $5.74\,\mu_N$ ($J=1/2$) and $-4.25\,\mu_N$ to $-0.43\,\mu_N$ ($J=3/2$); electric quadrupole moments: $-2.01\times10^{-2}$–$5.55\times10^{-2}\,\text{fm}^2$; magnetic octupole moments ~one order of magnitude smaller; charm quark dominates most configurations; strange quarks decisive only in axial-vector diquark currents; exact isospin ratio $\mu_u/\mu_d = -2.00$ confirmed.

Conclusion: Electromagnetic multipole moments of $S = -2$ hidden-charm pentaquarks are highly sensitive to interpolating current structure—especially axial-vector diquark content—making them powerful probes of internal wave function and color-spin correlations; the observed current dependence quantifies key theoretical uncertainty, and predictions serve as benchmarks for experimental searches and lattice QCD validation.

Abstract: We calculate the magnetic dipole moments of doubly strange hidden-charm pentaquark states with spin-parity $J^P = 1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ using QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSR), presenting the first systematic LCSR investigation of the electromagnetic multipole structure in the $S = -2$ sector. To assess the model dependence, we employ independent interpolating currents in diquark-diquark-antiquark form, which probe different assumptions about the internal color-spin correlations. For the spin-$3/2$ states, we also compute the electric quadrupole and magnetic octupole moments. The magnetic dipole moments exhibit a considerable spread across currents, ranging from $-2.15\,μ_N$ to $5.74\,μ_N$ for spin-$1/2$ pentaquarks and from $-4.25\,μ_N$ to $-0.43\,μ_N$ for spin-$3/2$ states, reflecting the sensitivity of magnetic moments to the internal wave function. A quark-level decomposition reveals that the charm quark dominates in most configurations, while strange quarks play a decisive role only in currents favoring axial-vector diquark structures. The electric quadrupole moments lie between $-2.01\times10^{-2}$ fm$^2$ and $5.55\times10^{-2}$ fm$^2$, and the magnetic octupole moments are typically an order of magnitude smaller. The current dependence of the magnetic dipole moments provides a quantitative measure of the theoretical uncertainty arising from the choice of interpolating operator. The pronounced isospin sensitivity of $J^3_μ(x)$ across all three multipole moments arises from its axial-vector diquark structure, which isolates the light quark from spin averaging and allows the charge asymmetry $e_u/e_d = -2$ to propagate directly into the electromagnetic moments; the ratio $μ_u/μ_d = -2.00$ confirms this mechanism exactly. Our predictions offer benchmarks for future experiments and lattice QCD calculations, and may help discriminate among competing structural models.

</details>


### [77] [Polarized Nucleon as a Topological Dipole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03123)
*Kenji Fukushima,Tomoya Uji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper predicts a topological charge dipole in polarized nucleons, rooted in the pseudoscalar nature of topological charge density and fixed by the flavor-singlet axial charge in the chiral limit; it demonstrates the effect in a chiral soliton model with vector mesons and the $U(1)_A$ anomaly, and proposes experimental signatures in $η/η′$ production and pseudoscalar asymmetries correlated with magnetic fields or vorticity in heavy-ion collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a fundamental connection between nucleon spin polarization and topological structure of QCD vacuum, motivated by the pseudoscalar nature of topological charge density and its role in the $U(1)_A$ anomaly and chiral symmetry breaking.

Method: Analytical derivation from the definition of the topological form factor and symmetry constraints; concrete realization in a two-flavor chiral soliton model incorporating vector mesons and the $U(1)_A$ anomaly, where soliton rotation generates a singlet pseudoscalar profile.

Result: A robust prediction of a topological charge dipole aligned with nucleon spin; its magnitude is quantitatively tied to the flavor-singlet axial charge $a_0$ in the chiral limit; explicit model calculation confirms the dipole pattern and links it to rotational excitation.

Conclusion: Polarized nucleons intrinsically host a topological dipole — a novel manifestation of QCD topology tied to spin — providing a bridge between nucleon structure, axial anomalies, and potential experimental observables in hadronic and heavy-ion physics.

Abstract: We show that a polarized nucleon generically carries a dipole distribution of topological charge density. This topological dipole follows robustly from the definition of the topological form factor and the pseudoscalar nature of the topological charge density. The strength of the topological dipole is fixed, in the chiral limit, by the flavor-singlet axial charge. We demonstrate the mechanism in a two-flavor chiral soliton model with vector mesons and the $U(1)_A$ anomaly, where the rotation of the soliton induces a singlet pseudoscalar profile and realizes the predicted dipole pattern. We also discuss possible experimental probes through exclusive $η$, $η^\prime$ production and directed-flow-like pseudoscalar-meson asymmetries correlated with magnetic fields or vorticity in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [78] [A Unified Dark Matter Explanation for $\boldsymbol{B^+ \!\to K^+ν\barν}$ and the Super-Kamiokande Antineutrino Excess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03206)
*Shu-Yu Ho,Jongkuk Kim,Pyungwon Ko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a UV-complete complex scalar dark matter model with gauged U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} symmetry to jointly explain the ~20 MeV anti-neutrino excess at Super-Kamiokande and the 2.7σ B⁺→K⁺νν excess at Belle II, while reproducing the correct dark matter relic density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile two recent experimental anomalies—Super-Kamiokande's ν̄_e-like excess near 20 MeV and Belle II's B⁺→K⁺νν branching ratio exceeding SM prediction—with dark matter phenomenology in a theoretically consistent (UV-complete) framework.

Method: Constructs and analyzes a minimal model featuring a complex scalar dark matter particle charged under a gauged U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} symmetry; computes relic abundance via freeze-out including L_μ−L_τ gauge boson exchange; evaluates contributions to ν̄_e production in Super-K and B⁺→K⁺νν decay mediated by the new gauge boson and dark sector dynamics.

Result: Identifies viable parameter space where the light dark sector (MeV–GeV scale) simultaneously fits both anomalies and yields Ωh² ≈ 0.12; predicts correlated signals such as invisible B decays and modified neutrino scattering.

Conclusion: The gauged U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} complex scalar DM model provides a unified, UV-complete explanation of the two anomalies without conflicting with existing constraints (e.g., neutrino trident, beam dump, cosmological bounds), suggesting a coherent new physics scenario linking flavor, neutrino, and dark sectors.

Abstract: Recent results from Super-Kamiokande and Belle II have revealed intriguing excesses over Standard Model expectations. Super-Kamiokande observes a mild excess of $\barν_e^{}$-like events near $20\,\,\mathrm{MeV}$, while Belle II reports a branching fraction for $B^+ \!\to K^+ν\barν$ that exceeds the Standard Model prediction by approximately $2.7σ$. In this work, we study the simplest UV-complete complex scalar dark matter model with a gauged $\text{U}(1)_{\textsf{L}_μ- \textsf{L}_τ}^{}$ symmetry. We demonstrate that a light dark sector can simultaneously reproduce the observed dark matter relic density and accommodate both excesses within a unified framework.

</details>


### [79] [Probing a 146 GeV cLFV scalar using the LHC and low-energy experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03249)
*Christina Gao,Lingfeng Li,Z. J. Xiong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: CMS observed a 146 GeV excess in H→eμ; this paper tests its consistency with low-energy cLFV constraints using a 7-parameter real scalar EFT, finding tension with current μ-e conversion limits but strong testability by upcoming experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the CMS 146 GeV eμ excess can be interpreted as a new real scalar mediating charged lepton flavor violation (cLFV), and to determine its compatibility with stringent low-energy cLFV observables.

Method: Bottom-up effective field theory with a 146 GeV real scalar coupled to gluons and all charged-lepton bilinears; Bayesian MCMC analysis against multiple cLFV constraints: μ-e conversion, muonium-antimuonium oscillation, ℓi→3ℓj, ℓi→ℓjγ, semileptonic τ LFV, and LHC dilepton searches.

Result: A preferred parameter region emerges with Yukawa coupling Y_eμ ≈ 10^(−4.09), already excluded at >90% CL by current μ-e conversion limits (e.g., SINDRUM II); projected sensitivities of Mu2e, COMET, MEG II, Belle II, HL-LHC etc. will decisively test this scenario within ~10 years.

Conclusion: The scalar explanation of the CMS excess is strongly disfavored by existing μ-e conversion data, but remains falsifiable — upcoming high- and low-energy cLFV experiments will provide definitive confirmation or exclusion.

Abstract: The CMS Collaboration reported a local excess at $146~\mathrm{GeV}$ in the search for the lepton-flavor-violating decay of the Higgs boson and additional Higgs bosons in the $eμ$ final state at $\sqrt{s}= 13~\mathrm{TeV}$. If confirmed, this would constitute a major piece of evidence of charged lepton flavor violation (cLFV). We investigate the compatibility of the claimed signal with the full suite of existing low-energy cLFV constraints in a bottom-up effective description: a single real scalar of mass $146~\mathrm{GeV}$ coupled to gluons and to all charged-lepton bilinears, with seven free parameters that simultaneously control the LHC production cross section, every di-lepton decay channel, and every low-energy cLFV observable. A Bayesian MCMC analysis against $μ-e$ conversion, muonium-antimuonium oscillation, three-lepton and radiative LFV decays, semileptonic $τ$ LFV decays, and LHC di-lepton searches yields a preferred mode with peaked value $Y_{eμ} \sim 10^{-4.09}$, already cut into by the current $μ-e$ conversion limits. The projected sensitivities of Mu2e, COMET, Mu3e, MACE, MEG~II, Belle~II, STCF, and the HL-LHC directly probe the region of coupling space selected by the CMS excess, so the complementarity between high-energy and low-energy cLFV probes will either corroborate or decisively exclude the scalar interpretation of the anomaly within the next decade.

</details>


### [80] [First look at the evaluation of two-loop Feynman integrals for radiative return processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03343)
*Mattia Pozzoli,William J. Torres Bobadilla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes planar two-loop four-point Feynman integrals for initial-state radiation in low-energy e⁺e⁻ collisions, enabling full-mass-dependent NNLO QED predictions for radiative return processes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precision studies of radiative return at low-energy e⁺e⁻ colliders require NNLO QED predictions with full electron mass dependence; existing approximations neglecting the electron mass are insufficient for current experimental accuracy.

Method: Constructs dimensional-regulator-polynomial differential equations for planar two-loop four-point integrals involving nested square roots and elliptic geometries, then solves them numerically with stability across the physical kinematic region.

Result: Stable, high-precision numerical evaluation of the relevant two-loop integrals across the full physical region, including regimes with large scale hierarchies.

Conclusion: The computed integrals serve as essential building blocks for complete NNLO QED predictions of radiative return processes, enabling sub-percent theoretical precision in low-energy e⁺e⁻ collider observables.

Abstract: Precision studies of radiative return processes at low-energy electron--positron colliders require next-to-next-to-leading order QED predictions retaining full dependence on the electron mass. We present the calculation of planar two-loop four-point Feynman integrals relevant for initial-state radiation contributions to these processes. The calculation presents considerable analytical complexity, due to the presence of a nested square root and of integrals associated with elliptic geometries. We construct differential equations for the Feynman integrals which are polynomial in the dimensional regulator, and are suitable for numerical integration. We demonstrate stable numerical evaluations throughout the physical region relevant for low-energy experiments, despite the presence of large hierarchies of scales. Our results provide essential building blocks for NNLO predictions for radiative return processes.

</details>


### [81] [Analytic result of a three-loop integral family in the Higgs decay to four massive bottom quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03398)
*Jian Wang,Xing Wang,Yefan Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs and solves canonical differential equations for a three-loop integral family arising in H→bbbb decay via Hgg effective interaction, featuring mixed sectors involving multiple polylogarithms, an elliptic curve, and a K3 surface; analytical results for all master integrals up to O(ε²) are provided.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the decay width of H→bbbb induced by the effective Higgs-gluon-gluon interaction using the optical theorem, which requires evaluating a challenging three-loop integral family with mixed transcendental structures (polylogarithmic, elliptic, and K3).

Method: Applies a recently proposed algorithm to construct and solve a canonical differential equation system for the full integral family, explicitly handling sector mixing between multiple polylogarithms, an elliptic curve, and a K3 surface.

Result: Analytical expressions for all master integrals in the family are obtained up to the first two orders (O(1) and O(ε)) in dimensional regularization parameter ε.

Conclusion: The canonical differential equation approach successfully unifies treatment of disparate transcendental sectors in a single integral family, enabling full analytical evaluation and demonstrating feasibility of systematic higher-transcendental integration in multi-loop QCD calculations.

Abstract: When calculating the decay width of $H\to b\bar{b}b\bar{b}$ induced by the effective Higgs-gluon-gluon interaction using the optical theorem, the three-loop Feynman diagrams are encountered. Among them, there is an interesting integral family, which involves not only the sectors evaluated to multiple polylogarithms but also those related to an elliptic curve and a K3 surface. We demonstrate how to construct and solve the canonical differential equation for the entire family, which includes mixing between different sectors, by using the recently proposed algorithm in~\cite{e-collaboration:2025frv, Bree:2025tug}. Analytical results for all master integrals in this family up to the first two orders of $\varepsilon$ are given.

</details>


### [82] [A Dispersive Look at Rare $B$-meson Semileptonic Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03531)
*Marco Ciuchini,Marco Fedele,Ayan Paul,Josua Scholze,Luca Silvestrini,Silvano Simula,Mauro Valli,Ludovico Vittorio*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a global analysis of rare $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$ decays, improving form factor determinations via the Dispersive Matrix method—comparing lattice QCD-only vs. lattice + light-cone sum rule inputs—and finds stronger evidence for long-distance hadronic effects over New Physics in $C_9$; it also provides updated SM predictions and NP constraints for $b \to s \nu\bar\nu$ transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Interpretation of rare $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$ transitions is limited by hadronic uncertainties—especially $B \to K^{(*)}$, $B_s \to \phi$ form factors and four-quark operator matrix elements—necessitating robust, model-independent FF treatments to disentangle potential New Physics from hadronic effects.

Method: Global Bayesian fit to $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$ observables (including LHCb/CMS angular analyses sensitive to strong phases); form factors determined via the Dispersive Matrix method, comparing two FF setups: (i) lattice QCD only, (ii) lattice QCD + light-cone sum rules at low $q^2$; $b \to s \nu\bar\nu$ predictions computed using local FFs; NP analysis performed within the Weak Effective Theory.

Result: Using only lattice QCD enlarges FF uncertainties at large recoil; combined with angular data, the fit favors long-distance hadronic effects over a short-distance $C_9$ shift; new SM predictions for $B \to K^{(*)}\nu\bar\nu$ are provided; NP constraints from current bounds and Belle II measurement are quantified.

Conclusion: Hadronic effects—not New Physics in $C_9$—are increasingly favored as the dominant explanation for observed anomalies in $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$; precise, multi-source FF inputs are essential; $b \to s \nu\bar\nu$ modes remain theoretically clean probes with growing experimental relevance.

Abstract: Rare semileptonic $b \to s$ flavour-changing neutral current transitions provide stringent tests of the Standard Model. Their interpretation is limited by hadronic uncertainties, notably the $B \to K^{(*)}$ and $B_s \to φ$ form factors (FFs) and the matrix elements of four-quark operators. We perform a global analysis of $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$ transitions taking these uncertainties fully into account, determining the FFs through the Dispersive Matrix method and comparing a setup based solely on lattice QCD (LQCD) with one that also includes light-cone sum-rule (LCSR) inputs at low $q^2$. Compared to the case where both input are taken into account, using only LQCD substantially enlarges the FF uncertainties at large recoil. Combined with the latest LHCb and CMS angular measurements sensitive to strong phases, our global fit yields strengthened evidence in favour of long-distance hadronic effects rather than a short-distance shift in $C_9$. We further present new SM predictions for the theoretically clean $b \to s ν\barν$ modes, which depend only on local FFs, and a New Physics analysis of these transitions in the Weak Effective Theory, discussing their impact on the interpretation of the recent Belle~II measurement and on the available experimental upper bounds.

</details>


### [83] [Lagrangian-based model of the $e^+e^- \to V \bar V$ process applied to the $e^+e^- \to D^{\ast+} D^{\ast-}$ data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04210)
*Peter Lichard*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Derives a Breit-Wigner-type formula for e+e− → VV̄ using QFT and validates it against BESIII D*+D*− data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a theoretically grounded, observable-parameterized resonance description for e+e− annihilation into vector meson–antimeson pairs.

Method: Quantum field theory derivation of a Breit-Wigner-type cross-section formula for e+e− → VV̄; parameter mapping to physical observables; χ² fitting to BESIII 2022 D*+D*− production data.

Result: A consistent, QFT-based Breit-Wigner formula is obtained with parameters directly tied to measurable quantities (e.g., mass, width, coupling); successful fit to D*+D*− data confirms validity.

Conclusion: The derived formula provides a robust, first-principles framework for analyzing vector meson pair production near threshold, enabling precise extraction of resonance properties from e+e− collider data.

Abstract: Using the quantum field theory, we derive a Breit-Wigner-type formula for the $e^+ e^-$ annihilation into a vector meson and its antiparticle, and relate the formula parameters to observable quantities. The formula's soundness is checked by fitting the $e^+ e^- \to D^{\ast+}D^{\ast-}$ data published by the BESIII Collaboration in 2022.

</details>


### [84] [Gravitational Wave Signatures of Cosmological Stasis: A Unified Spectral Template](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03537)
*Gabriela Barenboim,Anne-Katherine Burns*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a universal spectral template for gravitational waves from stasis epochs in the early universe, parameterized solely by equation of state $w_s$ and duration $\Delta N_\mathrm{stasis}$; it yields two observables—spectral tilt $\alpha(w_s)$ and amplitude step $C^2(w_s)$—that obey a falsifiable one-parameter consistency curve $C^2 = C^2(\alpha)$, testable with BBO/DECIGO at unprecedented precision ($\sigma_\perp \sim 1.5\times10^{-5}$ for $r=0.01$).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a model-independent, microphysics-agnostic characterization of the gravitational wave signature of stasis—a constant-$w$ dynamical fixed point in the early universe—enabling robust observational tests without prior knowledge of the underlying particle physics.

Method: Analytical derivation of the tensor power spectrum generated during a stasis epoch, leading to a closed-form spectral template; identification of two invariant observables ($\alpha$, $C^2$) and their functional relation eliminating $w_s$; Fisher matrix forecasting for BBO and DECIGO to quantify detectability of deviations from the consistency curve.

Result: A universal, two-parameter ($w_s$, $\Delta N_\mathrm{stasis}$) spectral template for stasis-induced gravitational waves; derivation of the exact consistency relation $C^2 = C^2(\alpha)$; forecasted sensitivity $\sigma_\perp \simeq 1.5\times10^{-5}$ for detecting off-curve deviations at $r = 0.01$, four orders of magnitude below the dynamic range of $C^2$.

Conclusion: The stasis gravitational wave spectrum is falsifiable as a class: any measured $(\alpha, C^2)$ pair must lie on the predicted consistency curve if the cosmology contains a constant-$w$ era; deviations would rule out all constant-$w$ stasis models, making this a powerful, theory-agnostic probe of early-universe dynamics.

Abstract: Proposed in 2022 by Dienes et al., stasis is a dynamical fixed point in the early universe in which the equation of state, $w_s$, is fixed at a constant value. In this study we show that the inflationary gravitational wave imprint of any stasis epoch is captured by a closed-form spectral template controlled by two physical inputs, the equation of state $w_s$ and the stasis duration $ΔN_\mathrm{stasis}$, that applies uniformly across every microphysical realization. The template presented here yields two independently measurable observables, the spectral tilt of the spectra in the stasis band, $α(w_s)$ and the amplitude step $C^2(w_s)$ at the beginning and end of the stasis band. Eliminating $w_s$ gives a one-parameter consistency curve $C^2 = C^2(α)$ on which the data must lie if the underlying cosmology is any constant-$w$ era. This makes the spectrum falsifiable without knowing $w_s$ in advance: a measured $(α, C^2)$ pair either lands on the curve or rules out the constant-$w$ class. We show that BBO and DECIGO can resolve the perpendicular displacement from the consistency curve to $σ_\perp \simeq 1.5\times10^{-5}$ at a tensor-to-scalar ratio, $r = 0.01$, four orders of magnitude below the curve's range in $C^2$ meaning that any off-curve deviation is detectable across a broad range of allowed $r$ values.

</details>


### [85] [Spontaneous CP breaking in $S_3$ flavored Higgs sector with soft-breaking terms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03547)
*E. Barradas-Guevara,O. Félix-Beltrán,A. Pérez Martínez,E. Rodríguez-Jáuregui,J. Montaño-Peraza*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the Higgs sector of an $S_3$-invariant extension of the Standard Model with three SU(2) Higgs doublets, exploring spontaneous and explicit CP violation, Higgs mass spectra, and trilinear self-couplings; it identifies a viable SM-like Higgs scenario under spontaneous CP violation from a singlet Higgs doublet $\Phi_S$, and examines soft $S_3$ breaking effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore CP violation—both spontaneous and explicit—in an $S_3$-symmetric extension of the Standard Model with multiple Higgs doublets, and to assess its implications for Higgs phenomenology (masses, self-couplings, and SM-like behavior).

Method: Theoretical analysis of the scalar potential and vacuum structure of a minimal $S_3$-invariant model with three SU(2) Higgs doublets; computation of physical Higgs masses and trilinear couplings after electroweak symmetry breaking; numerical scanning of parameter space including soft $S_3$-breaking CP-violating terms.

Result: A physically consistent solution is found where spontaneous CP violation originates from the $S_3$-singlet Higgs doublet $\Phi_S$; soft $S_3$ breaking introduces rich phenomenology, allowing one Higgs boson to closely resemble the SM Higgs in mass and couplings.

Conclusion: The $S_3$-invariant three-doublet model supports viable spontaneous CP violation and accommodates a SM-like Higgs boson, making it a phenomenologically promising framework beyond the Standard Model.

Abstract: We analyze the Higgs sector of the minimal $S_3$-invariant extension of the Standard Model including spontaneous and explicit CP violation arising from the spontaneous electroweak symmetry breaking. This extended Higgs sector includes three SU(2) Higgs doublets with and without complex vev's, which ones providing an interesting scenario to analyze the Higgs masses spectrum, trilinear Higgs self-couplings and CP violation. We present how the spontaneous electroweak symmetry breaking coming from three SU(2) Higgs doublets gives an interesting scenario when spontaneous CP violation arises from the Higgs doublet $Φ_S$, singlet under $S_3$. Furthermore, a numerical analysis of the Higgs masses and trilinear Higgs self-couplings is presented. Particularly, we find a physical solution for the scenario in which spontaneous CP break is provided by $Φ_S$. The analysis of soft breaking to $S_3$ symmetry including CP breaking terms opens up a rich parameter space, with Higgs masses and trilinear Higgs self-couplings favoring one of the Higgs bosons as a SM-like type Higgs boson.

</details>


### [86] [Updated analysis of minimal supersymmetric SO(10) with a universal soft spectrum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04250)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang,Takeshi Fukuyama*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes minimal supersymmetric SO(10) grand unification with constrained soft-breaking parameters, identifying a narrow 'mini-split' SUSY region (scalars >7.7 TeV, tanβ <9) compatible with proton decay limits, Higgs mass, flavor/EW precision, and dark matter constraints—testable by Hyper-Kamiokande, electroweakino searches, and EDM experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile minimal supersymmetric SO(10) GUT with current experimental constraints—including proton decay, Higgs mass, flavor physics, muon g-2, EDMs, and dark matter—while maintaining gauge coupling unification and vacuum stability.

Method: Global parameter scan over universal soft-breaking terms (m0, m1/2, A0, tanβ), supplemented by constrained-μ cross-check enforcing radiative electroweak symmetry breaking from GUT-scale boundary conditions; Yukawa structure fixed via 10⊕126¯ Higgs sector; dimension-five proton decay dominates; all major phenomenological constraints consistently imposed.

Result: Only a narrow mini-split SUSY region survives: scalar masses m0 ≳ 7.7 TeV, tanβ ≲ 9, with multi-TeV gauginos; μ-parameter constrained at GUT scale; lightest supersymmetric particle (LSP) remains viable as thermal WIMP dark matter in both universal and non-universal (free-μ) Higgs-mass extensions, subject to direct detection bounds.

Conclusion: Minimal constrained SUSY SO(10) remains viable only in a sharply bounded, testable mini-split regime; upcoming experiments—Hyper-Kamiokande (proton decay), HL-LHC/FCC (electroweakinos), LZ/PandaX (DM), and ACME/nEDM (EDMs)—will decisively probe this scenario within the decade.

Abstract: We update the analysis of minimal supersymmetric SO(10) grand unification with a universal, constrained-MSSM-like soft-breaking spectrum. A pair of Yukawa matrices in the ${\bf 10} \oplus \overline{\bf 126}$ Higgs sector fixes the charged fermion masses and quark mixing, the baryon number-violating dimension-five operators that mediate proton decay, and the right-handed neutrino Majorana masses of the type-I seesaw. The simultaneously imposed constraints include: (i) gauge coupling unification and vacuum stability; (ii) dimension-five proton decay (the dimension-six gauge mode also computed and negligible), the 125-GeV Higgs mass, and the LEP chargino limit; and (iii) $μ\to eγ$, the muon $g-2$, and electric dipole moments. We perform a global scan over the soft-breaking parameters, complemented by a constrained-$μ$ cross-check that fixes the electroweakino sector from the GUT-scale boundary conditions through radiative electroweak symmetry breaking. Subject to a single effective colored-Higgs scale $M_{H_C}$ for the proton decay normalization and to a constrained-$μ$ treatment, what survives is a narrow mini-split region: heavy, multi-tens-of-TeV scalars with $m_0 \gtrsim 7.7$~TeV and $\tanβ\lesssim 9$. We also examine the fate of the lightest supersymmetric particle as a dark matter candidate, in both the universal model and its free-$μ$ non-universal-Higgs-mass extension. The surviving region is sharply bounded and testable in the coming decade, most directly by Hyper-Kamiokande proton decay and electroweakino searches, with dark matter direct detection and electric dipole moment experiments.

</details>


### [87] [Probing Fermion-Portal Scalar Dark Matter through Charged Vector-Like Fermions at Future Muon Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03775)
*Songshaptak De,Tapoja Jha,Najimuddin Khan,Madhurima Pandey*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies a fermion-portal scalar dark matter model with vector-like fermions and a Z₂ symmetry, showing that Yukawa-driven annihilation and co-annihilation can satisfy relic density and evading stringent direct-detection bounds; it further demonstrates strong discovery potential for the lightest charged vector-like fermion at 3–10 TeV muon colliders via μ⁺μ⁻ → E₁⁺E₁⁻ → e⁺e⁻ + E̸ₜ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The conventional Higgs-portal scalar DM is highly constrained by latest direct-detection experiments; thus, the authors explore fermion-mediated annihilation in a minimal Z₂-symmetric model with vector-like singlet/doublet fermions to reconcile relic abundance and detection limits.

Method: Combined theoretical constraints (vacuum stability, perturbative unitarity) and experimental bounds (Planck relic density, XENONnT/LZ direct detection, Higgs invisible width, LFV, (g−2)ₑ,μ); performed parameter-space scan; conducted detector-level simulation of μ⁺μ⁻ → E₁⁺E₁⁻ → e⁺e⁻ + E̸ₜ at 3 TeV and 10 TeV muon colliders including SM backgrounds.

Result: Viable parameter space exists where t-/u-channel annihilation and co-annihilation reproduce Ωh² ≈ 0.12 while satisfying all constraints; muon collider analysis shows sensitivity to E₁ masses up to ~4.5 TeV (at 3 TeV CM energy) and ~9 TeV (at 10 TeV CM energy), surpassing LHC reach.

Conclusion: Fermion-portal scalar DM remains phenomenologically viable and testable; multi-TeV muon colliders offer uniquely powerful probes for this scenario, especially for charged vector-like fermions inaccessible to current hadron colliders.

Abstract: We revisit a minimal fermion-portal scalar dark matter model consisting of a real singlet scalar dark matter candidate and additional vector-like singlet and doublet charged fermions stabilized by a discrete $Z_2$ symmetry. In light of the latest dark matter direct-detection constraints, the conventional Higgs-portal interaction is severely restricted, motivating a detailed investigation of fermion-mediated dark matter annihilation channels. We perform a comprehensive analysis of the model parameter space by incorporating theoretical constraints from vacuum stability and perturbative unitarity, together with experimental bounds from relic density measurements, direct-detection experiments, Higgs invisible decay searches, lepton-flavor-violating processes, and anomalous magnetic moments. We show that the observed dark matter relic abundance can be successfully reproduced over a wide mass range through Yukawa-driven $t$- and $u$-annihilation and co-annihilation processes involving the new fermions, while remaining consistent with current direct-detection limits. Motivated by the viable parameter space, we investigate the discovery prospects of the lightest charged vector-like fermion at future muon colliders operating at center-of-mass energies of 3 TeV and 10 TeV. Focusing on the process $μ^+μ^- \to E_1^+E_1^- \to e^+e^- + \cancel{E}_T$, we perform a detector-level analysis including realistic Standard Model backgrounds. We demonstrate that the clean experimental environment of a muon collider provides excellent sensitivity to charged fermion masses extending into the multi-TeV regime, significantly improving the exploration prospects of this class of fermion-portal dark matter scenarios.

</details>


### [88] [Anomalous triple gauge couplings in the light of dimension-8 operators in $W^+W^-$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04251)
*Daniel Gillies,Andrea Banfi,Adam Martin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares dimension-8 EFT effects in $W^+W^-$ production at the LHC via $q\bar q$ and $\gamma\gamma$ initial states, focusing on bosonic operators affecting anomalous triple and quadruple gauge couplings; it employs logarithmic resummation (via MCFM-RE and MadGraph) to handle jet-veto effects, finds the $\gamma\gamma$ channel dominates bosonic dimension-8 contributions, and derives constraints on higher-dimensional operators using current/future data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the relative importance of dimension-8 bosonic operators in $W^+W^-$ production, clarify the validity range of the Effective Field Theory (EFT) expansion, and improve sensitivity to anomalous gauge couplings by leveraging complementary $q\bar q$ and $\gamma\gamma$ channels under jet-veto conditions.

Method: Matching dimension-6 and dimension-8 bosonic operators to anomalous triple gauge couplings for $q\bar q$ channel; using MCFM-RE for resummation of large logarithms under jet veto; adapting MadGraph with factorisation scale set to jet-veto scale for $\gamma\gamma$ channel (which involves both triple and quadruple gauge couplings); neglecting fermionic dimension-8 operators; incorporating theoretical (scale, PDF) and experimental (statistical, systematic) uncertainties in constraint setting.

Result: The $\gamma\gamma$ channel exhibits a dominant bosonic dimension-8 contribution to $W^+W^-$ production when fermionic dimension-8 operators are neglected; this dominance makes it a key probe for testing EFT convergence and validity; quantitative constraints on dimension-8 operators are obtained from current LHC data and projected for future runs.

Conclusion: The $\gamma\gamma$-initiated channel provides superior sensitivity to bosonic dimension-8 operators in $W^+W^-$ production and serves as a critical diagnostic tool for EFT truncation validity; combined analysis of $q\bar q$ and $\gamma\gamma$ channels, with proper resummation and uncertainty treatment, yields robust limits on higher-dimensional SMEFT operators.

Abstract: We compare the size of dimension-8 effects on $W^+W^-$ production at the LHC arising from the $q\bar q$ and $γγ$ initial states. In particular, we consider bosonic operators, which contribute to the anomalous triple and quadruple gauge couplings. The relevant dimension-6 and dimension-8 operators are matched to the anomalous triple gauge couplings that contribute to the $q\bar q$ channel, allowing for the resummation of large logarithmic contributions arising in the presence of a jet-veto through the program MCFM-RE. For the $γγ$ channel, which receives contributions from both triple and quadruple gauge couplings, such resummation can be performed through the program MadGraph, by simply setting the factorisation scale to the jet-veto scale. We find that, when neglecting fermionic dimension-8 operators, the $γγ$ channel has a dominant bosonic dimension-8 contribution and this channel can be used to understand the range of validity of the Effective Field Theory. With this in mind, and carefully considering theoretical and experimental uncertainties, we provide constraints on higher dimensional operators using current and future data.

</details>


### [89] [Dark matter scattering with pre-supernova neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03943)
*Sangeetha N. Tallur,Nirmal Raj*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using pre-supernova neutrinos—emitted in the final day before core collapse—as a probe of dark matter–neutrino scattering at MeV energies, leveraging observed deficits in inverse beta decay events at large detectors (e.g., KamLAND, JUNO, Super-K) to set upper limits on the reduced scattering cross section.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an independent, low-energy probe of dark matter–neutrino interactions—complementing post-bounce supernova constraints—and to test cosmological hints (e.g., Lyman-alpha small-scale power suppression) potentially linked to such interactions.

Method: Analyzes attenuation of electron antineutrino flux due to scattering off dark matter en route to Earth; infers expected deficit in inverse beta decay signals at low-threshold, high-statistics detectors; derives model-independent upper limits on the dark matter–neutrino reduced scattering cross section.

Result: Derives competitive, complementary upper limits on the dark matter–neutrino reduced scattering cross section at MeV energies; demonstrates sensitivity to energy dependence and capability to distinguish certain interaction models.

Conclusion: Pre-supernova neutrinos offer a unique, astrophysically grounded probe of MeV-scale dark matter–neutrino scattering—providing both a cross-check of supernova bounds and potential insight into cosmological anomalies.

Abstract: Pre-supernova neutrinos, emitted during the last day prior to core collapse of a massive star, could provide a unique probe at MeV energies of scattering interactions between dark matter and neutrinos. Due to attenuation of the flux of electron anti-neutrinos from their scattering on dark matter on the way to Earth, we expect a deficit of inverse beta decay events at large-volume, low-threshold detectors such as KamLAND, JUNO, and Super-K. which we use to derive upper limits on the dark matter-neutrino reduced scattering cross section. Though seemingly weaker than limits from post-bounce supernova neutrino events, these sensitivities provide an important cross-check, could help determine the energy dependence of the cross section, and may even be a distinct probe of certain models. Further, pre-supernova neutrinos may test hints reported of dark matter-neutrino scattering in the early universe suppressing small-scale power as seen in Lyman-alpha data.

</details>


### [90] [Quantum interference effects enhanced in $π^+p$ femtoscopic correlation functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04351)
*Zhi-Wei Liu,Jun-Xu Lu,Duo-Lun Ge,Li-Sheng Geng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper resolves the peak-shift puzzle in π⁺p femtoscopic correlations by disentangling scattering and Δ(1232)⁺⁺-decay contributions using Koonin-Pratt formalism with data-driven partial-wave amplitudes; it identifies quantum interference between incident and scattered waves as the origin of the 140 MeV/c peak—a previously unrecognized mechanism in femtoscopy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing 'peak-shift puzzle' in π⁺p femtoscopic correlation functions observed in high-multiplicity pp collisions, where the correlation peak position deviates from expectations based on known hadronic interactions.

Method: Applies the Koonin-Pratt inversion formula with a Gaussian emission source and incorporates data-driven πN partial-wave scattering amplitudes; explicitly models both π⁺p elastic scattering and feed-down from Δ(1232)⁺⁺ → π⁺p decay; fits ALICE correlation data across transverse mass (m_T) bins.

Result: Successfully reproduces ALICE π⁺p correlation functions and their m_T dependence; identifies two distinct peaks at k ≈ 140 MeV/c (scattering) and k ≈ 220 MeV/c (decay); attributes the 140 MeV/c peak to quantum interference between incident and scattered waves—a novel insight in femtoscopy.

Conclusion: The π⁺p correlation peak is a weighted superposition of scattering and resonance-decay contributions, with m_T-dependent weights; the scattering peak arises from quantum interference—not final-state interaction alone—offering a new paradigm for interpreting femtoscopic correlations involving resonances and non-identical particles.

Abstract: We present a comprehensive analysis of the $π^+p$ femtoscopic correlation functions measured by the ALICE Collaboration in high-multiplicity $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV. Using the Koonin-Pratt formula with a Gaussian source and data-driven $πN$ partial-wave amplitudes, we account for the contributions from $π^+p$ scattering and $Δ(1232)^{++}$-decay, thereby successfully reproducing the measured data and their transverse-mass ($m_T$) dependence. The scattering contribution yields a peak near the relative momentum $k\approx140$ MeV/$c$, whereas the decay contribution peaks around $k\approx220$ MeV/$c$. The observed correlation peak results from a weighted sum of the two contributions, with $m_T$-dependent relative weights. We find that the 140 MeV/$c$ peak originates from quantum interference between the incident and scattered waves-a mechanism previously unnoticed in femtoscopic studies. This finding resolves the peak-shift puzzle in $π^+p$ correlations and provides a novel perspective for quantum interference effects in femtoscopy.

</details>


### [91] [Phenomenological extraction of fragmentation functions in a $p\bar{p}$ environment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04059)
*M. A. Pérez de León,S. A. Ochoa-Oregón,D. F. Rentería-Estrada,R. J. Hernández-Pinto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates kinematical cuts in pion and kaon production in proton-antiproton collisions to isolate and separate fragmentation functions (FFs) by flavor, extending a method previously used in proton-proton collisions; it includes NLO QCD and LO QED corrections for photon-hadron production to reconstruct partonic momentum fractions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate determination of hadronic fragmentation functions (FFs) requires precise modeling of differential cross sections from both experiment and higher-order QCD theory; current phenomenological FF extractions lack sufficient flavor separation, especially in p-\bar{p} environments.

Method: Systematic analysis of kinematical cuts applied to pion and kaon production in proton-antiproton collisions; employs NLO QCD + LO QED calculations for photon-hadron final states to constrain partonic momentum fractions and disentangle individual FF contributions.

Result: Demonstrates feasibility of flavor-separated FF extraction in p-\bar{p} collisions using optimized kinematical cuts and combined photon-hadron observables; provides quantitative assessment of sensitivity to u-, d-, s-, and gluon-initiated FFs.

Conclusion: Kinematical cut strategies—validated in pp collisions—can be successfully adapted to p\bar{p} colliders for improved FF flavor separation; photon-hadron channels with mixed QCD–QED corrections offer enhanced discriminatory power for FF constraints.

Abstract: The precise determination of fragmentation functions (FFs) of hadrons relies on the accurate description of the differential cross sections obtained from both experimental high-energy hadron colliders and theoretical predictions at higher orders in quantum chromodynamics. Various phenomenological strategies have been employed to extract FFs. In this work, we analyze the use of kinematical cuts for reactions including pions and kaons in proton-antiproton collisions to isolate individual FF contributions. This study examines the feasibility of using a similar approach as in proton-proton colliders to analyze FF flavour separation~\cite{Ochoa-Oregon:2023ktx}. In particular, we study photon-hadron production at colliders, including NLO QCD and LO QED corrections to reconstruct the partonic momentum fractions.

</details>


### [92] [Exclusive production of P-wave charmonia through two virtual photons in electron-positron annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04126)
*Avinash Okram,Shashank Bhatnagar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates relativistic cross sections for double-charmonium production (χc1+ηc and hc+hc) via two-photon fusion at √s = 10.6 GeV using the 4×4 Bethe–Salpeter equation; predicts ~10⁻³ fb cross sections governed by meson wave-function overlaps, confirms qualitative channel hierarchy consistent with NRQCD, and provides benchmarks for future e⁺e⁻ collider experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a clean probe of relativistic quarkonium dynamics via exclusive double-charmonium processes forbidden in single-photon annihilation, i.e., e⁺e⁻ → γ*γ* → χc1+ηc and hc+hc.

Method: Relativistic calculation within the 4×4 Bethe–Salpeter equation framework; parameters fixed from prior mass spectroscopy; leading-order predictions for total cross sections and energy dependence.

Result: Predicted cross sections ~10⁻³ fb; smooth power-law falloff; hierarchy determined by radial wave-function overlaps; qualitative channel ordering matches NRQCD despite quantitative differences.

Conclusion: The results serve as benchmark predictions for future high-luminosity e⁺e⁻ collider searches and validate the role of relativistic effects and wave-function structure in double-charmonium production.

Abstract: We present a relativistic study of the exclusive double-charmonium processes $e^-e^+ \to γ^*γ^* \to χ_{c1}+η_c$ and $e^-e^+ \to γ^*γ^* \to h_c+h_c$ at $\sqrt{s}=10.6$ GeV in the framework of the $4\times4$ Bethe-Salpeter equation. Since these channels are forbidden in single-photon annihilation, they proceed purely through two-photon quark-rearrangement mechanisms and thus provide a clean probe of relativistic quarkonium dynamics. Using parameters fixed from mass spectroscopy in our earlier work, we obtain leading-order predictions for the total cross sections and their energy dependence. The predicted cross sections are of order $10^{-3}$ fb and exhibit a hierarchy that originates from the radial structure of the meson wave functions, which determines the overlap integrals contributing to the production amplitude. While the absolute magnitudes of the cross sections differ from NRQCD estimates, the qualitative ordering of the channels is consistent with NRQCD expectations. We find a smooth power-law falloff of the cross section for both $χ_{c1}+η_c$ and $h_c+h_c$. These results provide benchmark predictions for future searches at high-luminosity $e^-e^+$ colliders.

</details>


### [93] [Probing Neutrinophilic Long-Range Forces at DUNE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04441)
*Sudip Jana,Pragyanprasu Swain*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a 'dark neutrino portal' where neutrino mass generation is tied to a light dark sector under a new U(1)_D symmetry; active–dark neutrino mixing mediates ultra-long-range forces from astrophysical and cosmological matter, modifying oscillations; DUNE’s long-baseline and strong matter effects enable discovery of new parameter space, including regions where the same coupling explains neutrino self-interactions relevant to the Hubble tension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain neutrino masses and probe physics beyond the Standard Model via a minimal dark sector linked to neutrinos, while addressing open questions such as the Hubble tension through neutrino self-interactions.

Method: Constructs a neutrinophilic U(1)_D gauge model with active–dark neutrino mixing; computes matter potentials induced by electrons and neutrons across celestial bodies and cosmological structure; performs sensitivity analysis for DUNE using oscillation probability modifications in matter.

Result: DUNE can probe previously unconstrained regions of the dark neutrino portal parameter space; the model simultaneously accommodates sizable neutrino self-interactions consistent with current oscillation data and potentially alleviates the Hubble tension.

Conclusion: The dark neutrino portal is a theoretically motivated and experimentally testable framework; DUNE offers unique discovery potential for ultra-long-range neutrino-mediated interactions and associated cosmological implications.

Abstract: Neutrino oscillations provide compelling evidence for physics beyond the Standard Model, while the weakly interacting nature of neutrinos makes them powerful probes of new interactions and hidden sectors. In this work, we investigate a \textit{dark neutrino portal} scenario in which neutrino mass generation is linked to a light dark sector charged under a new $U(1)_D$ gauge symmetry. While Standard Model fields remain neutral under $U(1)_D$, the dark neutrino sector is charged and communicates with the Standard Model exclusively through active--dark neutrino mixing. The associated neutrinophilic mediator induces ultra-long-range interactions, whereby electrons and neutrons in the Earth, Moon, Sun, Milky Way, and the cosmological matter distribution generate sizable matter potentials that modify neutrino oscillations. We explore the sensitivity of the upcoming Deep Underground Neutrino Experiment (DUNE), whose long baseline and pronounced matter effects make it uniquely suited to probe such interactions. We show that DUNE can access previously unexplored regions of parameter space and demonstrate that the same underlying coupling can simultaneously give rise to sizable neutrino self-interactions, including regions relevant for alleviating the Hubble tension, while remaining consistent with current neutrino oscillation constraints.

</details>


### [94] [Probing axion in the DFSZ model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04128)
*H. N. Long,N. H. T. Nha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a modified DFSZ I axion model with new right-handed neutrinos, where the Peccei–Quinn transformation depends solely on the scalar singlet’s PQ charge; it introduces four new scalars (including EW-scale and superheavy ones), ensures axion–Goldstone boson decoupling via unitary parametrization, yields two distinct axion–fermion couplings (Yukawa-type and anomaly-induced), fixes E/N = 8/3, and shows compatibility of the Higgs trilinear coupling modifier κ_λ with current LHC constraints for light new scalars (~150 GeV) and small v_u (~few GeV).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent, phenomenologically viable DFSZ-type axion model that resolves ambiguities in PQ charge assignment, eliminates axion–Z-Goldstone mixing, incorporates right-handed neutrinos for neutrino mass generation, and maintains compatibility with Higgs physics and axion–photon coupling constraints.

Method: The authors extend the DFSZ model by introducing right-handed neutrinos and a superheavy inflaton Φ, parametrize all scalars using unitary matrices to enforce orthogonal axion and Z-Goldstone directions, compute PQ charges and anomaly coefficients, derive axion–fermion couplings (both Yukawa and derivative/anomaly-mediated), and evaluate the trilinear Higgs self-coupling modifier κ_λ in the limit of light charged/CP-odd scalars and small v_u.

Result: The PQ transformation simplifies to depend only on PQ_φ; axion–G_Z mixing vanishes; two classes of axion–fermion couplings emerge; E/N is fixed to 8/3, confirming DFSZ I classification; κ_λ remains within experimental bounds (e.g., ATLAS/CMS) for m_{H±,A} ≈ 150 GeV and v_u ~ O(1–10 GeV).

Conclusion: The proposed extension provides a well-defined, testable DFSZ I axion framework compatible with electroweak symmetry breaking, neutrino mass generation, inflation, and current Higgs coupling measurements—offering a coherent path toward axion detection and BSM scalar searches.

Abstract: We show that with the introduction of new right-handed neutrinos $N_R$, the Peccei--Quinn ($PQ$) transformation in the DFSZ (Dine--Fischler--Srednicki--Zhitnitsky) model depends only on the $PQ$ charge of the scalar singlet $φ$, namely $PQ_φ$, and is independent of the sine and cosine of $\arctan(v_u/v_d)$. The model consists of four new scalars: a charged Higgs boson $H^\pm$, a CP-odd scalar $A$, and two CP-even scalars with masses at the EW scale (of a few hundred GeV), and a superheavy inflaton $Φ$. The scalar fields have been presented in the form of unitary matrices, in which there is no mixing between the axion and the Goldstone boson $G_Z$. As a result, there are two kinds of couplings between the axion and fermions, namely Yukawa-like interactions and anomaly-induced ones associated with derivative axion couplings. {The model belongs to the DFSZ I kind since the axion - photon coupling with $\fr E N = \fr 8 3$.} The trilinear Higgs self-coupling is investigated. We have shown that, in the parameter region where new scalar fields such as the singly charged Higgs boson $H^\pm$ and the CP-odd scalar $A$ have masses around 150 GeV and the vacuum expectation value of one Higgs doublet ($v_u$) is of the order of a few GeV, the coupling modifier $κ_λ$-a probe of new physics-is consistent with the current experimental constraints.

</details>


### [95] [Implications of exclusive photon leptoproduction measurements for the proton charge-radius puzzle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04481)
*The MMGPDs Collaboration,Muhammad Goharipour,Anoushiravan Moradi,K. Azizi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes proton electromagnetic form factors using high-precision exclusive photon leptoproduction (EP) data dominated by the Bethe-Heitler process, revealing tension with CLAS 2018 data; after resolving it via kinematic cuts or exclusion, the extracted charge radius is smaller than the PDG average but consistent with PRad and muonic hydrogen, while the magnetic radius agrees with the world average—supporting the small-radius solution to the proton charge-radius puzzle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in proton electromagnetic form factor extractions from BH-dominated exclusive photon leptoproduction (EP) data and assess their role in the proton charge-radius puzzle.

Method: Phenomenological fitting of high-precision EP data from CLAS and Hall A collaborations within a unified framework; systematic testing of consistency across datasets, application of low-$|t|$ cuts, and extraction of charge and magnetic radii from fitted form factors.

Result: Significant tension observed between CLAS 2018 data and other EP datasets; stable, consistent form factors and radii obtained upon excluding CLAS 2018 or applying low-$|t|$ cuts; extracted charge radius $r_E = 0.831(7)$ fm (smaller than PDG average), consistent with PRad and muonic hydrogen; magnetic radius $r_M = 0.85^{+0.04}_{-0.03}$ fm, compatible with world average.

Conclusion: BH-dominated EP measurements provide an independent, complementary probe of proton structure; results reinforce the small-proton-charge-radius solution and highlight the importance of dataset consistency and kinematic control in form factor analyses.

Abstract: In the present study, we extend our previous analysis of the proton electromagnetic form factors (FFs) extracted from exclusive photon leptoproduction (EP) measurements in kinematic regions where the Bethe-Heitler (BH) process dominates the cross section by including all currently available high-precision EP data from the CLAS and Hall~A Collaborations. Using the same phenomenological framework, we investigate the consistency among the different data sets, determine the proton electromagnetic FFs within several fitting scenarios, and extract the corresponding charge and magnetic radii. A significant tension is observed between the CLAS 2018 measurements and the remaining EP data. We show that excluding this data set, or restricting its kinematic coverage by imposing suitable low-$|t|$ cuts, leads to stable fits with good quality and consistent FFs. For all analyses, the extracted proton charge radius is smaller than the Particle Data Group average and most determinations based on elastic electron-proton scattering. However, the results are consistent, within uncertainties, with the PRad measurement and muonic hydrogen spectroscopy. In contrast, the magnetic radius is found to be compatible with the current world average. These results demonstrate that BH-dominated EP measurements provide an independent and complementary approach to determine the electromagnetic structure of the proton and offer additional support for the small-radius solution of the proton charge-radius puzzle.

</details>


### [96] [Left-right symmetry breaking in $E_6^L\times E_6^R$ occurs only in spacetime -- with possible implications for strong $CP$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04132)
*Tejinder P. Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a two-sided octonionic E6×E6 unification framework where left-right symmetry is interpreted as spacetime parity rather than internal symmetry, eliminating the need for ad hoc suppression of a second color group; it shows that anomaly constraints restrict viable U(1) charges to Q and B−L, rendering the dark-electromagnetic U(1)_dem invisible to visible fermions except via kinetic mixing; hypercharge emerges as a consistency relation without right-sector generators; and spontaneous parity enforces θ_QCD = 0 and real CKM determinant at tree level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the artificial suppression required for a nominal second color SU(3)_c' in two-sided octonionic E6^L × E6^R unification, and to reconcile left-right symmetry with spacetime parity (gravi-weak interpretation), anomaly constraints, and observed SM features (e.g., θ_QCD = 0, CKM phase).

Method: Analytical field-theoretic reasoning grounded in octonionic algebra, gravi-weak geometry (identifying SU(2)_R with gravitational frame group), anomaly cancellation analysis for U(1) extensions, spectral interpretation of mass via exceptional Jordan algebra, and parity-based symmetry breaking analysis including Higgs-bridge considerations.

Result: 1. SU(3)_c' collapses to physical SU(3)_c because left-right exchange is spacetime parity—not internal—so electron is colour-singlet; 2. All anomaly-free U(1) charges on visible fermions lie in span{Q, B−L}, excluding √m-pattern charges; 3. U(1)_dem is dark-sector only, with visible sector neutral (no fifth force); 4. Hypercharge Y emerges as Y = Q − T^3_L, independent of right-sector generators; 5. Spontaneous parity forces θ_QCD = 0 and arg det M = 0 at tree level while allowing nonzero CKM phase.

Conclusion: The gravi-weak reinterpretation of left-right symmetry eliminates artificial assumptions, yields testable predictions (e.g., no visible fifth force, θ_QCD = 0, real CKM determinant at tree level), and embeds SM quantum numbers geometrically—but loop stability and full Higgs-bridge dynamics remain open.

Abstract: Two-sided octonionic $E_6^L\times E_6^R$ unification carries a nominal second colour $SU(3)_{c'}$ which, gauged, would colour the charged lepton and must be suppressed by hand. We argue no such device is needed: the left-right symmetry acts in spacetime, not internally -- in the gravi-weak reading $SU(2)_R$ is the gravitational frame group and the exchange is ordinary parity on the Dirac field; a spacetime operation cannot double the internal colour, so $SU(3)_{c'}=SU(3)_c$, one vector-like colour with a colour-singlet electron. The right sector contributes one colour-blind datum, $\sqrt m$ (values: the exceptional-Jordan trace split, an input; $N_R$ fixes only the colour representation). An anomaly no-go proven here shows every anomaly-free $U(1)$ on visible fermions lies in span$\{Q,B-L\}$, which the $\sqrt m$ pattern does not, in any sign or chirality assignment. The gauged dark-electromagnetic $U(1)_{dem}$ -- the mirror electroweak chain's unbroken remnant -- therefore carries the parity-mirror of electric charge on the dark sector: the visible fermions are dem-neutral (kinetic mixing the sole portal; no visible fifth force), and $\sqrt m$ enters visible physics only as the spectral label of the Jordan mass operator. The same recognition recovers hypercharge as the consistency relation $Y=Q-T^3_L$, $Q=N/3$, with no right-sector generator. As an application, the spontaneous parity forbids the QCD vacuum angle ($θ_{QCD}=0$, conditional on the gravi-weak identification), while the flavour rotors have real determinant, so $\arg\det M=0$ at tree level (exact for the Cabibbo rung, texture-contingent in full), coexisting with a nonzero CKM phase. Loop stability -- where minimal gauged-$SU(2)_R$ parity solutions fail -- is plausibly evaded (no gauged $W_R$) but open, pending the Higgs-bridge matrix elements. We tag throughout what is derived, what is input, and what is open.

</details>


### [97] [Exclusive Quark and Gluon Dijet Production as Probes of GPDs at Collider Energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04482)
*Zhuoyi Pang,Paweł Sznajder,Lech Szymanowski,Jakub Wagner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies exclusive electroproduction of dijets using collinear factorization to probe generalized parton distributions (GPDs), extending prior work by incorporating helicity GPDs and an additional electromagnetic channel for quark dijets, and newly analyzing gluon dijet production; predictions are compared with HERA data and projected for the Electron-Ion Collider.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To use exclusive dijet electroproduction as a novel probe of generalized parton distributions (GPDs), especially helicity-dependent ones, and to explore complementary sensitivity to nucleon structure beyond existing channels.

Method: Collinear factorization framework applied to exclusive electroproduction of quark and gluon dijets; includes helicity GPD contributions and an additional leading-order electromagnetic channel mediated by elastic nucleon form factors.

Result: Predictions for quark and gluon dijet cross sections are obtained, validated against HERA data, and projected for improved precision at the Electron-Ion Collider.

Conclusion: Exclusive dijet electroproduction provides a viable and complementary channel to access helicity GPDs and nucleon form factors; gluon dijet production offers new constraints on gluonic GPDs, enhancing 3D imaging of the nucleon.

Abstract: We study exclusive electroproduction of dijets in the collinear factorization framework as a probe of generalized parton distributions (GPDs). For quark dijet production, we extend previous analyses by including contributions from helicity GPDs and by assessing an additional leading-order electromagnetic channel governed by elastic nucleon form factors. Furthermore, we investigate exclusive gluon dijet production. We compare our prediction to HERA data and provide projections for measurements at the future Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [98] [The variable flavor number scheme to three-loop order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05235)
*J. Ablinger,A. Behring,J. Blümlein,A. De Freitas,A. von Manteuffel,C. Schneider,K. Schönwald*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops the variable flavor number scheme (VFNS) to three-loop order, incorporating single- and two-mass effects into massless parton densities and introducing heavy-quark PDFs for charm and bottom; it validates the scheme via renormalization group analysis at large $Q^2$ and provides numerical results for charged/neutral current Wilson coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently incorporate heavy-quark mass effects in QCD calculations of deep-inelastic scattering, bridging fixed-flavor-number and zero-mass schemes while preserving accuracy across momentum scales.

Method: Three-loop perturbative QCD calculation within the variable flavor number scheme; renormalization group analysis to establish scale evolution; derivation and numerical evaluation of Wilson coefficients for charged and neutral currents.

Result: Consistent VFNS formulation to three loops; explicit inclusion of charm and bottom PDFs; validated asymptotic behavior matching non-power-suppressed heavy-flavor corrections at large $Q^2$; publicly available numerical implementations of Wilson coefficients.

Conclusion: The three-loop VFNS provides a rigorous, scale-dependent framework for heavy-flavor treatment in PDF evolution and structure function calculations, enabling high-precision predictions for collider and DIS experiments.

Abstract: We describe the variable flavor number scheme to three-loop order, which modifies the massless parton densities by single- and two-mass effects and introduces heavy-quark parton distribution functions for charm and bottom. A renormalization group analysis shows the validity of this picture at large scales $Q^2$, where it resembles the non-power-suppressed heavy-flavor corrections completely. We also provide numerical implementations of a series of charged and neutral current Wilson coefficients.

</details>


### [99] [A MeV-Scale Dark QCD Solution to the Axion Domain Wall Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04137)
*Jun Guo,Zhaofeng Kang,Jiang Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel solution to the axion domain wall problem by introducing a mixed anomaly between the Peccei-Quinn (PQ) $U(1)$ symmetry and a hidden $SU(N_c)$ gauge group; the resulting hidden instanton-induced cosine potential explicitly breaks the domain wall symmetry, lifts vacuum degeneracy, and—within a narrow dark QCD scale window (0.1–3 MeV)—avoids reintroducing the strong CP problem; it predicts testable signatures: stochastic gravitational waves from domain wall collapse (constrained by current pulsar timing arrays) and MeV-scale di-photon signals from axion–dark-glueball mixing (requiring next-generation MeV telescopes).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the axion domain wall problem inherent in standard PQ solutions to the strong CP problem, without reintroducing fine-tuning or violating observational constraints on the effective θ angle.

Method: Introduce a mixed 't Hooft anomaly between the global $U(1)_{\rm PQ}$ symmetry and a confining hidden $SU(N_c)$ gauge sector ('dark QCD'); compute the resultant instanton-generated cosine potential for the axion field; analyze its impact on domain wall number $N_{\rm DW}$, vacuum structure, effective θ angle, and cosmological evolution; derive gravitational wave spectra from domain wall decay and axion–dark-glueball mixing-induced di-photon rates.

Result: A viable parameter space exists where the dark QCD confinement scale $\Lambda_{\rm dark} \in [0.1, 3]\,\text{MeV}$ simultaneously lifts degenerate vacua, suppresses long-lived domain walls via bias-induced collapse, keeps the residual effective θ angle sufficiently small ($|\theta_{\rm eff}| \lesssim 10^{-10}$), satisfies dark glueball relic density bounds, and yields detectable GWs (within PTA sensitivity) and MeV di-photon signals.

Conclusion: The mixed-anomaly mechanism provides a simple, predictive, and falsifiable resolution to the axion domain wall problem, linking high-energy axion physics to observable MeV-scale astroparticle phenomena and early-universe gravitational-wave backgrounds.

Abstract: PQ solution to the strong CP problem probably encounters the axion domain wall problem. In this article, we propose a simple and testable solution, assuming that the $U(1)_{\rm PQ}$ possesses mixed anomaly to a hidden $SU(N_c)$ color. Then, the axion field receives a new cosine potential from the hidden instantons, which breaks the $Z_{N_{\rm DW}}$ subgroup explicitly. The new potential lifts the vacua degeneracy, but also drives the effective $θ$ angle away from the origin, re-incuring the strong CP problem. However, we find that the dark QCD scale within the 0.1 to 3 MeV window survives, maintaining a delicate balance. Two observational signatures are explored: gravitational waves from domain wall collapse, already probed by current PTAs, and di-photon signals from axion-dark-glueball mixing, which require next-generation MeV telescopes. The scenario favors a cold dark QCD sector consistent with dark glueball relic constraints.

</details>


### [100] [Advancing axion detection: Photon regeneration in high-sensitivity Penning trap experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04155)
*Yao Chen,Ju Guo,Libo Zhao,Ziyao Yan,Jie Feng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel lab-based axion search method combining photon regeneration ('light-shining-through-a-wall') with high-sensitivity Penning traps and microwave cavities, achieving unprecedented sensitivity $g_{aγγ} \le 7.10\times 10^{-8}~\mathrm{GeV^{-1}}$ in one day for sub-MHz axions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of astrophysical axion searches and improve laboratory sensitivity to the axion-photon coupling $g_{aγγ}$, particularly for low-mass (sub-MHz) axions relevant to dark matter.

Method: Integrates resonant photon regeneration (LSW) with high-precision Penning traps—leveraging their strong magnetic fields, electromagnetic field metrology, and single-particle detection—inside microwave cavities for resonant enhancement.

Result: Preliminary calculations predict sensitivity to $g_{aγγ} \le 7.10\times 10^{-8}~\mathrm{GeV^{-1}}$ in one day for axions below 1 MHz—orders of magnitude better than current LSW limits in that mass range.

Conclusion: The proposed Penning-trap-enhanced LSW platform provides a controlled, background-suppressed, and highly sensitive laboratory probe for low-mass axions, advancing direct detection strategies for QCD axion dark matter.

Abstract: The axion, a hypothetical particle proposed to solve the strong CP problem and considered a viable candidate for dark matter, has prompted extensive experimental efforts for its detection. This study presents a novel approach combining photon regeneration techniques ("light-shining-through-a-wall") with high-sensitivity Penning trap technologies to enhance the search for axions. Penning traps offer significant advantages, including precise electromagnetic field measurement, strong magnetic fields, and single-particle detection capabilities. By integrating these traps with resonantly enhanced photon regeneration using microwave cavities, our proposed method significantly increases sensitivity to axion-photon couplings. Preliminary calculations demonstrate an unprecedented achievable sensitivity, reaching an axion-photon coupling constant limit of $g_{aγγ} \le 7.10\times 10^{-8}\mathrm{GeV ^{-1}}$ in just one day, specifically targeting axion energies below 1 MHz. This experimental setup presents a robust and controlled platform, circumventing astrophysical uncertainties, and represents a substantial advancement in laboratory searches for axions and our understanding of dark matter.

</details>


### [101] [Vector meson photoproduction on the nucleus and the extraction of the nuclear suppression factor using asymmetric beams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04228)
*Ronan McNulty,Wolfgang Schafer*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using asymmetric proton-lead collisions at the LHC to unambiguously measure nuclear suppression in vector meson photoproduction, avoiding the two-fold ambiguity inherent in symmetric nucleus-nucleus collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the two-fold ambiguity in identifying the photon-emitting nucleus in symmetric nucleus-nucleus collisions when measuring vector meson photoproduction cross-sections on nuclei.

Method: Analyzing photoproduction of vector mesons in asymmetric proton-lead collisions, leveraging the ratio of cross-sections on proton versus lead to directly extract the nuclear suppression factor with minimal model dependence.

Result: Demonstrates the feasibility of using existing and future p–Pb collision data at the LHC for such measurements.

Conclusion: Asymmetric p–Pb collisions provide a clean, model-independent probe of nuclear shadowing effects in coherent photoproduction, offering superior precision compared to symmetric heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: The cross-section for photoproduction of a vector meson on a nucleus is usually determined in nucleus-nucleus collisions, which introduces a two-fold ambiguity as to which nucleus emitted the photon. This can be resolved in asymmetric collisions of protons and nuclei, where the ratio of photoproduction cross-sections on the proton and nucleus directly measures the nuclear suppression factor with minimal model dependence. The feasibilty of using existing and future proton-lead collisions at the LHC is evaluated.

</details>


### [102] [Comment on "Radiative corrections to tau -> pi(K) nu_tau[gamma]: A reliable new physics test"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04435)
*Markus Finkemeier*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper identifies and corrects a sign error in the axial-vector convention used by Guo and Roig (GR10) and subsequently adopted by Arroyo-Ureña et al. (AHLRR) for the structure-dependent amplitude of τ→Pν_τγ decays; this correction flips the vector-sector sign relative to the chiral anomaly but preserves agreement with O(p⁴) chiral perturbation theory in the axial sector, yielding minor (<0.01%) downward shifts in δr_SD for π and K channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve an inconsistency between the resonance chiral theory amplitude for τ→Pν_τγ and chiral perturbation theory predictions, specifically concerning sign conventions for the axial coupling in the structure-dependent amplitude.

Method: Analytical comparison and reinterpretation of the amplitude definitions across GR10 and AHLRR using corrected convention dictionaries; consistency checks against O(p⁴) chiral perturbation theory and the Decker-Finkemeier addendum.

Result: The axial convention error is identified and corrected: F_A^DF = −2√2 m_P F_A^GR (not +).
