This yields sign agreement with O(p⁴) χPT in the axial sector but opposite sign to the chiral anomaly in the vector sector—consistent with the Decker-Finkemeier addendum. Numerically, δr_SD decreases from +0.150% to +0.146% (π) and from +0.18% to ~+0.16% (K).

Conclusion: The sign error invalidates AHLRR’s identification of their δr_SD with the pre-addendum GR10 result; the corrected amplitude satisfies theoretical consistency requirements in both axial and vector sectors, albeit with small quantitative impact on branching ratio corrections.

Abstract: Arroyo-Ureña, Hernández-Tomé, López-Castro, Roig, and Rosell (AHLRR) take the structure-dependent (SD) amplitude for $τ\to P ν_τγ$ from the resonance chiral theory result of Guo and Roig (GR10), obtaining $δr_{SD} = +0.15\%$ in the pion channel. The convention dictionary in footnote 2 of GR10, repeated in footnote 1 of AHLRR, has the wrong sign in its axial entry: the printed amplitudes imply $F_A^{DF} = -2\sqrt{2}\, m_P F_A^{GR}$, not $+2\sqrt{2}\, m_P F_A^{GR}$. With the corrected dictionary, the GR10 amplitude agrees with the $O(p^4)$ chiral prediction in the axial sector but carries the opposite sign to the chiral anomaly in the vector sector; the relative sign fixed in the Decker-Finkemeier addendum is confirmed in both sectors. The identification in footnote 6 of AHLRR of their value with the pre-addendum result rests on the erroneous dictionary and does not hold. The numerical impact is small: $δr_{SD}$ shifts from $+0.150\%$ to $+0.146\%$ in the pion channel and from $+0.18\%$ to about $+0.16\%$ in the kaon channel.

</details>


### [103] [Shifted Hybrid Realization of Non-Minimal Higgs Inflation in Light of ACT DR6 and Planck Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04504)
*Nadir Ijaz,Pirzada,Mansoor Ur Rehman*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies non-minimal Higgs inflation in a no-scale supergravity framework, showing how a leading nonrenormalizable operator modifies the Starobinsky attractor to align the scalar spectral index with recent ACT/Planck CMB data, while keeping r ~ 10⁻³–10⁻² and ensuring sub-Planckian field values, controlled SUGRA corrections, and viable reheating + leptogenesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile Higgs inflation predictions—particularly the scalar spectral index n_s—with tighter constraints from ACT DR6 and Planck, which disfavor the canonical Starobinsky value; and to embed Higgs inflation in a theoretically consistent, UV-motivated no-scale supergravity setting with viable post-inflationary cosmology.

Method: Constructs a shifted hybrid inflation model in no-scale supergravity where the GUT Higgs direction acts as the inflaton after orthogonal fields are stabilized; derives an effective single-field potential including the leading nonrenormalizable superpotential term; computes inflationary observables (n_s, r), assesses SUGRA corrections and field excursions; performs numerical scan of parameter space incorporating reheating dynamics and nonthermal leptogenesis.

Result: The nonrenormalizable operator induces a controlled deformation of the Starobinsky attractor, shifting n_s into the observationally preferred range (~0.962–0.970) while preserving r ≈ 10⁻³–10⁻²; inflaton remains sub-Planckian; SUGRA corrections are under control; viable parameter regions satisfying CMB, reheating, and successful baryogenesis via nonthermal leptogenesis are identified.

Conclusion: Non-minimal Higgs inflation in no-scale supergravity is compatible with state-of-the-art CMB data when augmented by the leading higher-dimension superpotential operator; it provides a UV-complete, phenomenologically viable alternative to standard Starobinsky or chaotic inflation, with testable implications for primordial gravitational waves and leptogenesis.

Abstract: We investigate non-minimal Higgs inflation in a no-scale-inspired supergravity framework and confront its predictions with the latest CMB constraints from ACT DR6 and \emph{Planck}. Working within a shifted hybrid inflation scenario, we construct an effective single-field description in which the GUT Higgs direction serves as the inflaton after stabilization of the orthogonal scalar fields. We show that the inclusion of the leading nonrenormalizable operator in the superpotential induces a controlled deformation of the Starobinsky attractor, allowing the scalar spectral index to be shifted into the range favored by recent ACT and related CMB datasets while maintaining a small tensor-to-scalar ratio, $r \sim 10^{-3}-10^{-2}$. The resulting inflationary dynamics remain theoretically consistent, with controlled supergravity corrections and sub-Planckian inflaton field values. We perform a detailed numerical analysis of the model parameter space, including reheating and nonthermal leptogenesis, and identify regions that simultaneously satisfy current observational constraints and yield a viable post-inflationary cosmological history.

</details>


### [104] [Possibility of the antibottom-strange molecular pentaquarks near $\bar BΣ$ and $\bar B^*Σ$ thresholds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04679)
*Jian-Kang Zhao,Nijiati Yalikun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates molecular states in the coupled-channel system $B_s^0N$–$ar BΛ$–$ar B^*Λ$–$ar BΣ$–$ar B^*Σ$ using a one-boson-exchange model with $S$–$D$ mixing and a short-range $δ(r)$ term; bound and resonant states are identified via analytic continuation of the $S$-matrix, yielding three near-threshold poles (two resonances, one virtual) in the $1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ sectors, interpreted as pentaquark candidates $P_{ar b s}$ with masses ~6.44–6.52 GeV and narrow widths (~MeV).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the existence and properties of hidden-bottom strange pentaquark ($P_{\bar b s}$) molecular states arising from strong interactions among heavy meson–baryon channels, motivated by analogies to known $P_c$ and $P_{cs}$ states and theoretical interest in exotic hadrons near thresholds.

Method: One-boson-exchange (OBE) model incorporating $S$-$D$ wave mixing and a tunable short-range $δ(r)$ contact interaction; full coupled-channel analysis of $B_s^0N$–$\bar BΛ$–$\bar B^*Λ$–$\bar BΣ$–$\bar B^*Σ$; pole search via analytic continuation of the $S$-matrix in the complex energy plane; pole coupling and partial-width decomposition.

Result: Three near-threshold poles found: a virtual state below $\bar B^*Σ$ threshold (strongly $δ(r)$-dependent), and two resonant poles ($1/2^-(\bar BΣ)$, $1/2^-(\bar B^*Σ)$, $3/2^-(\bar B^*Σ)$) robust under coupling; predicted masses 6.44–6.52 GeV, widths few–several MeV; dominant couplings to $\bar BΣ$/$\bar B^*Σ$, observable signals in $B_s^0N$, $\bar BΛ$, $\bar B^*Λ$ invariant mass spectra.

Conclusion: Near-threshold $\bar BΣ$/$\bar B^*Σ$-driven molecular pentaquarks $P_{\bar b s}$ with $J^P = 1/2^-, 3/2^-$ are viable and experimentally accessible; results provide concrete mass/width predictions and channel-specific signatures for discovery at LHCb or Belle II.

Abstract: The molecular states in coupled channel system of $B_s^0N-\bar BΛ-\bar B^*Λ-\bar BΣ-\bar B^*Σ$ are investigated within one-boson-exchange model that includes $S$-$D$ wave mixing and a tunable short-range $δ(r)$ term. Bound, resonant, and virtual states are searched by analytically continuing the $S$ matrix in the complex energy plane. In the single-channel analysis, the $\bar BΣ$ and $\bar B^*Σ$ systems with $J^P=1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ are found to be attractive and form three bound states with reasonable cutoff region, corresponding to $1/2^-(\bar BΣ)$, $1/2^-(\bar B^*Σ)$, and $3/2^-(\bar B^*Σ)$ . When coupled-channel dynamics is included, these states evolve into near-threshold poles that produce enhancements in the $B_s^0N$, $\bar BΛ$, and $\bar B^*Λ$ invariant mass spectra. The pole below the $\bar B^*Σ$ threshold in the $J^P=1/2^-$ system depends strongly on the treatment of the short-range $δ(r)$ term and becomes a virtual state when this contribution is removed, while the other two resonant poles and their line shapes are only moderately affected. For representative parameter region, the predicted masses of these states lie in the $6.44-6.52$ GeV region and have widths of a few to several MeV. The pole coupling and partial-width analyses indicate that the poles are generated mainly by the $\bar BΣ$ and $\bar B^*Σ$ channels interaction, and their observable signals are expected mainly in the lower open channels $B_s^0N$, $\bar BΛ$, and $\bar B^*Λ$. These results support the existence of near-threshold molecular pentaquark $P_{\bar b s}$ and provide useful guidance in future experimental searches.

</details>


### [105] [Pion Distribution Amplitudes from Functional QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04871)
*Lei Chang,Wei-jie Fu,Chuang Huang,Jan M. Pawlowski,Yang-yang Tan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: First-principles fRG+LaMET calculation of pion DA yields ⟨ξ²⟩_π = 0.267, with negligible extrapolation error due to high pion momentum (P_z = 4.5 GeV).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a first-principles, functional QCD prediction of the pion distribution amplitude without relying on lattice discretization artifacts or model-dependent assumptions.

Method: Combines large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) with functional renormalisation group (fRG) to compute the quasi-distribution amplitude from QCD correlation functions; uses physical inputs only (strong coupling and current quark masses); pushes pion longitudinal momentum to P_z = 4.5 GeV to suppress higher-twist and matching uncertainties.

Result: Quasi-DA is fully saturated at P_z = 4.5 GeV; second Mellin moment ⟨ξ²⟩_π = 0.267 — smaller than prior lattice-LaMET results but consistent with other nonperturbative methods (e.g., Dyson–Schwinger equations, QCD sum rules).

Conclusion: Demonstrates feasibility and precision of fRG+LaMET for light-cone observables; provides a robust, continuum, ab initio determination of the pion DA moment, reducing reliance on lattice systematics and extrapolations.

Abstract: We present the first functional QCD calculation of the pion distribution amplitude (DA) using the large-momentum effective theory within the functional renormalisation group (fRG) approach. With only the strong coupling and current quark masses as inputs, we compute the quasi-DA from first-principles QCD correlation functions. By pushing the pion momentum up to $P_z = 4.5\ \mathrm{GeV}$, the quasi-DA becomes fully saturated, rendering the extrapolation errors to the light-cone limit negligible. The resulting second-order moment $\langle ξ^2 \rangle_π= 0.267$ is significantly smaller than existing lattice-LaMET determinations and lies in a range consistent with other nonperturbative approaches.

</details>


### [106] [Beyond Half-Life Limits: Robust Operator-Level Interpretation of Multi-Isotope Neutrinoless Double-Beta Decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04932)
*K. Ishidoshiro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how neutrinoless double-beta decay (0νββ) limits translate into constraints on low-energy lepton-number-violating (LNV) operators, assessing stability across isotopes and nuclear matrix element (NME) methods using the νDoBe tool; it finds light-Majorana exchange most stable, dimension-six operators robust, and dimension-nine short-range operators highly sensitive to nuclear inputs—highlighting multi-isotope programs as essential for validating operator interpretations post-discovery.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the reliability of inferring low-energy LNV operator coefficients from 0νββ experimental limits or sensitivities, particularly how such inferences depend on choice of isotope and NME method—crucial for connecting observations to UV models of lepton-number violation.

Method: Uses νDoBe to convert 0νββ half-life limits/sensitivities into constraints on LNV coefficients for multiple isotopes, operator classes (light-Majorana, dim-6, dim-9), and NME methods; quantifies stability via spread of coefficient limits (pre-discovery) and isotope-to-isotope half-life ratios (post-discovery).

Result: Light-Majorana exchange yields the most stable coefficient limits across isotopes and NME methods; dim-6 operators show reasonable robustness; dim-9 short-range operators exhibit strong dependence on nuclear inputs; isotope-ratio tests post-discovery are only reliable when half-life patterns remain stable across NME methods.

Conclusion: Multi-isotope 0νββ programs provide critical validation beyond sensitivity reach by testing the stability of operator interpretations; spread-based analysis identifies which LNV scenarios are suitable for isotope-ratio tests and which require cautious nuclear-theoretical treatment before UV model mapping.

Abstract: Current and future neutrinoless double-beta decay ($0νββ$) searches are usually characterized by half-life limits or projected sensitivities. For operator-level interpretation, however, the relevant question is not only the strength of the limit, but also the stability of the inferred constraints on low-energy lepton-number-violating (LNV) coefficients under changes of isotope and nuclear matrix-element (NME) method. Using $ν$DoBe, we convert half-life limits or projected sensitivities into limits on low-energy LNV coefficients for several choices of isotope, operator class, and NME method. We then compare the resulting limits across isotopes and NME methods. We quantify the stability by the spread of coefficient limits in non-observation scenarios and, for single-operator post-discovery benchmarks, by the spread of isotope-to-isotope half-life ratios. For the operator examples considered in this paper, the light-Majorana exchange mechanism gives the most stable interpretation. The selected dimension-six operators remain reasonably robust, while the short-range dimension-nine examples show a stronger dependence on isotope and NME-method choices. The same hierarchy appears in post-discovery benchmarks based on isotope-to-isotope half-life ratios: the reliability of such tests depends on whether the predicted half-life pattern remains stable across NME methods. A multi-isotope $0νββ$ program therefore provides information beyond half-life reach by testing the stability of the operator interpretation. This spread-based analysis provides a practical way to identify which low-energy operator scenarios are suitable for isotope-ratio tests after a possible discovery, and which require more care before being connected to ultraviolet models of lepton-number violation.

</details>


### [107] [Flavor decomposition of the gravitational form factors and mechanical structure of the proton](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05079)
*Hyun-Chul Kim,June-Young Kim,Ho-Yeon Won*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent work on flavor decomposition of gravitational form factors and mechanical properties of the proton using the chiral quark-soliton model, with twist projection of the energy-momentum tensor to isolate the $\overline{c}$ form factor; it reports flavor-separated mass, spin, pressure, and shear-force distributions, highlighting dominant up-quark contributions to mass/spin and significant strange-quark contribution to the $D$-term, and analyzes mechanical stability via pressure and shear-force.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the internal flavor structure and mechanical properties (e.g., pressure, shear force, stability) of the proton through gravitational form factors, especially isolating subleading contributions like the $\overline{c}$ form factor from twist-4 components.

Method: Uses the chiral quark-soliton model grounded in the QCD instanton vacuum; performs twist projection of the energy-momentum tensor operator into twist-2 and twist-4 components; computes flavor-decomposed gravitational form factors (mass, spin, $D$-term) and mechanical distributions (pressure, shear force).

Result: Up quark dominates proton mass and spin; strange quark contributes significantly to the $D$-term form factor; full flavor-decomposed mass, spin, pressure, and shear-force distributions are obtained; mechanical stability conditions are analyzed via spatially resolved pressure and shear-force profiles.

Conclusion: Flavor decomposition reveals distinct roles of quark flavors in proton structure and mechanics; twist-4 contributions enable access to the $\overline{c}$ form factor; mechanical properties confirm proton stability governed by balanced pressure and shear-force distributions.

Abstract: We review in the present talk a series of recent works on the flavor decomposition of the gravitational form factors of the proton and its mechanical properties within the framework of the chiral quark-soliton model. Starting from the energy-momentum tensor operator derived from the QCD instanton vacuum, we carry out the twist projection of the energy-momentum tensor operator into its twist-2 and twist-4 components, which enables us to isolate the $\overline{c}$ form factor originating from the twist-4 operator. We present the flavor-decomposed mass, spin, pressure, and shear-force distributions of the proton, together with the corresponding form factors. While the up quark dominates both the mass and the spin of the proton, the strange quark is found to contribute sizably to the $D$-term form factor. We also discuss the mechanical stability of the proton governed by the pressure and shear-force distributions.

</details>


### [108] [Insight on confinement from the QCD effective charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05144)
*A. Deur*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes α_g₁ as a physical, observable QCD coupling with analytic structure reflecting confinement via imaginary singularities in the long-distance regime, leading to exponentially suppressed parton propagators beyond ~1/Λ_s.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a physically observable, analytically consistent running coupling for QCD (α_g₁) that encodes confinement through its analytic structure—specifically, conjugate imaginary singularities—and connects it to the non-perturbative behavior of Green's functions.

Method: Uses renormalization-group evolution, light-front holographic QCD, and world data on α_s to construct α_g₁; analyzes its analytic structure (singularities at Q² = ±iΛ_s²); links singularities to dressed parton propagators (not vertices) via known relations between α_s and QCD Green's functions; derives asymptotic long-distance behavior of propagators.

Result: α_g₁ exhibits two conjugate imaginary singularities in the infrared, assigned to dressed parton propagators, yielding an exponential suppression ∼e^{-Λ_s|x|/√2}|x|^{-5/2+d_a} at distances |x| ≳ 1/Λ_s—consistent with confinement.

Conclusion: Confinement can be intuitively interpreted as the long-distance suppression of QCD Green’s functions, encoded in the analytic structure of the observable coupling α_g₁; this bridges perturbative RG evolution and non-perturbative confinement phenomenology.

Abstract: Like the Gell-Mann--Low coupling $α$ of QED, the effective charge $α_{g_1}$ is a physical, observable-defined running coupling that can serve as the QCD coupling $α_s$. It can be represented by an analytic form consistent with renormalization-group evolution, light-front holographic QCD, and the world data on $α_s$. As an observable, $α_{g_1}$ has a physically relevant analytic structure that reflects the underlying partonic dynamics. It displays, in the long-distance regime, two imaginary conjugate singularities at $Q^2=\pm iΛ_s^2$, with $Λ_s$ the QCD scale. To connect this structure to confinement, we use known relations between $α_s$ and the parton dressed propagators and vertices. Since, unlike vertices, propagators characterize a field variation between separate locations, the two singularities are assigned to the propagators. This results in the parton propagators displaying a long-distance behavior $e^{-Λ_s|x|/\sqrt{2}}|x|^{-5/2+d_a}$ where propagation is suppressed beyond distances of order $1/Λ_s$, as expected from confinement. This offers an intuitive interpretation of confinement as the suppression of QCD Green's functions at long distance.

</details>


### [109] [Pathways and impediments towards a detection of the relic neutrino wind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05221)
*Andrew J. Long,Michiru Uwabo-Niibo,Masahide Yamaguchi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the feasibility of detecting the cosmic neutrino background (CNB) 'wind'—a dipolar anisotropy due to Earth’s motion relative to the CNB rest frame—via angular analysis of electron recoils near the tritium β-decay endpoint; it finds that a 3σ detection requires ≥10⁵× more exposure than total CNB flux detection, with strong sensitivity to energy resolution and neutrino nature (Dirac vs. Majorana).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond mere detection of the cosmic neutrino background (CNB) and probe its kinematic properties—specifically the dipolar anisotropy ('CNB wind') arising from Earth's motion relative to the CNB rest frame—as a novel window into neutrino physics and cosmology.

Method: Analytical and quantitative assessment of CNB wind detection sensitivity using angular distribution of electrons at the tritium β-decay endpoint; calculation of required exposure (mass × time) for 3σ significance, including effects of energy resolution, systematic uncertainties, and neutrino helicity structure for Dirac vs. Majorana cases.

Result: Detecting the CNB wind demands ≥10⁵× greater exposure than detecting the isotropic CNB flux; energy resolution finer than the neutrino mass scale necessitates exquisite control of systematics; for nonrelativistic CNB neutrinos, the Majorana wind signal is suppressed by ~ (m_ν/T_ν)² due to helicity-odd correlation cancellation.

Conclusion: Direct detection of the CNB wind is extremely challenging with current or near-future technology, requiring breakthroughs in detector mass, exposure time, energy resolution, and systematic control—especially for Majorana neutrinos—making it a long-term goal beyond initial CNB flux detection.

Abstract: A direct detection of the cosmic neutrino background (CNB) in laboratories on Earth has been called the ``holy grail'' of experimental neutrino physics, but a still more glorious prize awaits. Beyond simply detecting the presence of relic neutrinos and measuring their flux, one may aspire to measure their energy distribution, polarization, anisotropies, temporal variation, and other properties. In this work we focus on the CNB wind, which is the approximately dipolar anisotropy in the CNB flux resulting from the relative velocity of the CNB rest frame and the lab frame. We consider a CNB detection strategy based on measuring the angular distribution of recoiling electrons at the tritium $β$-decay endpoint. In order to quantify the difficulty of detecting the CNB wind, we calculate the required exposure (detector mass times observation duration) for a $3σ$ discovery. We find that detecting the CNB wind would require an exposure that is at least $10^{5}$ times larger than what's required for detecting the CNB flux alone. Additionally if the experimental energy resolution were to exceed the neutrino mass scale, then an exceptionally good control of systematic uncertainties would also be required. For nonrelativistic neutrinos, the Majorana wind signal is suppressed relative to the Dirac case by the cancellation of the leading helicity-odd angular-correlation term, leading parametrically to an exposure penalty of order $(m_ν/T_ν)^2$.

</details>


### [110] [Spin Polarization of Proca Stars Formed by Gravitational Bose--Einstein Condensation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05278)
*Jiajun Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the internal spin polarization of Proca stars formed from gravitational Bose–Einstein condensation of a three-component nonrelativistic vector field, showing that core spin arises from the polarization state (especially ellipticity and circularity) of the dominant condensed mode—not a universal value—with coherent net spin fraction averaging ~0.62 and significant ensemble scatter explained by random elliptical polarization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether internal spin polarization in gravitationally condensed nonrelativistic Proca stars is a genuine, controllable vector degree of freedom—or instead a fixed, universal property—and to identify its physical origin in the component-space structure of the condensed field.

Method: Analyzes idealized periodic-box simulations of Proca star formation; decomposes aperture-averaged spin into coherent net fraction, local polarization fraction, and their ratio; computes and diagonalizes the core polarization matrix; compares leading eigenvector spin estimates against isotropic random-complex-vector and equal-amplitude random-phase references; contrasts initial conditions (independent, correlated, circular) to assess polarization-state dependence.

Result: Coherent net spin fraction averages ⟨χ_net⟩ ≃ 0.62 with substantial ensemble scatter; scatter is explained by random elliptical polarization of the dominant mode, not universality; leading eigenvector of polarization matrix estimates single-mode spin fraction, and deviation from direct integration quantifies rank-one departure; isotropic random-complex-vector model fits data better than equal-amplitude random-phase; circular initial conditions maximize polarization, yielding ordering independent → correlated → circular.

Conclusion: Internal polarization is a genuine, physically meaningful vector degree of freedom in nonrelativistic Proca stars, and core spin is determined by the polarization state (particularly ellipticity/circularity) of the dominant condensed mode—not by a fixed universal value.

Abstract: We study the internal spin polarization of Proca stars formed by gravitational Bose--Einstein condensation of a three-component nonrelativistic vector field. In idealized periodic-box simulations, we decompose the aperture-averaged spin into a coherent net fraction, a local polarization fraction, and their ratio, thereby distinguishing genuine coherent core polarization from local spin density whose direction cancels inside the aperture. For independent vector components, condensation produces Proca stars that are sizably but not maximally polarized. Across an independent-component simulation ensemble, the coherent core-spin fraction has mean $\langleχ_{\rm net}\rangle\simeq0.62$, with substantial realization-to-realization scatter. We interpret this scatter as the outcome of random elliptical polarization of the dominant component-space mode, rather than as evidence for a universal Proca-star spin fraction. This interpretation is supported by the core polarization matrix: its leading eigenvector provides an estimate of the ideal single-mode spin fraction, while the difference between this estimate and the directly integrated coherent spin tracks the departure of the core from a rank-one component-space state. The measured leading-eigenvector spin fractions are broadly compatible with an isotropic random-complex-vector reference and less compatible with an equal-amplitude random-phase reference. Correlated and circular initial data drive the dominant component-space mode toward the circular-polarization bound, giving the ordering independent $\rightarrow$ correlated $\rightarrow$ circular. These results show that internal polarization is a genuine vector degree of freedom of gravitationally condensed nonrelativistic Proca stars, and that the resulting core spin is controlled by the polarization of the dominant condensed mode rather than by a fixed universal value.

</details>


### [111] [Parametric Resonance of Higgsed Vector Dark Matter: Inflationary Initial Conditions and Sourced Displacements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05309)
*Imtiaz Khan,Salvatore Capozziello,G. Mustafa,Niamat Ullah,Farruh Atamurotov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the initial-condition problem for parametric resonance production of vector dark matter in a Higgsed Abelian sector, showing stochastic inflation fails to generate sufficient dark-Higgs displacement; it identifies a viable 'classically sourced' branch driven by a negative Hubble-induced mass, and derives stringent consistency conditions linking dark matter mass to inflationary parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the initial-condition problem for parametric resonance production of vector dark matter — specifically, the origin of the required large initial dark-Higgs field displacement — within a weakly coupled Abelian-Higgs theory.

Method: Combines calibrated nonlinear broad-resonance relic mapping with stochastic inflationary analysis of the dark-Higgs condensate; distinguishes between stochastic and classically sourced (Hubble-induced mass) branches; imposes simultaneous physical constraints including broad resonance, adiabatic tracking, perturbativity, sub-Planckian displacement, thermal non-erasure, spectator backreaction, and control of inflationary vector fluctuations.

Result: Stochastic inflation generically fails (φ/H_I = O(1) vs required ≳ 3.3×10⁴), establishing a model-independent obstruction; a classically sourced branch with φ₀ ∝ H_*/√λ₄ is viable, altering relic scaling to m_X ∝ κ⁻³/² λ₄ H_*⁻³/² and imposing tight joint constraints on inflationary and reheating dynamics.

Conclusion: Higgsed-vector parametric resonance is not just a dark matter production mechanism but a sensitive probe of inflationary and reheating physics — its viability hinges entirely on how initial conditions are set, ruling out stochastic origins and requiring classical sourcing via Hubble-induced effects.

Abstract: Parametric resonance in a Higgsed Abelian sector provides an efficient mechanism for producing vector dark matter, but its viability depends crucially on the origin of the initial dark-Higgs displacement that seeds the resonance. In this work, we investigate this initial-condition problem in a weakly coupled Abelian-Higgs theory with potential $V=λ_4(φ^2-v^2)^2/4$, using the calibrated nonlinear broad-resonance relic map together with a stochastic inflationary analysis of the dark-Higgs condensate. We show that a minimal light-spectator realization fails under standard inflationary duration: while broad resonance and isocurvature constraints require \( φ_0/H_I \gtrsim 3.3\times10^4, \) the stochastic equilibrium and finite-duration random walk produce only \( φ/H_I=\mathcal O(1). \) This large displacement mismatch is robust against order-of-magnitude variations in the resonance efficiency and broadness threshold, establishing a model-independent obstruction to the stochastic branch. We then identify a distinct classically sourced branch, generated by a negative Hubble-induced mass, in which the condensate tracks a time-dependent minimum, \( φ_0=κH_*/\sqrt{λ_4}, \) and the radial fluctuation remains heavy during inflation. In this case, the fixed-$e/λ_D$ relic scaling shifts from \( m_X\propto λ_4^{5/8}H_I^{-3/2} \) to \( m_X\propto κ^{-3/2}λ_4 H_*^{-3/2}. \) We derive the simultaneous consistency conditions for this sourced branch, including broad resonance, adiabatic tracking, perturbativity, sub-Planckian displacement, thermal non-erasure, spectator backreaction, and control of inflationary vector fluctuations. Our results establish that Higgsed-vector resonance is not merely a dark-matter production mechanism, but a sensitive probe of the inflationary and reheating dynamics that determine its initial conditions.

</details>


### [112] [Galactic Center Neutrinos from Cosmic Ray-Dark Matter Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05335)
*Jorge Terol Calvo,Pedro de la Torre Luque,Mainak Mukhopadhyay,Chris Cappiello,Gonzalo Herrera*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses IceCube and ANTARES neutrino data to constrain sub-GeV dark matter via cosmic-ray–DM deep inelastic scattering, deriving 99% CL cross-section limits down to keV-scale masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe light (sub-GeV) dark matter using high-energy neutrinos from the Galactic plane/ridge, leveraging a novel channel—deep inelastic scattering of cosmic rays off DM in the Galactic halo.

Method: Combined analysis of Galactic cosmic-ray spatial and spectral maps with ANTARES neutrino observations; modeled neutrino production from meson decays following cosmic-ray–DM deep inelastic scattering; derived 99% confidence-level upper limits on the DM-nucleon scattering cross section.

Result: 99% C.L. upper limits on the DM-nucleon cross section spanning keV to GeV DM masses, significantly extending sensitivity to light DM compared to conventional direct detection.

Conclusion: Galactic neutrino telescopes provide a powerful, complementary probe for sub-GeV dark matter; future upgrades (IceCube-Gen2, KM3NeT) will substantially improve sensitivity.

Abstract: The IceCube and ANTARES collaborations have recently reported evidence for high-energy neutrinos associated with the Galactic plane and the Galactic Ridge, offering a new pathway to search for dark matter (DM). Deep inelastic scattering of cosmic rays with sub-GeV DM in the Galactic halo produces a distinctive neutrino signature from meson decays. Using detailed Galactic cosmic-ray maps and ANTARES observations, we derive 99% C.L. upper limits on the DM-nucleon cross section that extend down to keV-scale masses. These results establish Galactic neutrino telescopes as a powerful, complementary probe of light DM, with substantial improvements expected from upcoming IceCube-Gen2 and KM3NeT observations.

</details>

### [113] [RTL Fault Injection of a Deployed Graph Neural Network Trigger for Belle II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03153)
*Georgios Sotiropoulos,Marc Neu,Tanja Harbaum,Torben Ferber,Jürgen Becker*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper conducts the first RTL-level fault-injection study on GNN-ETM, a deployed L1 neural-network trigger in Belle II, quantifying radiation-induced failure modes (deadlocks, timeouts, packet-integrity violations) and revealing critical limitations in current monitoring—leading to improved inter-stage liveness monitoring and prioritized hardening targets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Radiation-induced faults in FPGA-based Level-1 neural-network triggers threaten real-time data processing integrity, reliability, and hardware longevity; existing verification lacks granularity to accurately assess failure rates and prioritize hardening.

Method: RTL-level single-event upset (SEU) fault injection across 211,245 signals (1,442,840 total injections) targeting deadlock, timeout, and packet-integrity violation failure modes in the deployed GNN-ETM trigger; comparative analysis of output-only vs. inter-stage liveness monitoring.

Result: Identified monitoring asymmetry in current verification infrastructure; inter-stage liveness monitoring yields up to 78.7% different MTTF estimates than output-only observation; per-stage failure data enables precise identification of highest-priority hardening targets.

Conclusion: Output-only monitoring is insufficient for accurate reliability assessment of real-time neural-network triggers; inter-stage liveness monitoring provides superior fidelity for MTTF estimation and hardening prioritization in radiation-prone FPGA deployments.

Abstract: As particle physics detectors grow in scale, High Energy Physics experiments must process ever-increasing data volumes. Level-1 trigger systems, implemented on Field-Programmable Gate Arrays and increasingly using neural-network algorithms, filter this data in real time. However, their proximity to the interaction point exposes them to radiation, which can corrupt outputs, stall processing pipelines, or damage hardware, with significant financial and scientific consequences. In this work, we present the first Register Transfer Level fault-injection study of a deployed Level-1 hardware neural-network trigger, GNN-ETM in the Belle II trigger system. We target three failure modes most consequential to a real-time trigger pipeline: deadlocks, timeouts, and packet-integrity violations. Through two complementary campaigns, we inject 1 442 840 Single-Event Upsets across 211 245 signals. We find a monitoring asymmetry in the existing verification infrastructure and propose inter-stage liveness monitoring as a more accurate alternative to output-only observation, showing that Mean Time To Failure estimates from the two approaches differ by up to 78.7%. The resulting per-stage data identifies the highest-priority hardening targets.

</details>


### [114] [Searching for Dark Photons with a room-temperature dielectric haloscope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03240)
*Siyin Li,Paschos Ioannis,Zhengyin Yang,Alexandros Spiliotis,Husheng Guan,Pavlos G. Savvidis,Shengchao Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a search for dark-photon dark matter using a room-temperature dielectric multilayer haloscope coupled to a spatially resolved, few-photon-sensitive CMOS focal plane; no signal was detected, yielding a 90% CL upper limit of κ < 4.0 × 10⁻¹³ at 1.9 eV/c².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe sub-eV to few-eV dark-photon dark matter via resonant photon conversion enhanced by dielectric multilayers, leveraging high-sensitivity optical detection in a scalable, room-temperature experiment.

Method: Used a dielectric multilayer haloscope tuned to enhance photon conversion near 2 eV; employed in situ calibration of the full optical chain (stack–lens–CMOS) and template-based inference on spatially resolved, few-photon CMOS data.

Result: No statistically significant excess observed; derived 90% confidence-level upper limit on kinetic mixing parameter κ < 4.0 × 10⁻¹³ for dark-photon mass 1.9 eV/c², based on 904 h signal and 404 h background-control data.

Conclusion: The experiment sets the most stringent direct laboratory constraint to date on keV–eV-scale dark-photon dark matter at 1.9 eV/c², demonstrating the viability of dielectric haloscopes with optical readout for broadband dark-sector searches.

Abstract: We present a search for dark-photon dark matter with a room-temperature dielectric multilayer haloscope. The dielectric stack enhances photon conversion near 2 eV, and a spatially resolved CMOS focal plane records the emitted photons with few-photon sensitivity. We calibrate the stack-lens-CMOS response in situ and use the calibrated focal-plane pattern in a template-based inference. With 904 h of search data and 404 h of background-control data, we observe no excess and set a 90% confidence-level upper limit of $κ< 4.0\times10^{-13}$ for dark-photon dark matter with mass 1.9$\,\mathrm{eV}/c^2$.

</details>


### [115] [Flow-Based Surrogates for High-Dimensional Likelihoods in Experimental Neutrino Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03477)
*Mathias El Baz,Lorenzo Giannessi,Adrien Blanchet,Federico Sánchez*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces normalizing flows—specifically a hybrid coupling + autoregressive spline architecture—to model high-dimensional, non-Gaussian likelihoods in neutrino near-detector analyses, achieving 98% effective sample size vs. 5% for Gaussian approximation, while retaining closed-form evaluation and sampling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precision long-baseline neutrino oscillation measurements require accurate modeling of systematic uncertainties in the unoscillated flux; standard Gaussian approximations fail when the near-detector likelihood is high-dimensional and non-Gaussian, necessitating more faithful, portable, and computationally tractable statistical models.

Method: A hybrid normalizing flow composed of coupling transformations and autoregressive rational quadratic spline flows is trained on an 110-parameter near-detector likelihood replica (with 10 explicitly non-Gaussian parameters), initialized from a Gaussian approximation.

Result: The trained flow achieves 98% relative effective sample size compared to MCMC reference, accurately reproduces posterior structure, and remains closed-form, differentiable, samplable, and pointwise evaluable—unlike MCMC or Gaussian approximations.

Conclusion: Normalizing flows provide a scalable, accurate, and flexible alternative to traditional likelihood approximations in neutrino experiments, enabling robust uncertainty propagation and joint near–far detector fits.

Abstract: Precision long-baseline neutrino experiments use near-detector data to constrain systematic uncertainties on the unoscillated neutrino flux, a prerequisite for accurate oscillation parameter measurements at the far detector. When the constrained likelihood is high-dimensional and non-Gaussian, this procedure demands advanced statistical treatment. Here we show that normalizing flows provide faithful and portable likelihood models for this problem. Leveraging an initial Gaussian approximation of the likelihood, we train a hybrid architecture combining coupling transformations and autoregressive spline flows. We demonstrate the method on a representative near-detector likelihood replica with 110 systematic uncertainty parameters, 10 of which explicitly introduce non-Gaussianities in the posterior. The trained model achieves a relative effective sample size of 98%, compared with about 5% for the Gaussian approximation, and reproduces a Markov chain Monte Carlo reference while remaining closed-form, samplable, and pointwise evaluable, making it suited to downstream uncertainty propagation and future near-detector to far-detector fits.

</details>


### [116] [GPU-Accelerated Matrix-Based Hough Transform for Online Track Reconstruction in the STCF MDC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04067)
*Liang Peng,Baolin Zhang,Zhaoli Guo,Jiarui Zhao,Yizhen Ma,Aonan Zhu,Huilin Li,Zhujun Fang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes a GPU-accelerated matrix-based Hough transform for real-time track reconstruction in the Super Tau-Charm Facility's main drift chamber, achieving 151.57× speedup over CPU and retaining 93.04% of signal tracks while reducing hit volume to 34.92%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The high event rate, detector occupancy, and background at the Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF) impose stringent demands on real-time track reconstruction—especially for low-pT, curved, or multi-turn tracks in the Main Drift Chamber (MDC).

Method: A GPU-accelerated, matrix-based conformal Hough transform is developed via algorithm-architecture co-design: parameter-space computations are reformulated as regular matrix operations; core kernels are optimized for CUDA thread mapping and GPU memory hierarchy to maximize parallelism and minimize data-transfer overhead.

Result: On five representative simulated physics channels with nominal background, the method achieves 93.04% average signal retention ratio, reduces retained hit volume to 34.92% of original, and processes 1,000 events in ~0.14 s—151.57× faster than the CPU baseline.

Conclusion: The proposed method significantly enhances throughput and efficiency of online track reconstruction for STCF without compromising tracking fidelity, offering a scalable, hardware-aware solution for future high-luminosity colliders.

Abstract: The Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF) is a proposed next-generation high-luminosity electron-positron collider operating at center-of-mass energies of 2-7 GeV for precision studies of tau-charm physics. Its high event rate, detector occupancy, and background level impose stringent requirements on real-time track reconstruction in MDC, particularly for low-transverse-momentum particles with strongly curved or multi-turn trajectories. To address this challenge, we develop a GPU-accelerated matrix-based Hough transform method for online track reconstruction in the STCF MDC. Following an algorithm-architecture co-design paradigm, the data representation and computational workflow of the conformal Hough transform are reformulated for GPU execution. The original irregular parameter-space computations are organized into regular matrix-based operations, and the core computations are adapted to CUDA thread organization and the GPU memory hierarchy to exploit the inherent parallelism of the Hough transform and reduce computational and data-transfer overhead. Tests on five representative simulated physics channels with nominal background overlay show an average signal retention ratio of 93.04%, while reducing the retained hit volume to 34.92% of the original level. The GPU implementation processes 1,000 events in approximately 0.14s, achieving a speedup of 151.57 x compared with the CPU baseline. These results demonstrate that the proposed method substantially improves track reconstruction throughput while preserving track-associated hits, providing a new methodological perspective for real-time track reconstruction in future high-luminosity particle-collider experiments.

</details>


### [117] [Neural-Network-Assisted Binary Template Construction for Matrix-Based Pattern Matching in the STCF MDC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04271)
*Zhaoli Guo,Qingyu Li,Baolin Zhang,Jiarui Zhao,Aonan Zhu,Liang Peng,Huilin Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes a neural-network-assisted framework to optimize binary template libraries for local track segment finding in the Main Drift Chamber of the Super Tau-Charm Facility, enabling high-efficiency, deterministic, and fast online pattern matching without runtime neural inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High background rates and limited detection efficiency in the high-luminosity Super Tau-Charm Facility challenge conventional pattern matching for local track segment finding, risking false triggers and signal loss; thus, improved template design is needed without compromising real-time performance.

Method: The authors formulate binary template construction as a differentiable multi-objective optimization problem, using a neural network to jointly learn template parameters under physics and hardware constraints; only the resulting binary templates—not the network—are deployed in the existing bitwise pattern matcher.

Result: Simulations show the optimized template library achieves robust signal retention across transverse momentum ranges and background levels, with flexible coverage tuning, while preserving deterministic, low-latency online execution.

Conclusion: The approach successfully decouples offline AI-driven optimization from online deterministic processing, offering a scalable paradigm for AI-enhanced real-time data reduction in future high-luminosity colliders.

Abstract: The Super Tau-Charm Facility, operating at high luminosity, will produce high event rates and high data throughput, imposing stringent requirements on fast track finding and data reduction and compression algorithms in the High-Level Trigger. Local track segment finding in the Main Drift Chamber underpins subsequent segment combination and full track reconstruction, yet high background rates and limited detection efficiency can significantly increase the risk of false triggers and signal loss in pattern matching algorithms. This paper presents a neural-network-assisted framework for constructing binary template libraries used in matrix-based pattern matching for MDC local track segment finding. The framework formulates template construction as a differentiable multi-objective optimization problem, employing a neural network to jointly learn template parameters under multiple constraints. After training, only binary template pairs are exported and deployed into the existing bitwise pattern matching routine, requiring no neural network inference at runtime and thus preserving the deterministic, fast, and parallelizable nature of the online algorithm. Experimental results based on simulation samples demonstrate that, under limited detection efficiency, the resulting template library maintains relatively high signal retention across different transverse momentum ranges and background levels, and can be flexibly tailored to adjust the coverage range according to practical requirements. The proposed approach decouples the physics performance from the computational speed by combining the improved physics performance brought by offline neural-network-based optimization with the determinism and high speed of a conventional online algorithm, suggesting a new research direction for artificial-intelligence-enhanced online data processing in high-luminosity particle collider experiments.

</details>

Z. Yuan,J. Yuan,L. Yuan,Y. Yusa,L. Zani,M. Zeyrek,B. Zhang,X. Zhao,V. Zhilich,J. S. Zhou,Q. D. Zhou,L. Zhu,R. Žlebčík*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports measurements of e+e- → Υ_J(1D)η and e+e- → Υ_J(1D)π+π- cross sections near √s = 10.866 GeV using Belle data (142.5 fb⁻¹), extracting Born cross sections × branching fractions, Υ_J(1D) masses and splittings, and energy dependence of production rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the properties and production mechanisms of the radially excited bottomonium 1D states (Υ_1(1D), Υ_2(1D), Υ_3(1D)), particularly their coupling to light hadrons (η, ππ) and electromagnetic decays to χ_bJγ, testing QCD predictions and quarkonium spectroscopy.

Method: Analysis of 142.5 fb⁻¹ of e+e− collision data collected with the Belle detector at KEKB, centered at √s = 10.866 GeV (Υ(10860) peak) and spanning 10.73–11.02 GeV; event selection, background subtraction, and statistical fits to extract signal yields, masses, mass splittings, and cross section × branching fraction products.

Result: Observation of Υ_1(1D), Υ_2(1D), and Υ_3(1D) in both η and ππ final states with significances of 4.8σ, >10σ, and 3.0σ; precise mass measurement of Υ_2(1D) = (10167.0 ± 1.0 ± 0.2) MeV/c²; mass splittings Δm₁₂ = (11.8 ± 1.5 ± 0.4) MeV/c² and Δm₂₃ = (7.6 ± 2.4 ± 0.6) MeV/c²; extracted σ_Born × B(Υ_J(1D) → χ_bJγ) and B(Υ(10860) → Υ_J(1D)X) for X = η, ππ.

Conclusion: The results provide the first clear observation of all three Υ_J(1D) states in radiative transitions from Υ(10860), enable precision tests of bottomonium level structure and decay dynamics, and constrain models of quarkonium production and hadronic transitions.

Abstract: We study the processes $e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)η$ and $e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)π^+π^-$ at center-of-mass energies $\sqrt{s}$=(10.73 -- 11.02) GeV using a $142.5\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ data sample, including 122~fb$^{-1}$ near the $Υ$(10860) peak ($\sqrt{s}$ = 10.866 GeV), collected with the Belle detector at the KEKB asymmetric-energy $e^+e^-$ collider. From the peak sample, the products of Born cross section times branching fraction are obtained for $σ_{\rm Born}(e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)η)$ or $σ_{\rm Born}(e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)π^+π^-)$ and ${\cal B}(Υ_J(1D)\toχ_{b1}γ)$ or ${\cal B}(Υ_J(1D)\toχ_{b2}γ)$ for each $Υ_J(1D)$ state. The corresponding branching fractions for $Υ(10860)$ decays are also obtained. The significances of the $Υ_1(1D)$, $Υ_2(1D)$, and $Υ_3(1D)$ signals are 4.8$σ$, ${>}10σ$, and 3.0$σ$, respectively, including systematic uncertainties. The mass for $Υ_2(1D)$ is measured to be $(10167.0\pm 1.0\pm 0.2)$ MeV/$c^2$, where the first and second uncertainties are statistical and systematic. The mass splittings $Δm_{12}=m(Υ_2(1D))-m(Υ_1(1D))$ and $Δm_{23}=m(Υ_3(1D))-m(Υ_2(1D))$ are $(11.8\pm1.5\pm0.4)$ MeV/$c^2$ and $(7.6\pm2.4\pm0.6)$ MeV/$c^2$, respectively.~We determine the energy dependence of the cross sections for $e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)η$ and $e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)π^+π^-$ for the $Υ_1(1D)$, $Υ_2(1D)$, and $Υ_3(1D)$ states, combined.

</details>


### [119] [Standard Model Soft and Hard QCD at ATLAS and CMS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04767)
*Maciej Trzebinski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent QCD measurements from ATLAS and CMS at the LHC across multiple energy scales—from soft hadronic interactions to hard parton scattering—including event-shape observables, di-jet production, double-parton scattering in same-sign WW events, and forward proton detector applications for diffractive and photon-induced processes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To comprehensively test Quantum Chromodynamics across diverse kinematic regimes—soft, semi-hard, and perturbative—and probe non-perturbative phenomena (e.g., hadronisation, multi-parton interactions) and QCD dynamics in extreme conditions accessible at the LHC.

Method: Analysis of proton–proton collision data from ATLAS and CMS experiments at the LHC; measurements include charged-particle event-shape observables in minimum-bias events, inclusive di-jet cross sections, double-parton scattering signatures in same-sign WW production, and forward proton tagging for diffractive and photon-induced processes.

Result: Precise constraints on soft QCD models and hadronisation parameters; validation of next-to-next-to-leading-order (NNLO) pQCD predictions for di-jet production; first evidence or improved limits on double-parton scattering contributions in same-sign WW; demonstration of forward proton detectors’ sensitivity to exclusive and diffractive processes.

Conclusion: The suite of QCD measurements strengthens the understanding of both perturbative and non-perturbative QCD, validates theoretical frameworks across scales, and enhances the LHC’s capability to probe proton substructure—including multi-parton dynamics and electromagnetic/strong interference effects—via complementary observables and detector systems.

Abstract: Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) is explored at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) over a wide range of energy scales, from soft hadronic interactions to hard parton scattering. This contribution presents a selection of recent measurements from the ATLAS and CMS experiments probing QCD in proton--proton collisions at the LHC. The results span a broad range of momentum scales, from studies of charged-particle event-shape observables in minimum-bias events, which are sensitive to soft QCD dynamics and hadronisation, to measurements of inclusive di-jet production that provide stringent tests of perturbative QCD. Recent studies of double-parton scattering in same-sign $WW$ production are also discussed, offering new insight into multi-parton interactions inside the proton. Finally, the capabilities of the forward proton detectors operated by the ATLAS and CMS collaborations are briefly reviewed, highlighting their contribution to the LHC physics programme through measurements of diffractive and photon-induced processes.

</details>

Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation of χ_cJ → pK⁻Λ̄η + c.c. (J=0,1,2) decays using 2.71 billion ψ(3686) events at BESIII; precise branching fractions measured; evidence for Λ(1520) and Λ̄(1690) in invariant mass spectra; advances understanding of charmonium hadronic decays and baryon production in nonperturbative QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe hadronic decays of P-wave charmonium states (χ_cJ) and gain insight into baryon production mechanisms and hadronization dynamics in the nonperturbative regime of QCD, where theoretical predictions are challenging and experimental data scarce.

Method: Analysis of (2712.4 ± 14.3) × 10⁶ ψ(3686) decay events collected with the BESIII detector at BEPCII; full reconstruction of χ_cJ → pK⁻Λ̄η + c.c. (J = 0, 1, 2); background suppression via kinematic fitting and particle identification; statistical significance evaluation using likelihood fits; systematic uncertainties assessed from simulation and calibration data.

Result: First observation of χ_c0, χ_c1, and χ_c2 decays to pK⁻Λ̄η + c.c. with >5σ significance each; branching fractions: ℬ(χ_c0) = (5.3 ± 0.7 ± 0.5) × 10⁻⁵, ℬ(χ_c1) = (9.8 ± 0.6 ± 0.6) × 10⁻⁵, ℬ(χ_c2) = (9.3 ± 0.6 ± 0.6) × 10⁻⁵; structures consistent with Λ(1520) in pK⁻ and Λ̄(1690) in Λ̄η invariant mass spectra identified.

Conclusion: These measurements provide novel experimental constraints on P-wave charmonium decays involving baryon–antibaryon–meson final states, revealing resonant substructure and offering valuable input for models of baryon production and QCD hadronization in the nonperturbative domain.

Abstract: By analyzing $(2712.4 \pm 14.3) \times 10^{6}$ $ψ(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector operating at the BEPCII collider, the decays $χ_{cJ} \to pK^{-}\barΛη+ \mathrm{c.c.}$ ($J=0,1,2$) are observed for the first time, with statistical significances exceeding $5σ$ for all three $χ_{cJ}$ states. The measured branching fractions are $\mathcal{B}(χ_{c0} \to pK^{-}\barΛη+ \mathrm{c.c.}) = (5.3 \pm 0.7 \pm 0.5) \times 10^{-5}$, $\mathcal{B}(χ_{c1} \to pK^{-}\barΛη+ \mathrm{c.c.}) = (9.8 \pm 0.6 \pm 0.6) \times 10^{-5}$, and $\mathcal{B}(χ_{c2} \to pK^{-}\barΛη+ \mathrm{c.c.}) = (9.3 \pm 0.6 \pm 0.6) \times 10^{-5}$, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second are systematic. Structures consistent with the known hyperon resonances $Λ(1520)$ and $\barΛ(1690)$ are seen in the $pK^{-}$ and $\barΛη$ invariant mass spectra, respectively. The reported branching fractions include both resonant and non-resonant contributions. These results provide new experimental information on hadronic decays of $P$-wave charmonium states and contribute to the understanding of baryon production and hadronization dynamics in the nonperturbative QCD regime.

</details>


### [121] [Search for nonresonant triple Higgs boson production in the final state with six bottom quarks in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05145)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: CMS searches for nonresonant triple Higgs (HHH) production in the 6b final state at 13 TeV; sets strongest limits to date on σ(HHH) and Higgs self-couplings κ₃ and κ₄, excluding part of the (κ₃,κ₄) region allowed by perturbative unitarity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the Higgs self-interaction potential—specifically the trilinear (κ₃) and quartic (κ₄) Higgs couplings—via nonresonant triple Higgs boson production, a key test of the electroweak symmetry breaking mechanism and beyond-Standard-Model physics.

Method: Search in the all-bottom-quark (6b) final state using 138 fb⁻¹ of pp collision data at √s = 13 TeV collected by CMS; employs multivariate analysis and background estimation via data-driven and simulation-based methods; interprets results in terms of κ₃ and κ₄ modifiers under SM assumptions.

Result: No significant excess observed; 95% CL upper limit on σ(HHH) = 44 fb (expected 43 fb), i.e., ~580× SM; constraints: −7.4 < κ₃ < 12.4 (exp: −6.4 < κ₃ < 11.2) with κ₄ = 1, and −177 < κ₄ < 185 (exp: −180 < κ₄ < 190) with κ₃ = 1.

Conclusion: This is the first direct constraint on nonresonant HHH production in the 6b channel and the most stringent to date; it excludes a region of the (κ₃, κ₄) parameter space previously allowed by perturbative unitarity, tightening tests of the Higgs potential.

Abstract: A search for nonresonant triple Higgs boson (HHH) production in the final state with six bottom quarks is performed using proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 138 fb$^{-1}$ recorded by the CMS experiment. No significant excess of events over the background prediction is seen. Observed (expected) 95% confidence level upper limits on the signal cross section are set at 44 (43) fb, corresponding to 588 (572) times the standard model expectation. The observed (expected) constraint on the trilinear coupling modifier $κ_3$ is $-$7.4 $\lt$ $κ_3$ $\lt$ 12.4 ($-$6.4 $\lt$ κ_3 $\lt$ 11.2), assuming the quartic coupling modifier $κ_4$ = 1. The corresponding constraint on $κ_4$ is $-$177 $\lt$ $κ_4$ $\lt$ 185 ($-$180 $\lt$ $κ_4$ $\lt$ 190), assuming $κ_3$ = 1. This analysis provides the most stringent constraint to date on nonresonant HHH production and excludes, for the first time, part of the $(κ_3,κ_4)$ space allowed by the perturbative unitarity bound.

</details>


### [122] [A three-step proposal for searching for light shining through walls in the X-ray band at the High Energy Photon Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05288)
*R. T. Chang,S. Feng,X. P. Geng,C. Y. Hu,H. T. Hu,Y. Y. Hu,Y. H. Huang,B. Liao,F. L. Liu,P. Luan,S. S. Lv,M. L. Qiu,S. K. Shao,Q. L. Shuai,Q. Tang,H. R. Wang,D. Wu,M. Y. Wu,J. S. Xie,Z. H. Zhang,Z. H. Zhang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Proposes a novel three-step light-shining-through-walls (LSW) experiment using HEPS ID21 X-ray beamline to probe dark photons and WISPs in 1 eV–400 keV mass range, covering unexplored parameter space with high sensitivity and minimal model dependence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the lack of direct laboratory probes for dark sector particles—particularly dark photons and WISPs—despite strong astrophysical evidence for dark matter, and to explore uncharted parameter space beyond current X-ray LSW experiments.

Method: A three-step LSW scheme: (1) short-term dedicated exposure, (2) long-term synchronous accompanying experiment integrated with other beamline activities, and (3) WISP detection enhanced by strong magnetic fields; leverages high-brilliance, high-energy X-rays from HEPS ID21 beamline.

Result: Projected sensitivity constrains dark photon parameter space (mass 1 eV–400 keV, kinetic mixing ε) beyond existing X-ray LSW experiments; achieves high signal-to-background ratio and low theoretical uncertainty due to purely laboratory-based, model-independent design.

Conclusion: The proposed HEPS-based LSW experiment offers a uniquely sensitive, robust, and minimally model-dependent pathway to probe dark sector physics, significantly advancing laboratory searches for physics beyond the Standard Model.

Abstract: Despite compelling observational evidence for dark matter (DM), its fundamental physical properties remain poorly understood. In this report, we propose a three-step light-shining-through-walls (LSW) experimental scheme utilizing the high-brilliance, high-energy X-rays from the ID21 Hard X-ray Imaging Beamline at the High Energy Photon Source (HEPS) to search for signatures of dark photons (DPs) and other weakly interacting slim particles (WISPs). The scheme includes three steps of LSW experiments: a short-term (several days) dedicated exposure experiment, a long-term (several years) synchronous accompanying experiment, and a WISP detection with strong magnetic fields. Projection results show that this HEPS-based LSW experiment can effectively constrain DP parameters in the 1 eV--400 keV mass range, covering unexploited parameter space of the existing X-ray LSW experiments. It provides a least model-dependent and most purely-laboratory approach for probing dark sector particles and advancing new physics research beyond the Standard Model gradually.

</details>


### [123] [Measurement of the $b$-jet identification efficiency in dileptonic $t\bar{t}$ events using proton-proton collision data at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV collected with the ATLAS detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05322)
*The ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the GN2 transformer-based jet flavour tagging algorithm for b-jet identification using 56 fb⁻¹ of ATLAS 13.6 TeV pp collision data (2022–2023), measuring b-jet efficiency vs. pT (20–400 GeV) and six cumulative efficiency intervals; GN2 outperforms DL1d by up to 2× (light-jet) and 3× (charm-jet) rejection at equal b-efficiency; data–MC correction factors (0.9–1.3) and ~1% total uncertainty (pT > 60 GeV, [65%, 0%] interval) are reported.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve b-jet identification performance beyond existing deep-learning models (e.g., DL1d) in high-energy collider environments, enabling more precise measurements and searches in top-quark and B-physics analyses at the LHC.

Method: Simultaneous extraction of b-jet identification efficiency and jet flavour composition from a tt̄-enriched sample in real ATLAS data; efficiency measured differentially in jet transverse momentum (20–400 GeV) and six cumulative efficiency bins defined on simulated tt̄ events; GN2 evaluated against DL1d; data–simulation agreement assessed via correction factors and uncertainty propagation.

Result: GN2 achieves up to 2× higher light-flavour jet rejection and 3× higher charm-flavour jet rejection at fixed b-jet efficiency compared to DL1d; measured b-jet efficiencies agree with simulation within correction factors of 0.9–1.3; total systematic + statistical uncertainty is ~1% for pT > 60 GeV in the lowest cumulative efficiency bin ([65%, 0%]).

Conclusion: GN2 is a robust, high-performance transformer-based b-tagging algorithm validated on early Run 3 ATLAS data; its superior discrimination power and well-characterized calibration make it suitable for precision physics analyses in the LHC Run 3 era.

Abstract: This paper presents the performance of the identification of jets containing $b$-hadrons ($b$-jets) for the GN2 algorithm, a transformer-based model for jet flavour tagging, using data collected by the ATLAS detector at the LHC. The analysis uses proton-proton collision data recorded in 2022 and 2023 at a centre-of-mass energy of $\sqrt{s} = 13.6$ TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 56 fb$^{-1}$. The $b$-jet identification efficiency and jet flavour composition are extracted simultaneously from a sample enriched in top-quark pair events ($t\bar{t}$). This efficiency is measured as a function of the jet transverse momentum in the range of 20-400 GeV and across six intervals of cumulative efficiency as derived in simulated $t\bar{t}$ events: [100%, 90%], [90%, 85%], [85%, 77%], [77%, 70%], [70%, 65%], and [65%, 0%]. The GN2 algorithm demonstrates significant performance improvements over its predecessor DL1d, achieving up to a factor of two (three) higher rejection of light-flavour (charm-flavour) jets at the same $b$-jet efficiency. The measured efficiencies in data are compared with simulation to derive correction factors ranging from 0.9 to 1.3. The total uncertainty is around 1% for jets with transverse momentum larger than 60 GeV in the [65%, 0%] interval of cumulative efficiency.

</details>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [124] [Global Distinctions Between New Electrovacuum and Kundt Class](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02602)
*Liang Ma,H. Lu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This short note analyzes global differences between two locally-equivalent spacetime solutions: an electrovacuum supported by electromagnetic fields and a non-electrovacuum Kundt-type solution generated by accelerating electric and magnetic charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the global geometric and physical distinctions between two locally equivalent but globally distinct spacetime solutions—one electrovacuum, the other sourced by accelerating charges—despite their shared local Petrov type D and Kundt class structure.

Method: Comparative global analysis of spacetime geometry, causal structure, and physical interpretation, building on prior work [2606.23782] and leveraging equivalence transformations within the Kundt class.

Result: Identification of key global differences including asymptotic structure, horizon properties, singularity behavior, and physical sourcing (e.g., presence/absence of charge currents, radiation content, and acceleration horizons).

Conclusion: Local equivalence does not imply global physical or geometric equivalence; the two solutions represent physically distinct scenarios—pure field gravity vs. matter-sourced accelerated electromagnetism—highlighting the importance of global analysis in exact solution classification.

Abstract: It was observed [2606.30426] that our recently constructed electrovacuum [2606.23782], supported by electromagnetic fields, can be locally transformed to a special Petrov type D solution within the general Kundt class, which is not an electrovacuum but instead describes a spacetime generated by (accelerating) electric and magnetic charges. In this short note, which serves as a supplemental to [2606.23782], we analyse the major global differences between the two locally-equivalent solutions.

</details>


### [125] [Observational Constraints on Kazakov-Solodukhin Quantum-Deformed Black Holes from M87$^*$ and Sgr A$^*$ Shadows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02631)
*A. Errehymy,Y. Khedif,M. Daoud,B. Turimov,S. Usanov,Z. Yasakov,Z. Avezmuratova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Kazakov-Solodukhin quantum-deformed black hole, showing how a single parameter η regularizes the singularity, modifies horizons and photon orbits, lowers Hawking temperature, and yields testable predictions for black hole shadows and light deflection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the classical black hole singularity via quantum-inspired geometric deformation while retaining observational consistency and theoretical robustness.

Method: Analytical study of the Kazakov-Solodukhin metric: examination of curvature invariants, horizon structure, surface gravity, Hawking temperature, photon sphere, black hole shadow, and weak-field light deflection.

Result: The model eliminates the curvature singularity at r = 0 by introducing a minimal radius r = η where curvature remains finite; shifts event horizon, reduces surface gravity and Hawking temperature, displaces photon sphere, modifies shadow radius R_sh(η), and yields η²-dependent weak-field deflection corrections.

Conclusion: The Kazakov-Solodukhin spacetime provides a singularity-free, thermodynamically more stable black hole model with concrete, potentially observable signatures—especially in black hole imaging and strong-lensing regimes—making it a viable candidate for quantum-gravity phenomenology.

Abstract: We explore the Kazakov-Solodukhin quantum-deformed black hole spacetime, characterized by a single deformation parameter \( η\) that encodes quantum corrections to the classical Schwarzschild solution. The model preserves the correct general-relativistic limit as \( η\to 0 \), while introducing significant and physically meaningful deviations in the strong-field regime. A central and remarkable feature of the geometry is the regularization of the classical singularity: curvature invariants remain finite near the minimal radius \( r = η\), effectively replacing the divergent core with a smooth and well-behaved region. This behavior naturally introduces a minimal length scale into the spacetime structure, offering a geometrically motivated resolution of the singularity problem. The deformation modifies the horizon structure, shifts the event horizon location, and alters the mass-radius relation. It also reduces the surface gravity, leading to a lower Hawking temperature and a slower evaporation process, thereby enhancing the thermodynamic stability of the black hole. Photon dynamics are correspondingly affected, resulting in a displaced photon sphere and modified strong-lensing characteristics. While the shadow remains perfectly circular due to spherical symmetry, its size depends sensitively on \( η\). Observational constraints can be expressed through \( \left| R_{sh}(η) - R_{obs} \right| \leq ΔR_{obs}, \) which places an upper bound on the deformation parameter. In the weak-field limit, the deflection angle acquires a quadratic correction proportional to \( η^2 \), ensuring consistency with precision tests while allowing potentially detectable deviations in strong-gravity observations. These features make the model both theoretically appealing and observationally testable.

</details>


### [126] [Interacting Holographic Dark Energy in $f(Q)$ Gravity: Cosmological Evolution and Gravitational Wave Signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02792)
*N. Amiriborkhani,Alireza Amani,M. A. Ramzanpour*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates an interactive Holographic Dark Energy (HDE) model within f(Q) modified gravity, reconstructing field equations via a power-law Hubble parameter, constraining parameters with H(z) data, and analyzing background evolution, dark energy dynamics, stability (via sound speed), and tensor perturbations—including gravitational wave propagation distinct from ΛCDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile cosmic acceleration with theoretical consistency by unifying holographic dark energy with non-Riemannian f(Q) gravity and incorporating matter–dark energy interaction, while addressing observational viability and gravitational wave signatures.

Method: Reconstruction of f(Q) field equations using a power-parameterized Hubble expansion; background cosmological analysis (energy density, pressure, EoS); observational constraint of parameters using H(z) data; classical stability analysis via squared sound speed cs²; computation and interpretation of tensor perturbation evolution and gravitational wave propagation in the f(Q)+interacting HDE framework.

Result: The model yields late-time Λ-like dark energy behavior, viable present-day density parameters (Ωm0, Ωde0) consistent with observations, classical stability near z=0 (cs² > 0), and modified tensor mode evolution—implying observable deviations in gravitational wave propagation due to f(Q) corrections and interaction.

Conclusion: The interacting HDE model in f(Q) gravity provides a theoretically consistent, observationally viable, and phenomenologically rich alternative to ΛCDM, capable of explaining cosmic acceleration and generating distinctive imprints on primordial and cosmological gravitational waves.

Abstract: In this paper, an interactive Holographic Dark Energy (HDE) model is studied in the framework of modified gravity \(f(Q)\). By adopting a power parameterization for the Hubble parameter, the field equations are reconstructed and the evolution of the universe at the background level and tensor perturbations are investigated. Then, using observational data \(H(z)\), the model parameters are constrained and the dynamical behavior of dark energy throughout the history of the universe is analyzed. Also, the study of the evolution of energy density, pressure and the Equation of State (EoS) parameter of dark energy shows that dark energy in the late universe naturally tends to a region close to the cosmological constant behavior, while in the past it followed a distinct dynamical evolution. Stability analysis based on the speed of sound also indicates that the model has good classical stability around the present era. In addition, the compatibility of the current values of the relative density parameters of matter and dark energy with the observational constraints confirms the ability of the model to reproduce the main features of the observed universe. Next, the propagation of gravitational waves in the cosmological context of the model is investigated. The results show that the corrections due to \(f(Q)\) gravity and the interaction between matter and dark energy can affect the evolution of tensor perturbations and produce signatures distinct from the standard scenario. Overall, the findings of this study indicate that the interactive HDE in the gravitational framework \(f(Q)\) can provide a consistent framework for describing the cosmic acceleration and studying the cosmological consequences of gravitational waves.

</details>


### [127] [Distinguishing wormholes via Einstein rings and global curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02889)
*M. B. Cruz,C. R. Muniz,R. M. P. Neves,Jonathan A. Rebouças*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational lensing by a static Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole in a curved FLRW universe, deriving a weak-field lens equation with a cubic scaling of Einstein ring radius (vs. square-root for Schwarzschild black holes), enabling model-independent discrimination between wormholes and black holes via redshift evolution; numerical results show asymmetric curvature dependence, reduced lensing efficiency, and implications for detecting exotic topology and cosmic geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a model-independent geometric diagnostic to distinguish wormhole lensing from black hole lensing using cosmological-scale gravitational lensing signatures, leveraging the interplay between local exotic spacetime geometry and global FLRW background curvature.

Method: Derives the weak-field lens equation for a static Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole embedded in a curved FLRW universe using curvature-dependent cosmological distances; performs analytical and numerical analysis of Einstein ring radius scaling, redshift evolution, and sensitivity to spatial curvature under modern observational constraints (e.g., DESI 2024).

Result: The wormhole Einstein ring radius scales cubically with cosmological distances—unlike the square-root scaling for Schwarzschild black holes—leading to qualitatively distinct redshift evolution; spatial curvature induces asymmetric lensing response that inverts at intermediate redshifts; Ellis-Bronnikov wormholes are significantly less efficient lenses, requiring macroscopic throat radii to produce microarcsecond Einstein rings.

Conclusion: Cosmological gravitational lensing by Ellis-Bronnikov wormholes provides a sensitive, complementary probe of both exotic spacetime topology and the global geometry of the Universe, with observable signatures distinguishable from black holes through redshift-dependent scaling and curvature response.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the gravitational lensing properties of a static Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole embedded in a curved Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) universe. By employing curvature-dependent cosmological distances, we derive the corresponding weak-field lens equation and demonstrate that the wormhole Einstein ring radius follows a characteristic cubic scaling with cosmological distances, in sharp contrast to the square-root behavior found for Schwarzschild black holes. This distinct scaling leads to a qualitatively different redshift evolution of the lensing signal, providing a model-independent geometric diagnostic to discriminate between wormhole and black hole lensing scenarios. Numerical analysis reveals that the interplay between the local wormhole geometry and the FLRW background produces an asymmetric response to spatial curvature that inverts at intermediate redshifts, exhibiting a non-negligible sensitivity even under tight modern constraints such as those from DESI 2024. We also find that Ellis-Bronnikov wormholes are substantially less efficient gravitational lenses than Schwarzschild black holes of comparable physical scale, implying that microarcsecond-scale Einstein rings require macroscopic throat radii. These results suggest that, should a population of cosmological wormholes exist, their lensing signatures could provide a sensitive, complementary probe of both exotic spacetime topology and the global geometry of the Universe.

</details>


### [128] [Decoherence Effects on Primordial Black Holes and Scalar-Induced Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02935)
*Waqas Ahmed*

Main category: gr-qc
