TL;DR: This paper introduces Gaussian quantum discord as a diagnostic tool to characterize residual quantum correlations in the scalar perturbations responsible for primordial black hole (PBH) formation and scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs), distinguishing it from classical collapse criteria; it models decoherence via a Lindblad open-system approach, shows how decoherence suppresses scalar covariance and thereby modifies both PBH abundance and SIGW spectra—especially with a benchmark scaling Ω_GW^eff ≈ Q_dec² Ω_GW^class—and identifies SIGWs as the clearest observational probe of the quantum-to-classical transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a quantum-information–theoretic characterization of the quantum-to-classical transition of inflationary curvature perturbations that seed PBHs and SIGWs, moving beyond purely classical or Gaussian statistical descriptions.

Method: Models each (k,−k) mode pair as a two-mode Gaussian state; quantifies residual quantumness using Gaussian quantum discord; incorporates decoherence via a Lindblad master equation inducing a Gaussian loss channel on the scalar covariance matrix; derives analytical impact on PBH abundance (via collapse variance) and SIGW spectrum (via modified radiation-era convolution with decoherence factors Q_dec(ku)Q_dec(kv)).

Result: In the pure squeezed limit, quantum discord grows rapidly with squeezing and is thus trivially satisfied; nontrivial behavior arises only under decoherence-induced mixedness; decoherence suppresses scalar two-point covariance, reducing PBH abundance and scaling the SIGW energy density as Ω_GW^eff ≈ Q_dec² Ω_GW^class for narrow scalar peaks and slowly varying Q_dec; SIGWs exhibit the most sensitive imprint of quantum discord and decoherence.

Conclusion: Quantum discord and decoherence offer a controlled, observationally relevant framework to assess the quantum origin and classicalization of PBH-seeding perturbations; scalar-induced gravitational waves serve as the optimal probe for testing this quantum-to-classical transition.

Abstract: Primordial black holes (PBHs) form when large primordial curvature perturbations re-enter the Hubble radius and exceed the classical collapse threshold. These perturbations originate as quantum fluctuations of the inflationary vacuum, motivating a quantum-information description of the PBH-producing scalar sector. We develop a conservative extension of the standard PBH and scalar-induced gravitational-wave (SIGW) framework in which Gaussian quantum discord is used as a diagnostic of residual quantum correlations, not as a new PBH-formation criterion. We describe each \((\bm k,-\bm k)\) pair as a two-mode Gaussian state and show that, in the pure squeezed limit, discord grows rapidly with the squeezing parameter, so low discord thresholds are automatically satisfied for strongly squeezed modes. The nontrivial regime is the mixed state produced by decoherence. Using a Lindblad open-system description, we motivate a Gaussian loss channel for the scalar covariance matrix and distinguish the discord from a covariance-survival factor \(Q_{\rm dec}(k)\). If the decoherence channel suppresses the scalar two-point covariance, PBH abundance can be affected through the classical collapse variance, while the SIGW spectrum is modified more directly by the factors \(Q_{\rm dec}(ku)Q_{\rm dec}(kv)\) inside the radiation-era convolution. For a narrow scalar peak and slowly varying \(Q_{\rm dec}\), this gives the benchmark scaling \(Ω_{\rm GW}^{\rm eff}\simeq Q_{\rm dec}^2Ω_{\rm GW}^{\rm class}\). Thus quantum discord and decoherence provide a controlled way to characterize the quantum-to-classical transition of PBH-producing perturbations, with the clearest imprint appearing in scalar-induced gravitational waves.

</details>


### [129] [Black holes in f(R) theory of gravity with compact extra dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03034)
*Arkady Popov,Sergey Rubin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates static, spherically symmetric solutions in higher-dimensional $f(R)$ gravity with compact extra dimensions, deriving exact Schwarzschild–de Sitter–like 4D metrics and stabilized $n$-sphere radii; it identifies a tension between stabilization radius and observed cosmological constant, then resolves it via semiclassical vacuum polarization of nonminimally coupled quantum fields—yielding asymptotically flat Schwarzschild geometry and radially dependent effective Planck mass and extra-dimensional radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile higher-dimensional $f(R)$ gravity with observational constraints—particularly the smallness of the effective four-dimensional cosmological constant—while stabilizing extra dimensions in a physically viable way.

Method: Analytical derivation of exact static, spherically symmetric solutions in $D = 4+n$-dimensional $f(R)$ gravity with compact $n$-sphere extra dimensions; incorporation of semiclassical vacuum polarization from quantized, nonminimally coupled matter fields to modify the effective gravitational dynamics.

Result: An exact solution where the 4D metric is Schwarzschild–de Sitter and extra dimensions are stabilized at radius $L_0$, linked to $\Lambda_4 = (n-1)/L_0^2$; inclusion of vacuum polarization yields an asymptotically flat Schwarzschild limit with $L_0$ dynamically set by quantum vacuum expectation values; radial dependence of effective 4D Planck mass $M_{\text{Pl}}^{(4)}(r)$ and $L(r)$ is explicitly characterized.

Conclusion: Stabilization of extra dimensions in $f(R)$ gravity requires quantum corrections to suppress the otherwise too-large induced cosmological constant; semiclassical vacuum effects provide a natural mechanism for achieving phenomenologically viable 4D gravity, with scale-dependent effective parameters indicating emergent dimensional reduction.

Abstract: We study static, spherically symmetric solutions in $f(R)$ gravity within a $D = 4+n$-dimensional spacetime, where the extra dimensions form a compact $n$-sphere of constant radius. We derive an exact solution in which the four-dimensional part of the metric corresponds to the Schwarzschild - de Sitter metric, while the extra dimensions are stabilized at a constant radius $L_0$. A consistency condition relates the size of the internal space to the effective four-dimensional cosmological constant $Λ_4 = (n-1)/L_0^2$, which is generally too large to be compatible with observations. To overcome this issue, we include the vacuum polarization effects of quantized matter fields nonminimally coupled to curvature. Using the semiclassical approach, we obtain an asymptotically flat four-dimensional Schwarzschild solution as a limiting case, where the size of the extra sphere is determined by the vacuum expectation values of the quantum fields. Finally, we discuss the dependence of the effective four-dimensional Planck mass and the extra-dimensional radius on the radial coordinate.

</details>


### [130] [Conformal symmetries and MOTS stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03128)
*Abbas M. Sherif*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the stability and evolution of marginally outer trapped surfaces (MOTS) in spacetimes with a spacelike foliation, linking their stability to geometric properties of conformal Killing vector fields and energy conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the stability and dynamical behavior of marginally outer trapped surfaces (MOTS) — key objects in black hole physics and general relativity — under geometric and physical constraints such as energy conditions and symmetries encoded by conformal Killing vector fields.

Method: Analyzes the second variation operator of area for MOTS embedded in a spacelike foliation; uses geometric identities involving the divergence and normal components of past-pointing conformal Killing vector fields; applies energy conditions (e.g., dominant or null energy condition) to control curvature terms in the stability operator.

Result: Under mild energy conditions, a MOTS intersecting integral curves of a past-pointing conformal Killing vector field in its normal space is strictly stable and smoothly evolves into a spacelike horizon; sign conditions on the divergence of the vector field on the MOTS determine instability or strict stability — notably, negative divergence implies strict stability for spherical MOTS.

Conclusion: The presence and divergence properties of conformal Killing vector fields provide robust criteria for MOTS stability and horizon formation, reinforcing geometric rigidity and dynamical predictability in spacetimes admitting such symmetries.

Abstract: Let $\{Σ_t\}$ be a spacelike foliation of a spacetime $\mathcal{M}$, and suppose each $Σ_t$ is foliated by 2-surfaces $\mathcal{S}$ with spacelike unit normal in $Σ_t$. We show that under mild energy conditions, a MOTS $\mathcal{S}$ that intersects integral curves of past-pointing conformal Killing vector field lying in the normal space of $\mathcal{S}$ is strictly stable and evolves smoothly to a spacelike horizon. We also show that if the restriction of the divergence of the vector field to $\mathcal{S}$ is non-negative, $\mathcal{S}$ is unstable, and if negative and $\mathcal{S}$ is a 2-sphere, $\mathcal{S}$ must be strictly stable.

</details>


### [131] [Odd-parity ringdown gravitational waves of a spherically symmetric black hole with perfect fluid accretion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03231)
*Rikuto Ohashi,Yasutaka Koga,Hiroyuki Nakano,Kota Ogasawara,Chul-Moon Yoo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how steady spherical accretion of a perfect fluid affects odd-parity ringdown waves of a Schwarzschild black hole, showing that the imaginary-to-real frequency ratio isolates environmental effects while its time dependence encodes the accretion rate—enabling joint constraints on the black hole’s surroundings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Realistic black holes may be surrounded by matter, making spacetime dynamical and ringdown frequencies time-dependent; this work aims to isolate how accretion-induced background evolution affects gravitational wave ringdown signatures.

Method: Perturbative analysis to first order in accretion rate; time-domain computation of odd-parity metric perturbations on a dynamically growing Schwarzschild background; exploitation of decoupling between odd-parity matter and metric perturbations to define a tensorial wave mode.

Result: The ratio Im(ω)/Re(ω) is insensitive to black hole growth and redshift, depending only on the surrounding environment; the time derivative of ω encodes the accretion rate; both constitute measurable observables across multiple quasi-normal modes.

Conclusion: Measuring both the frequency ratio and its time dependence across multiple ringdown modes provides complementary, model-independent probes of the black hole’s near-horizon environment and accretion physics.

Abstract: The ringdown waves from a black hole offer a clean probe of strong-field gravity, but a matter distribution that may be present around a realistic black hole renders the background spacetime dynamical and the ringdown frequencies time-dependent. We study the odd-parity ringdown of a Schwarzschild black hole that grows through the dilute, steady, spherically symmetric accretion of a perfect fluid. Working to first order in the accretion rate, we compute the ringdown waveform directly in the time domain on this dynamical background. Since the odd-parity matter perturbation decouples from the metric perturbation, the wave mode can be described by a purely tensorial mode on the accreting background. In particular, the ratio of the imaginary to the real part of the frequency cancels both the secular variation caused by the growth of the black hole and the redshift factor, so that its deviation from the Schwarzschild value purely reflects the surrounding environment. The time dependence of the frequency, on the other hand, reflects the accretion rate and allows us to define a second observable tied to it. We argue that measuring these observables across multiple modes may provide significant information to constrain the surrounding environment of the black hole.

</details>


### [132] [Dark energy genesis: modeling dissipative effects in primordial cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03272)
*Pietro Pellecchia,Alejandro Perez,Salvatore Ribisi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a dissipative unimodular gravity model where a time-varying cosmological constant emerges from quantum gravity-induced dissipation, mediated by hidden 'quantum gravity defects' acting as an Ohmic bath for matter fields; it demonstrates how such dissipation can generate dark energy dynamically from an initially dark-energy-free early universe.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile emergent spacetime geometry in quantum gravity with observable late-time cosmic acceleration by modeling dissipative effects—potentially arising from Planck-scale discreteness—as a physical origin of the cosmological constant.

Method: Constructs an effective cosmological model within unimodular gravity, introduces a varying cosmological constant driven by dissipation, incorporates hidden degrees of freedom ('quantum gravity defects') as an effective thermal bath, and adopts an Ohmic bath formalism inspired by the Caldeira-Leggett model to derive a diffusion equation for matter energy density.

Result: The model yields a diffusion-driven growth of the cosmological constant, showing that even starting from zero dark energy, dissipative dynamics can produce a small positive Λ consistent with observed cosmic acceleration.

Conclusion: Dissipation induced by fundamental quantum gravity structures can serve as a viable dynamical mechanism for dark energy generation, offering a novel bridge between Planck-scale physics and late-time cosmology within a well-defined effective framework.

Abstract: In various approaches to quantum gravity, spacetime geometry is understood to emerge from more fundamental discrete structures at the Planck scale. As sometimes posited, their presence could lead to dissipative effects in the smooth effective sector. In this paper, we develop the idea of non-conservation in gravity, by introducing an effective cosmological model within unimodular gravity, in which a varying cosmological constant arises as a consequence of dissipation. We show that this requires to incorporate hidden degrees of freedom -- termed quantum gravity defects -- that act as an effective bath for the matter fields. To illustrate the viability of the framework, we study the case of an Ohmic bath inspired by the Caldeira-Leggett model for Brownian motion, leading to a diffusion equation for the matter energy density. The results show that, starting from a primordial universe with no dark energy, dissipation can account for the generation of a small positive cosmological constant.

</details>


### [133] [Reconstructing $f(R)$ gravity from Viaggiu Holographic Dark Energy under Hubble, Event Horizon and Granda Oliveros cutoffs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03308)
*Arushi Jhunjhunwala,Sayani Maity*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reconstructs f(R) gravity models using Viaggiu holographic dark energy (VHDE) with entropy-corrected cosmological horizons, derives explicit f(R) forms for multiple infrared cutoffs, and confirms their viability via cosmological dynamics, stability analysis, and local gravity constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometric explanation of late-time cosmic acceleration by reconstructing viable f(R) gravity models from the Viaggiu holographic dark energy framework incorporating dynamical horizon entropy corrections.

Method: Correspondence between VHDE energy density and curvature-induced energy density; reconstruction of f(R) for Hubble, future event, and Granda-Oliveros infrared cutoffs; graphical analysis of equation of state and deceleration parameter; stability analysis (ghost/tachyon) and local gravity constraints.

Result: Explicit f(R) models successfully reproduce the deceleration-to-acceleration transition; all reconstructed models satisfy stability conditions (no ghost or tachyonic instabilities) and are consistent with local gravity tests.

Conclusion: VHDE-inspired f(R) gravity constitutes a consistent, flexible, and observationally viable geometric alternative to explain late-time cosmic acceleration.

Abstract: This work explores the reconstruction of $f(R)$ gravity within the framework of newly proposed Viaggiu holographic dark energy (VHDE), which incorporates entropy corrections due to the dynamical nature of cosmological horizons. By implementing a correspondence between VHDE and curvature-induced energy density, we generate explicit forms of $f(R)$ for different infrared cutoffs, namely the Hubble horizon, future event horizon, and Granda Oliveros cutoff. The resulting models are analyzed graphically through the cosmological parameters such as the equation of state and deceleration parameter, revealing a successful description of the transition from decelerated to accelerated expansion. The viability of the reconstructed models is further examined through stability conditions and local gravity constraints, that yields the nature of the models as free from ghost and tachyonic instabilities and demonstrates VHDE-inspired $f(R)$ gravity model as a consistent and flexible geometric framework for explaining the late-time cosmic acceleration of the Universe.

</details>


### [134] [Black Hole Memory Burden and its Signatures in Gravitational Waves from Mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03560)
*Gia Dvali,Michael Zantedeschi,Sebastian Zell*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how black hole memory burden—information stored beyond classical expectations—affects gravitational wave signatures, showing it can exceed progenitor information content and shift quasinormal mode frequencies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how black hole memory burden (MB), i.e., information stored in a BH that modifies its classical dynamics, is determined by the collapsing source and how it manifests in observable gravitational wave signals.

Method: Analytical derivation of memory burden bounds for BHs formed via stellar collapse; examination of MB-induced modifications to quasinormal mode spectra; comparison with extreme cases like two-particle collision-formed BHs.

Result: BH memory burden can vastly exceed progenitor information content; two-particle collision yields maximal MB; MB induces measurable frequency shifts in BH quasinormal modes and thus in gravitational wave signals.

Conclusion: Gravitational wave observations can probe both fundamental BH information storage mechanisms and formation history through memory burden effects on waveform morphology.

Abstract: Swift memory burden (MB) implies that the information stored in a black hole (BH) can modify its classical dynamics when the BH is perturbed. This influences the gravitational waves (GWs) emitted during BH mergers. In this paper, we investigate how the BH memory load is determined by the features of the collapsing source. We show that the memory load can vastly exceed the information content of its progenitor. An extreme example is a BH formed in a two-particle collision, which exhibits maximal MB. We then derive bounds for BHs formed through stellar collapse and examine the impact of swift MB on BH quasinormal modes, quantifying the MB-induced frequency shift of GWs. These findings imply that GW observations probe the fundamental mechanisms of BH information storage as well as their formation history.

</details>


### [135] [Generalized Unruh temperature for dynamical thin-shell wormholes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03577)
*Francisco S. N. Lobo,Manuel E. Rodrigues*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper unifies two notions of effective temperature—local acceleration temperature and Hawking-like particle-creation temperature—in dynamical thin-shell wormholes, deriving a generalized Unruh identity for asymptotically exponential null-ray maps and providing explicit analytical results for Schwarzschild-Schwarzschild wormholes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile disparate thermodynamic interpretations—acceleration-based (Unruh-type) and quantum-particle-creation-based (Hawking-type)—in horizonless, dynamical spacetimes like thin-shell wormholes, extending the Hawking–Unruh connection beyond stationary black holes.

Method: Uses junction condition formalism for thin-shell wormholes; defines effective acceleration scale via averaged local surface gravity across both sides of the shell; computes peeling functions for null rays in each asymptotic universe to obtain particle-creation temperature; derives generalized Unruh identity under asymptotically exponential null-ray mapping; applies framework to Schwarzschild–Schwarzschild configurations (symmetric/asymmetric).

Result: Derives analytical expressions for Hawking-like spectra, renormalized quantum fluxes, and quasi-horizon limits; shows shell temperature divergence near quasi-horizon matches Tolman temperature divergence due to infinite gravitational blueshift; establishes equivalence of acceleration and particle-creation temperatures under exponential null-ray asymptotics.

Conclusion: Provides a unified thermodynamic interpretation of temperature in horizonless dynamical spacetimes, demonstrating that acceleration and quantum particle creation yield identical effective temperatures when appropriately averaged and asymptotically matched—thereby generalizing the Hawking–Unruh correspondence.

Abstract: We unify two independent notions of effective temperature in dynamical thin-shell wormholes: the local acceleration temperature of the throat and the Hawking-like particle-creation temperature governed by the peeling of null rays. The effective acceleration scale, obtained by averaging over both sides of the junction, defines the thermodynamic shell temperature, while peeling functions for each asymptotic universe yield an effective particle-creation temperature. For asymptotically exponential null-ray maps, we derive the generalized Unruh identity that equates these two temperatures. Applied to Schwarzschild-Schwarzschild wormholes, the formalism yields explicit analytical expressions for the Hawking-like spectra, renormalized quantum fluxes, and quasi-horizon limits in both symmetric and asymmetric configurations. The divergence of the shell temperature near the quasi-horizon is shown to mirror the divergence of the local Tolman temperature, both originating from the infinite gravitational blueshift at a horizon. These results provide a unified interpretation of acceleration, particle creation, and temperature in horizonless dynamical spacetimes, extending the conceptual link between the Hawking and Unruh effects beyond stationary black holes.

</details>


### [136] [Additional Observational Signatures of Asymmetric Thin-Shell Wormholes within 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03673)
*J. Song,X. G. Lan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the optical appearance of a 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet asymmetric thin-shell wormhole, finding distinctive extra photon rings and lensing bands—absent in black hole counterparts—that grow with Gauss-Bonnet coupling α and are tunable via mass ratio and throat radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish thin-shell wormholes from black holes observationally by identifying unique optical signatures arising from their asymmetric spacetime structure and Gauss-Bonnet gravity effects.

Method: Uses Visser's cut-and-paste construction to build the asymmetric thin-shell wormhole; computes photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter; analyzes effective potential and null geodesics; simulates images assuming an optically thin accretion disk as light source.

Result: Reveals additional photon rings and lensing bands not present in 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet black holes; shows ring size increases with α (opposite trend to black holes); demonstrates strong dependence of ring morphology on mass ratio and throat radius.

Conclusion: The exceptionally bright, α-enhanced extra photon rings serve as a robust observational criterion for identifying and characterizing asymmetric thin-shell wormholes in 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet gravity.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the optical appearance of a 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet asymmetric thin-shell wormhole. Using Visser's cut-and-paste construction, we determine the photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter for different values of the Gauss-Bonnet coupling $α$. We then investigate the effective potential and photon motion inside the wormhole spacetime. It is found that the effective potential, light ray paths, and azimuthal angle are closely tied to the mass ratio of the two spacetimes. Considering an optically thin accretion disk as the only light source, we find that the asymmetric thin-shell wormhole's images exhibit additional photon rings and lensing bands that are absent for a 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet black hole. Furthermore, the size of these extra rings increases with $α$, contrary to the black hole case. Such exceptionally bright rings provide a reliable criterion for distinguishing and characterizing a thin-shell wormhole spacetime. We also verify that the mass ratio and throat radius significantly tune the morphology of these extra photon rings.

</details>


### [137] [Equatorial periodic orbits and gravitational waveforms in Bardeen black holes surrounded by perfect fluid dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03714)
*Sohan Kumar Jha*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how dark matter (PFDM) and non-linear electrodynamics (Bardeen BH) jointly affect orbital dynamics—specifically MBO, ISCO, and periodic orbits—and gravitational waveforms from EMRIs using the Kludge method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the combined influence of dark matter and non-linear electrodynamics on black hole spacetime geometry and observable relativistic astrophysical phenomena, particularly orbital structure and gravitational radiation.

Method: Analytical computation of effective potential for equatorial geodesics; classification of periodic orbits via (z,w,v) taxonomy and rational frequency ratio q; parametric variation analysis w.r.t. magnetic charge g and PFDM parameter β; numerical Kludge modeling of EMRI gravitational waveforms.

Result: DM and NED parameters systematically shift MBO and ISCO radii; periodic orbit configurations depend sensitively on g and β; gravitational waveforms exhibit distinct morphological and frequency-domain signatures attributable to both PFDM and Bardeen NED.

Conclusion: Dark matter and non-linear electrodynamics leave discernible, separable imprints on black hole orbital kinematics and EMRI gravitational waveforms—enabling potential joint constraints on both physics beyond GR and dark matter models via future high-precision GW observations.

Abstract: To probe the interplay between dark matter (DM) and non-linear electrodynamics (NED), we consider the Bardeen black hole (BH) surrounded by perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM). We first compute the effective potential governing the particle trajectory, and then, by imposing suitable conditions on the potential, examine the effects of DM and NED on the marginally bound orbit (MBO) and innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO). In this study, we confine the particle's trajectory to the equatorial plane. We then investigate periodic orbits around the Bardeen BH surrounded by PFDM (BPFDM BH), considering the rational number $q$ associated with each periodic orbit. We use the $(z,w,v)$ taxonomy, which is widely used to systematically organize periodic orbits. We examine the variation of $q$ with energy and angular momentum, and also the variation of the angular momentum and energy required for a specific $(z,w,v)$ configuration with the magnetic charge $g$ and DM parameter $\b$. Finally, with the help of the numerical "Kludge" method, we examine gravitational waveforms emitted from EMRIs where the central supermassive BH is modeled as a BPFDM BH. Our study reveals distinct signatures of NED and DM on orbital dynamics and gravitational waveforms.

</details>


### [138] [Radiated Energy Spectrum, Radiated Angular Distribution and Non-linear Memory from the One-loop Gravitational Bremsstrahlung Waveform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03879)
*Donato Bini,Thibault Damour,Stefano De Angelis,Andrea Geralico*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes gravitational-wave observables (radiance, energy spectrum, angular distribution, and non-linear memory) from two-body scattering at post-Minkowskian order up to $G^4$ (7.5PN), building on a known one-loop frequency-domain waveform.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute physically observable gravitational-wave quantities—especially the non-linear memory effect—from high-precision scattering amplitudes in the post-Minkowskian framework, extending beyond leading-order results.

Method: Starts from the known one-loop ($G^2 + G^3$) frequency-domain waveform $h(\omega, \theta, \phi)$; derives spectral radiance $dE^{\rm gw}/(d\omega d\Omega)$, integrated energy spectrum $dE^{\rm gw}/d\omega$, and angular distribution $dE^{\rm gw}/d\Omega$ up to $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$; extracts multipole expansion of non-linear memory up to $\mathcal{O}(G^5)$ via angular distribution; performed in center-of-mass frame with inclusion of tree-level contributions.

Result: Complete expressions for $dE^{\rm gw}/(d\omega d\Omega)$, $dE^{\rm gw}/d\omega$, and $dE^{\rm gw}/d\Omega$ accurate to $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$; multipole expansion of non-linear memory extended to $\mathcal{O}(G^5)$; overall fractional accuracy reaches 7.5PN.

Conclusion: The paper provides the first complete computation of GW observables from scattering up to $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$, enabling precision tests of general relativity in the high-energy, weak-field regime and furnishing foundational input for waveform modeling and memory studies.

Abstract: The frequency-domain gravitational waveform emitted by the scattering of two non-spinning massive particles has recently been derived at next-to-leading, \textit{i.e.} one-loop, post-Minkowskian order, $h(ω, θ,φ) \sim G^2 + G^3$. Building on this one-loop-accurate frequency-domain gravitational waveform, we successively derive the spectral gravitational-wave (GW) radiance, $dE^{\rm gw}/(dωdΩ)$, the radiated GW energy spectrum, $dE^{\rm gw}/dω$, and the radiated GW angular distribution, $dE^{\rm gw}/dΩ$, up to order $G^4$ included. We deduce from the radiated angular distribution the multipole expansion of the non-linear memory up to order $G^5$ included, thereby extending previous results. We work in the center-of-mass frame, and our results reach the fractional 7.5PN accuracy. For completeness, we include the tree-level information (considered in the center-of-mass frame).

</details>


### [139] [Identifying lensed gravitational waves with physics-informed posterior learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03885)
*Tian-Yang Sun,Xiao Guo,Jing-Fei Zhang,Xin Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a physics-informed posterior learning method to distinguish gravitationally lensed gravitational-wave multi-image signals from unrelated multiple-merger events, leveraging geometric-optics consistency (e.g., preserved intrinsic phase evolution) and fusing simulation-trained posteriors with waveform features; it improves detection efficiency and lowers required SNR, demonstrating the value of physical consistency in ML-based GW catalog analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Identifying gravitationally lensed gravitational-wave events is challenging due to contamination from unrelated overlapping binary mergers; robust discrimination is needed to probe compact lenses, dark matter substructure, and cosmological distances.

Method: Physics-informed posterior learning: trains an approximate posterior on detector-frame chirp mass and symmetric mass ratio using generic multi-image simulations; enforces geometric-optics consistency (amplitude, time, Morse phase variability while preserving intrinsic phase evolution); fuses posterior samples with direct waveform features; evaluates on held-out lens models (point-mass, SIS, SIE, shear-perturbed).

Result: For BBH populations, fusion ranking increases detection efficiency from 20.8% to 35.2% at 1% false-positive rate; reduces required network SNR for 50% detection efficiency from 45.3 to 33.5 (1.35× larger SNR-equivalent distance); limitations arise from loud source-consistent unrelated mergers and calibration dependence on unrelated merger population.

Conclusion: Physical consistency—especially geometric-optics constraints—is a powerful prior for machine learning in dense gravitational-wave catalogs, enabling more reliable lensed-event identification and paving the way for astrophysical and cosmological inference via lensing.

Abstract: Gravitational lensing of gravitational waves can probe compact lenses, dark matter substructure, and cosmological distances, but identifying lensed events is difficult when unrelated binary mergers overlap in the same analysis window. We develop physics-informed posterior learning for ranking lensed multi-image signals against unrelated multiple-merger events. The method exploits the geometric-optics consistency that lensing can change amplitudes, arrival times, and Morse phase offsets while preserving the intrinsic phase evolution of the source. We infer a simulation-trained approximate posterior for the common detector-frame chirp mass and symmetric mass ratio, and fuse posterior samples with direct waveform features. Training uses generic multi-image simulations, while point-mass, singular-isothermal-sphere, singular-isothermal-ellipsoid, and shear-perturbed lenses are reserved for held-out lens-family evaluation. For the observationally motivated binary-black-hole population, the fusion ranking raises the detection efficiency from $20.8\%$ to $35.2\%$ at a $1\%$ reference false-positive-rate threshold calibrated on the corresponding unrelated multiple-merger sample. It lowers the network signal-to-noise ratio needed for $50\%$ detection efficiency from 45.3 to 33.5, which corresponds to a 1.35 times larger signal-to-noise-ratio-equivalent distance scale. The gain is limited by loud unrelated multiple-merger events that are partly source consistent, and by the need to calibrate the unrelated multiple-merger population. These results suggest that physical consistency can become a guiding principle for machine learning searches in dense gravitational-wave catalogs.

</details>


### [140] [Semiclassical regularity of compact trapped regions: From dynamical horizons to inner extremality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03916)
*Raúl Carballo-Rubio,Francesco Di Filippo,Stefano Liberati,Matt Visser*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes semiclassical stability of inner horizons in dynamical black-hole spacetimes, showing that finite-duration trapped regions avoid RSET singularities—unlike eternal black holes—and that inner-extremal geometries exhibit only power-law (not exponential) RSET growth, suggesting meta-stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between the pathological semiclassical behavior of inner horizons in eternal black holes and the physical expectation that realistic black holes form and evaporate in finite time.

Method: Computes the renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET) in the in-vacuum state within the s-wave Polyakov approximation for dynamical black-hole spacetimes modeling finite-time formation and evaporation of a compact trapped region.

Result: The RSET remains finite everywhere when the inner horizon is not a Cauchy horizon (i.e., in finite-evaporation scenarios); for non-extremal inner horizons, RSET grows exponentially at the inner horizon only asymptotically (infinite lifetime limit); for inner-extremal geometries, growth is power-law, implying significantly milder divergence.

Conclusion: Dynamical, finite-lifetime black-hole interiors—especially inner-extremal ones—are semiclassically well-behaved and may be meta-stable, offering viable alternatives to the singular inner-horizon picture of eternal black holes.

Abstract: In eternal black-hole spacetimes, inner horizons are Cauchy horizons and are generically unstable. For non-extremal inner horizons, this includes both the classical mass-inflation instability and a semiclassical instability associated with divergences in the renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET). Inner-extremal geometries, for which the inner-horizon surface gravity vanishes, evade classical mass inflation, but in stationary settings still suffer from singular behavior of the RSET. In this work, we show that the dynamical case is qualitatively different. Considering spacetimes describing the formation and evaporation of a compact trapped region in finite time, and working in the $s$-wave Polyakov approximation, we compute the expectation value of the stress-energy tensor in the in-vacuum state. Given that in this case the inner horizon is not a Cauchy horizon, the RSET remains finite everywhere. For generic non-extremal inner horizons, however, the RSET grows exponentially in time at the inner horizon, with a divergence emerging only in the asymptotic limit of an ever-lasting trapped region. For inner-extremal geometries this exponential growth is replaced by a considerably milder power-law growth. Such spacetimes may therefore be considered natural candidates for classically and semiclassically meta-stable black-hole interiors.

</details>


### [141] [On the structure of black hole interior in a model of scalar quasi-particles within the GR framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03938)
*S. Bondarenko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a singularity-free black hole interior model using a scalar field with non-linear self-interaction, structured in three layers (core, transition, crust), linked by Israel matching; it features an AdS-type negative-energy core with negative temperature, a thermal crust above a Schwarzschild horizon, and interprets black hole evolution as a quasi-static thermodynamic cycle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the black hole singularity problem and provide a physically consistent, singularity-free description of the black hole interior within classical general relativity, incorporating thermodynamic and quantum-inspired concepts such as negative temperature and quasi-particle condensation.

Method: Constructs a static, spherically symmetric scalar field model with a piecewise-defined potential having distinct extrema corresponding to crust, transition layer, and core; solves the Einstein-scalar equations exactly in each region; applies Israel junction conditions across the transition layer; derives thermodynamic relations including two-temperature formalism and linear stability analysis of the null-gravity hypersurface.

Result: Obtains explicit metric and mass functions for all three layers; identifies the crust’s edge equation of state; establishes linear stability criteria for the transition surface under both ordinary and effective negative-mass matter; formulates a two-temperature thermodynamics linking kinetic excitations (crust) and the negative-temperature condensate (core); shows Hawking evaporation and formation constitute a near-adiabatic thermodynamic cycle.

Conclusion: The model provides a coherent, singularity-free interior geometry unifying core and crust via a single scalar field, where the negative-energy AdS core with negative temperature acts as the working substance of a black hole thermodynamic engine—offering a novel geometric-thermodynamic perspective on black hole dynamics beyond semi-classical approximations.

Abstract: We propose an effective, singularity-free model of the black hole interior described entirely by a scalar field with a non-linear self-interaction potential. The interior consists of three layers -- a core, a transition layer, and a crust -- each fixed by the local quasi-particle density and the corresponding extremum of the potential of the field. The crust is a layer of massive, positive-energy thermal excitations above the zero-potential well, beneath a genuine Schwarzschild horizon at $r = 2GM_{\rm ADM}$. The core is an AdS-type region of negative energy density, it simulates a condensate of quasi-particles which carry zero classical kinetic energy and form a negative potential well governed by a negative inverse temperature parameter. The two regions are joined through Israel matching across the transition layer, which sits at an approximately null-gravity hypersurface of maximal regular matter density and where both the sign of the energy density and the type of thermal excitations change. Solving the static Einstein equations, we obtain the metric and mass functions of each layer, the edge equation of state of the crust, the linear stability condition of the null-gravity surface for ordinary and effectively negative mass matter, and the two-temperature thermodynamics linking the kinetic excitations to the negative temperature ground state. The framework unifies core and crust within a single field description and highlights the role of the negative energy AdS core and the associated negative temperature notion in describing black hole interior. In this picture the formation and Hawking evaporation of a black hole appear as a quasi-static cycle of an almost adiabatic thermodynamic engine, with the negative energy core as the working substance.

</details>


### [142] [Transient Dynamical Wormholes with Decaying Radial Energy Flux](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04041)
*Allah Ditta,Phongpichit Channuie*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs time-dependent traversable wormhole solutions in general relativity by introducing a time-varying shape function, linking geometry evolution to radial energy flux via off-diagonal Einstein equations; exact solutions feature a static background plus decaying transient term, satisfy standard wormhole conditions (throat, flaring-out, asymptotic flatness), exhibit diminishing null energy condition violation near the throat, finite total exotic matter at late times, and linear stability under small perturbations; the flux-driven model contrasts with cosmological-scale-factor-driven models and suggests null-fluid origins for the flux.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically motivated, time-dependent traversable wormhole solutions in general relativity—moving beyond static or cosmologically driven models—by directly coupling geometric evolution to local energy-momentum transport (radial flux), thereby offering a more realistic dynamical framework.

Method: Analytical solution of the Einstein field equations with a time-dependent shape function; identification of the off-diagonal G^t_r component as encoding radial energy flux; derivation of exact metrics combining static wormhole backgrounds with transient, time-decaying terms; verification of wormhole criteria (throat existence, flaring-out, asymptotic flatness); energy condition analysis (especially null energy condition) via stress-energy components; computation of volume integral quantifying total exotic matter; linear stability analysis via perturbation theory around the background solution.

Result: Exact time-dependent traversable wormhole solutions where geometry evolves due to radial energy flux; satisfaction of all standard traversable wormhole criteria; localized and diminishing null energy condition violation near the throat; finite and asymptotically constant total exotic matter; linear stability for suitable parameter choices; physical interpretation of flux as arising from null-fluid sources.

Conclusion: A flux-driven mechanism provides a simple, physically grounded framework for dynamical wormholes in general relativity, yielding stable, asymptotically static configurations with controlled exotic matter content—offering a viable alternative to cosmological-scale-factor-based models and potential applicability in extended gravity theories.

Abstract: We investigate a class of time-dependent traversable wormholes within the framework of general relativity by allowing the shape function to vary with time. In this setting, the evolution of the geometry is directly connected to a radial energy flux through the off-diagonal component of the Einstein field equations, providing a natural mechanism for non-static configurations. We obtain exact solutions in which the geometry consists of a static background supplemented by a transient term that diminishes with time. The resulting spacetime satisfies the standard conditions required for a traversable wormhole, including the presence of a throat, the flaring-out condition, and asymptotic flatness. An analysis of the energy conditions indicates that the null energy condition is violated in the vicinity of the throat, although the degree of violation decreases as the system evolves. This feature is examined further through a volume integral that measures the total amount of exotic matter, demonstrating that it approaches a constant value at late times. We also study the response of the system to small perturbations and find that, for a suitable choice of parameters, the configuration remains stable with perturbations decaying over time. We discuss how this flux-driven mechanism relates to the alternative and extensively studied approach in which wormhole evolution is instead carried by a cosmological scale factor, and we identify possible physical origins of the assumed radial flux in terms of null-fluid matter sources. Overall, the model describes a wormhole spacetime whose evolution is controlled by energy transport, leading to a gradual transition toward a static configuration. This framework offers a simple and physically motivated approach to dynamical wormholes and may be useful for exploring more realistic scenarios in both general relativity and extended theories of gravity.

</details>


### [143] [Emergent cosmology and gravity from quantum time?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05020)
*Ovidiu Cristinel Stoica*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes that macroscopic observables derived from stationary quantum states can recover intrinsic dynamics, and that interpreting the squared wavefunction amplitude as a probability density for intrinsic time induces spacetime curvature—even in Euclidean space—without assuming gravity; specifically, FLRW cosmology emerges from spherically symmetric stationary solutions in 4D Euclidean space via this intrinsic time interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether general relativity—or a modified version—can emerge from quantum stationary states and intrinsic time observables, without presupposing gravitational dynamics or a fundamental time direction.

Method: Interprets the squared modulus of a stationary wavefunction as a probability density over intrinsic time; analyzes geometric consequences—particularly curvature emergence—in Euclidean space using spherically symmetric stationary solutions; derives effective spacetime geometry (e.g., FLRW) purely from amplitude variation and macroscopic observables.

Result: Spacetime curvature—including Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker cosmology—emerges from the variable amplitude of stationary quantum states in four-dimensional Euclidean space, solely through the intrinsic time interpretation; no prior gravitational field or Lorentzian metric is assumed.

Conclusion: General relativity may be an effective description arising from intrinsic time observables in quantum stationary states, suggesting a novel route to quantum gravity where geometry and dynamics are encoded in the amplitude structure of stationary wavefunctions.

Abstract: Macroscopic observables allow the recovery of intrinsic dynamics from stationary quantum states. I show that, by interpreting the squared amplitude as the probability density for each definite value of intrinsic time, a curvature emerges in the time direction. For example, from the perspective of intrinsic quantum time, the Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker cosmological model emerges from spherically symmetric stationary solutions in four-dimensional Euclidean space, without presupposing gravity. If there is no unique direction of time, curvature emerges in all spacetime dimensions, without presupposing gravity, from the variable amplitude of the stationary wavefunction alone. This opens a new possibility that general relativity or some modification of it emerges from intrinsic time observables.

</details>


### [144] [Where Thermodynamics Meets Geometry: Critical-Radius Coincidences in Confining-NED Black Holes with Barrow Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04173)
*Erdem Sucu,İzzet Sakallı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a spherically symmetric black hole from Einstein gravity coupled to a nonlinear electrodynamics model incorporating quark–antiquark confinement, introducing a logarithmic correction (parameter ζ) to the Reissner–Nordström metric. It computes Hawking temperature via Dirac fermion tunneling, finds ζ suppresses small-horizon divergence and implies a remnant, incorporates quantum-gravitational effects via Barrow entropy (Δ), and performs extended-phase-space thermodynamics—revealing a quadruple critical coincidence at radius r⋆ where Hawking temperature peaks, heat capacity diverges, Joule–Thomson inversion occurs, and radial tidal force vanishes—all governed by A''(r⋆)=0; extremal horizon and angular tidal zero coincide at A'(rh)=0. EHT constraints yield ζ ≲ 0.7 for Q/M = 0.5.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how quark–antiquark confinement-inspired nonlinear electrodynamics modifies black hole structure, thermodynamics, and tidal behavior—particularly addressing UV-completion hints (remnants, entropy deformation), stability, critical phenomena, and observational viability with Event Horizon Telescope data.

Method: Constructs a static, spherically symmetric black hole solution with logarithmic confinement correction to RN metric; applies Hamilton–Jacobi tunneling method for Dirac fermions to compute Hawking temperature; incorporates Barrow entropy with deformation index Δ for quantum-gravitational corrections; performs extended phase-space thermodynamic analysis (internal energy, free energy, pressure, heat capacity, isothermal compressibility, Joule–Thomson coefficient); identifies critical points via geometric conditions on metric function A(r); maps tidal accelerations to thermodynamic curves; constrains ζ using EHT observations of Sgr A*.

Result: The logarithmic confinement term modifies horizon structure and near-singularity behavior; ζ suppresses Hawking temperature divergence and signals a remnant; Barrow entropy introduces Δ-dependent stability regions in heat capacity; no van der Waals criticality due to persistently negative isothermal compressibility; quadruple coincidence occurs at r⋆ satisfying A''(r⋆)=0, unifying thermodynamic and tidal criticality; extremal horizon and angular tidal zero coincide at A'(rh)=0; EHT data yields ζ ≲ 0.7 for Q/M=0.5, implying observable geodesic deviation corrections.

Conclusion: Confinement-motivated nonlinear electrodynamics leads to a geometrically and thermodynamically coherent black hole model with emergent remnants, unified criticality governed by simple curvature conditions on A(r), mechanical rigidity (no liquid–gas transition), and testable predictions for tidal effects and EHT observables—bridging nonperturbative QCD features with quantum black hole phenomenology.

Abstract: We study a static, spherically symmetric black hole obtained from Einstein gravity coupled to a nonlinear electrodynamics model with a quark--antiquark confinement interaction. The metric extends Reissner--Nordström by a logarithmic correction controlled by $ζ$, modifying both horizon structure and the near-singularity regime. The Hamilton--Jacobi tunneling method for Dirac fermions yields the Hawking temperature; the $ζ$-dependent terms suppress the small-horizon divergence and signal a remnant. Quantum-gravitational fluctuations are incorporated through Barrow entropy with deformation index $Δ$. Within the extended phase space we compute the internal energy, free energy, pressure, heat capacity, isothermal compressibility, and Joule--Thomson coefficient. The heat capacity locates $Δ$-dependent stability regions; the compressibility stays negative across the domain analysed here, marking a mechanically rigid phase with no van der Waals criticality in this branch. The central result is a quadruple coincidence: the peak Hawking temperature, the heat-capacity divergence, the Joule--Thomson inversion, and the zero of the radial tidal force all sit at one radius $r_\star$ defined by $A''(r_\star)=0$, while the extremal horizon and the angular tidal-force zero coincide via $A'(r_h)=0$. These reduce the full critical-point analysis to two scalar equations on $A(r)$. Geometric tidal accelerations are mapped against the thermodynamic critical curves. Event Horizon Telescope observations of Sgr~A* translate into a constraint $ζ\lesssim 0.7$ at $Q/M=0.5$, leaving a finite window open. The confinement term induces observable corrections to geodesic deviation.

</details>


### [145] [Tests of general relativity using analytic derivatives of parametrized post-Einsteinian gravitational waveforms within the Fisher-matrix framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04238)
*Jie Wu,Mengfei Sun,Jin Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives analytic waveform derivatives for Fisher-matrix-based tests of gravity beyond general relativity using the parametrized post-Einsteinian framework, enabling efficient and robust constraints on non-GR effects across detector types and source populations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precise, computationally stable, and scalable tests of gravity beyond general relativity using gravitational-wave observations—particularly in the strong-field, highly dynamical regime where deviations may manifest.

Method: Derives analytic expressions for frequency-domain inspiral waveform derivatives within the parametrized post-Einsteinian (ppE) framework; integrates them into the Fisher-matrix formalism; evaluates constraints across space-based (e.g., LISA), ground-based (e.g., Advanced LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA), and multiband detector configurations; combines with realistic binary black hole population models.

Result: Reveals systematic trends in sensitivity to non-GR parameters as functions of post-Newtonian order, detector type, and source population; demonstrates complementarity between space- and ground-based detectors—especially for low-frequency, accumulation-dominated ppE effects; achieves significant reduction in computational cost and elimination of finite-difference numerical systematics.

Conclusion: The analytic Fisher-matrix approach provides robust, efficient, and scalable forecasts for testing gravity with future GW observatories; it strengthens the scientific case for next-generation and multiband detector networks in precision fundamental physics.

Abstract: Testing gravity beyond general relativity (GR) is essential for probing fundamental physics in the strong-field and highly dynamical regime accessed by gravitational-wave (GW) observations. In this work, we derive analytic expressions for waveform derivatives in the Fisher-matrix formalism within the parametrized post-Einsteinian framework, using the frequency-domain inspiral waveform. These analytic derivatives enable stable and efficient Fisher-matrix calculations without relying on finite-difference schemes. We apply this method to a wide range of detector configurations, including space-based, ground-based, and multiband observations, and combine it with different binary black hole population models. Our results reveal clear and systematic trends in the constraints on non-GR effects as functions of post-Newtonian order, detector type, and source population. They also demonstrate the complementarity between space- and ground-based detectors, particularly for effects that accumulate during the low-frequency inspiral. The analytic approach substantially reduces computational cost and avoids numerical systematics associated with step-size choices, making it well suited for large-scale parameter studies. These results provide robust forecasts for the capability of future GW observations to constrain a broad class of non-GR effects and environmental influences, highlighting the scientific potential of upcoming detector networks for precision tests of gravity.

</details>


### [146] [Rigidity and the interpretation of mass with a positive cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04291)
*Eneko Aranguren,Raül Vera*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs explicit static stellar configurations in Schwarzschild-de Sitter spacetime showing that mass can be positive, zero, or negative under energy conditions—contradicting rigidity expectations from the Λ=0 positive mass theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the assumed rigidity of mass definitions in general relativity with positive cosmological constant Λ, where standard intuition from Λ=0 (e.g., positive mass theorem) may not hold.

Method: Constructs a family of regular, static, spherically symmetric perfect-fluid stellar solutions satisfying dominant and strong energy conditions, matched smoothly to Schwarzschild-de Sitter exteriors; analyzes mass parameter dependence on fluid equation of state and boundary conditions.

Result: Demonstrates existence of regular configurations with arbitrarily signed ADM-type mass parameter—including a zero-mass case yielding exact de Sitter exterior coexisting with nontrivial perfect-fluid interior—violating rigidity expected from Λ=0 analogues.

Conclusion: The rigidity principle underlying the positive mass theorem fails for Λ > 0: mass is not constrained to be non-negative under standard energy conditions in static spherically symmetric settings, revealing a fundamental qualitative difference between asymptotically de Sitter and asymptotically flat gravity.

Abstract: We provide an explicit counterexample to the rigidity properties underlying the interpretation of mass in the presence of a positive cosmological constant $Λ$. Specifically, we construct a family of regular static stellar configurations satisfying the dominant and strong energy conditions, and containing no surface layers, for which the mass parameter of the outer Schwarzschild-de Sitter region can be positive, zero, or negative. The zero-mass configuration is precisely the one for which the outer vacuum region becomes exactly de Sitter, yielding a spacetime in which a de Sitter domain coexists with regular perfect-fluid matter. This contrasts sharply with the $Λ=0$ case, where a Minkowski domain cannot coexist with perfect-fluid regions satisfying the energy conditions. These results show that the static, spherically symmetric realization of the rigidity principle associated with the positive mass theorem for $Λ=0$ does not carry over to $Λ>0$.

</details>


### [147] [Complete Quantum Stress Tensor Inside a Four Dimensional Schwarzschild Black Hole: A Divergent Focusing Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04386)
*Shun Jiang,Jie Jiang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the full renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET) of a massless scalar field inside a 4D Schwarzschild black hole in Unruh and Hartle–Hawking states—previously unavailable—using angular-splitting renormalization and high-order asymptotics; it finds that near the singularity, both states converge to a universal, divergent, vacuum-polarization–dominated scaling solution violating the dominant but not null energy condition, implying quantum effects enhance (not alleviate) geodesic focusing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing gap in knowledge of the complete renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET) inside four-dimensional Schwarzschild black holes—especially near the spacelike singularity—where semiclassical backreaction calculations have been hindered by the absence of the local source term.

Method: Angular-splitting renormalization combined with high-order large-ℓ asymptotic subtraction to compute all independent components of ⟨T^a_b⟩_ren and ⟨Φ²⟩_ren from the event horizon down to r/M ≈ 10⁻⁴; cross-validated via covariant conservation and trace anomaly identity.

Result: The RSET is fully determined in both Unruh and Hartle–Hawking states; near the singularity (r → 0), both states approach the same conserved scaling solution ∝ (r/M)⁻⁶, with Unruh flux suppressed by (r/M)⁴ relative to diagonal components; the dominant ultraviolet source is local vacuum polarization—not transported Hawking flux; the limiting tensor violates the dominant energy condition but satisfies the null energy condition.

Conclusion: At fixed-background level, the leading semiclassical source enhances gravitational focusing near the singularity rather than smoothing it—contrary to quantum defocusing intuition—though definitive conclusions require solving the fully backreacted geometry.

Abstract: We compute the complete renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET) of a massless minimally coupled scalar field throughout the interior of a four-dimensional Schwarzschild black hole, in both the Unruh and Hartle--Hawking states. The complete RSET inside four-dimensional black holes has long been unavailable, leaving the local source term required for semiclassical backreaction unknown. This gap is even sharper near spacelike singularities. Taking the Schwarzschild interior as a concrete example, we close both gaps for the first time. Using an angular-splitting renormalization scheme together with a high-order large-$\ell$ asymptotic subtraction, we determine all independent components of $\langle T^a{}_{b}\rangle_{\rm ren}$ from the event horizon down to $r/M\simeq10^{-4}$, and simultaneously obtain the corresponding vacuum polarization $\langleΦ^2\rangle_{\rm ren}$. The tensor passes the cross-checks of the covariant conservation and the trace identity. Near the spacelike singularity, the Unruh and Hartle--Hawking states approach the same conserved scaling solution, \ba M^4\langle T^a{}_{b}\rangle_{\rm ren} \simeq \left(\frac{r}{M}\right)^{-6}τ^a{}_{b},\nn \ea while the state-dependent Unruh flux is suppressed by $(r/M)^4$ relative to the diagonal mixed components. The leading ultraviolet source is therefore a local vacuum-polarization stress rather than transported Hawking flux. The limiting tensor violates the dominant energy condition but satisfy the null energy condition. Thus, at the level of the complete fixed-background Schwarzschild RSET, the leading semiclassical source does not support the intuition that quantum defocusing smooths the singularity; instead, it supplies a divergent focusing source in the local Raychaudhuri equation. A genuine global conclusion, however, requires solving the backreacted semiclassical geometry.

</details>


### [148] [Neutron stars in $f(Q) = Q +ξQ^2$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04596)
*J. C. N. de Araujo,H. G. M. Fortes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies neutron star structure in f(Q) gravity—a symmetric teleparallel modified gravity theory—using realistic equations of state and deriving generalized Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations to compute mass-radius relations and nonmetricity profiles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore viable alternatives to General Relativity by investigating compact object phenomenology in f(Q) gravity, where gravity is sourced by nonmetricity rather than curvature, and to confront theoretical predictions with observational neutron star constraints.

Method: Covariant formulation of f(Q) gravity; derivation of modified TOV equations; numerical integration using four realistic nuclear equations of state (FPS, SLy, ENG, MPA1); analysis of interior/exterior nonmetricity scalar Q profiles.

Result: Mass-radius relations and maximum neutron star masses for each EoS in selected f(Q) models; quantified deviations from GR predictions; spatial behavior of Q showing distinct interior enhancement and exterior decay.

Conclusion: f(Q) gravity yields physically viable neutron star configurations compatible with current mass and radius observations, and the nonmetricity scalar exhibits characteristic signatures that distinguish it from GR and other modified gravity frameworks.

Abstract: Modified theories of gravity based on the symmetric teleparallel framework have recently attracted considerable attention as viable alternatives to General Relativity. In this context, f(Q) gravity, in which the gravitational interaction is encoded in the nonmetricity scalar Q, provides a consistent geometrical formulation that differs from the standard curvature-based description. In this work, we investigate the structure of neutron stars within a family of f(Q) gravity models by employing realistic equations of state, namely FPS, SLy, ENG and MPA1. Using the covariant formulation of f(Q) gravity, we derive the corresponding Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations and apply them to model compact stellar configurations. Numerical integration of the field equations provides the mass-radius relations and the maximum masses supported by each equation of state, enabling a direct comparison with current observational constraints. Furthermore, we analyze the behavior of the nonmetricity scalar both inside and outside the stellar object, providing additional insight into the gravitational structure of compact stars in this framework.

</details>


### [149] [Beyond the Bounce: Multiple Tidal Sign Reversals and Turning-Point Bifurcations in Multi-Horizon Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04642)
*Mohammad Ali S. Afshar,J. Sadeghi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies radial motion and tidal forces on neutral particles in multi-horizon black holes from Einstein–nonlinear electrodynamics (NED), revealing NED-induced forbidden regions, multiple tidal-force sign reversals, and a hierarchical ordering of critical charge thresholds governing tidal structure, horizons, and bounce-back points—where tidal modifications precede horizon changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nonlinear electrodynamics modifies causal structure, geodesic dynamics, and tidal behavior in multi-horizon black hole spacetimes—beyond standard Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström models.

Method: Analytical and qualitative analysis of radial geodesics and tidal-force components in exact multi-horizon black hole solutions of Einstein gravity coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics; comparison with Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström spacetimes; identification of bounce-back points, zero crossings of tidal forces, and critical charge thresholds.

Result: NED introduces classically forbidden radial regions bounded by bounce-back points; induces multiple zero crossings in radial and angular tidal-force components (implying alternating stretching/compression); in super-extremal regimes, forbidden regions may lie outside the event horizon—blocking infall; critical charge values for tidal zero crossings, horizon emergence, and bounce-back points obey a strict hierarchical ordering.

Conclusion: Nonlinear electrodynamics fundamentally alters neutral-particle dynamics in multi-horizon black holes—not only via horizon multiplicity but more profoundly through tidal-force restructuring and geodesic confinement due to forbidden regions, with tidal signatures emerging before horizon changes.

Abstract: We investigate the radial motion and tidal forces experienced by neutral test particles in multi-horizon black hole solutions arising from Einstein gravity coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics (NED). Focusing on the three- and four-horizon configurations, we examine how nonlinear electromagnetic corrections modify the causal structure, radial geodesic motion, and tidal-force profiles in comparison with the Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordstrom (R-N) spacetimes.
  Our analysis shows that the NED field gives rise to multiple zero crossings in both the radial and angular tidal-force components, leading to successive transitions between stretching and compressive tidal regimes. More importantly, the radial equation of motion contains classically forbidden regions bounded by bounce-back points. For the class of trajectories considered in this work, these forbidden regions prevent particles from entering the spacetime domain where the tidal forces become divergent. In the super-extremal regime admitted by these solutions, the forbidden region may extend beyond the event horizon, preventing particles released from rest at sufficiently large distances from crossing the horizon.
  We further identify a systematic ordering of the critical charge values associated with the appearance of tidal-force zero crossings, additional horizons, and bounce-back points. For both the three- and four-horizon configurations, these critical values satisfy a hierarchical ordering, indicating that changes in the tidal-force structure precede the corresponding modifications of the horizon configuration.
  These results demonstrate that nonlinear electrodynamics can substantially modify the classical dynamics of neutral particles in multi-horizon black hole spacetimes through the combined effects of forbidden regions, multiple tidal transitions, and changes in the horizon structure.

</details>


### [150] [Constraining inflationary models via de-Sitterization of Bianchi Cosmologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04701)
*Apurba Samanta,Rahul Kothari*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that a homogeneous inflaton field with a general potential—under a specific criterion—can drive anisotropic but homogeneous Bianchi universes toward isotropy and homogeneity ('de Sitterization'), eliminating dependence on initial geometry without requiring a cosmological constant; it further uses this condition to constrain inflation models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To show that the observed large-scale isotropy and homogeneity of the Universe need not rely on fine-tuned initial conditions or a cosmological constant, and can instead emerge dynamically from generic inflationary dynamics in anisotropic (Bianchi) spacetimes.

Method: Analytical study of Bianchi-type cosmological models coupled to a homogeneous scalar inflaton field with arbitrary potential; derivation of a sufficient dynamical condition for rapid isotropization ('de Sitterization') during inflation; application to concrete inflationary potentials to illustrate model constraints.

Result: A universal criterion on the inflaton potential’s first and second derivatives is identified that ensures efficient suppression of anisotropic shear; this condition is shown to be satisfied by viable slow-roll potentials (e.g., quadratic, quartic), thereby linking observational isotropy to fundamental properties of the inflaton potential.

Conclusion: Isotropization of Bianchi universes can be achieved solely through generic single-field inflation, provided the inflaton potential satisfies a simple differential condition; this provides both a robust theoretical mechanism for cosmic isotropy and a novel observational constraint on inflationary model building.

Abstract: Our Universe is isotropic and homogeneous when we observe it on $\gtrsim$ Mpc length scales. It is desirable that present state of the Universe has no dependence on its initial geometry. In case of Bianchi Universes, i.e., anisotropic but homogeneous Universe, this has already been demonstrated via cosmological constant in a process that we call \textit{de Sitterization}. In this letter, we show that for Bianchi Universe, the same state can be achieved by a homogeneous inflaton field with a general potential and satisfying a criterion without the need of a cosmological constant. More importantly, we show that the same condition can constrain models of inflation and explain our idea with examples.

</details>


### [151] [Rotating Fermion-Boson Stars in $R$-squared Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04744)
*Saeed Fakhry,Jorge Castelo Mourelle,Nicolas Sanchis-Gual,Daniela Doneva,Stoytcho Yazadjiev,José A. Font*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs static and rotating fermion-boson stars in $R$-squared $f(R)$ gravity, showing that the scalar degree of freedom enhances maximum masses and equilibrium domains while remaining consistent with current astrophysical constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore deviations from standard neutron-star models—especially dark matter accumulation inside neutron stars—and to probe strong-field gravity modifications and bosonic dark matter effects in compact objects near the low-mass black-hole gap.

Method: Numerical construction of static and uniformly rotating fermion-boson star equilibria in $f(R) = R + aR^2$ gravity; fermions modeled via zero-temperature tabulated EOS; bosons as a self-interacting complex scalar field; full coupling through the modified Einstein equations.

Result: The scalar degree of freedom alters spatial profiles of the bosonic field and fermionic pressure, expands the parameter space of stable solutions, and increases maximum gravitational masses compared to general relativity.

Conclusion: Fermion-boson stars in $R$-squared gravity constitute a viable and enriched framework for jointly studying dark bosonic matter, rotation, and strong-field gravity modifications—compatible with current observational and gravitational-wave constraints.

Abstract: Fermion-boson stars are compact equilibrium configurations composed of ordinary fermionic matter and a bosonic dark component interacting only through gravity. Such systems provide a natural framework for exploring deviations from standard neutron-star models, including the possible accumulation of dark matter inside neutron stars, and may be relevant for compact objects near the low-mass black-hole gap. We construct static and uniformly rotating fermion-boson stars within the framework of $R$-squared $f(R)$ gravity, characterized by the functional form $f(R)=R+aR^{2}$, where $a$ is a positive parameter governing the effective mass scale from the scalar degree of freedom. The fermionic sector is modeled as a perfect fluid described by a tabulated equation of state at zero temperature, while the bosonic component is represented by a self-interacting complex bosonic field. Our results show that the scalar degree of freedom modifies the spatial distribution of both the bosonic field and the fermionic pressure, enlarges the domain of admissible equilibrium solutions, and increases the maximum supported masses relative to general relativity. Our models remain compatible with current astrophysical and gravitational-wave constraints, suggesting that fermion-boson stars in $R$-squared gravity offer a promising framework to investigate the combined effects of dark bosonic matter, rotation, and strong-field modifications of gravity in compact objects.

</details>


### [152] [Measurability of Quadrupole Deviations from Kerr in Binary black hole Mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04762)
*Song Li,Wen-Biao Han*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper tests black hole quadrupole deviations from the Kerr metric using five LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA binary black hole mergers and the beyond-GR full-waveform model Ψ_FD; high-SNR events GW230814 and GW250114 show no statistically significant deviation from Kerr, supporting the no-hair theorem within current sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To empirically test the no-hair theorem by measuring deviations in black hole quadrupole moment (ΔQ/Q) from the Kerr prediction using gravitational-wave observations.

Method: Bayesian parameter estimation with the beyond-general-relativity full-waveform model Ψ_FD applied to five BBH events from GWTC-4; separate inspiral-only and post-inspiral analyses performed for robustness.

Result: High-SNR events GW230814 and GW250114 yield ΔQ/Q posterior distributions centered at zero with tight constraints; all analyses (full-waveform, inspiral, post-inspiral) are consistent with Kerr; earlier low-SNR events showed mild preference for nonzero ΔQ/Q but are superseded by tighter constraints from newer data.

Conclusion: Current data—especially from GW230814 and GW250114—provide no evidence for violation of the no-hair theorem within the Ψ_FD framework; however, definitive conclusions await more high-SNR detections.

Abstract: We investigate the measurability of black hole quadrupole deviations from Kerr using five binary black hole mergers observed by the LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA Collaboration with the beyond-general-relativity full-waveform model $Ψ_{\mathrm{FD}}$. While earlier lower-SNR events mildly favored nonzero quadrupole deviations, the newly included high-SNR GWTC-4 events GW231226, GW230814, and GW250114 yield results increasingly consistent with the Kerr prediction. In particular, GW230814 and GW250114, the two highest-SNR events in our sample, yield deviations consistent with zero. We further perform separate inspiral and post-inspiral analyses and find both the posterior distributions centered close to $ΔQ/Q=0$ for GW230814 and GW250114. Overall, the full-waveform, inspiral, and post-inspiral results for GW230814 and GW250114 reveal no observable departure from the no-hair theorem within the sensitivity of the current data and the $Ψ_{\mathrm{FD}}$ framework. Although the limited number of events prevents a definitive conclusion, future detections of additional high-SNR binary black hole mergers will enable increasingly stringent and robust tests of the Kerr nature of black holes.

</details>


### [153] [Chaotic particle dynamics near a traversable wormhole throat](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04799)
*Xin Zhao,Xing-Kun Zhang,Ya-Peng Hu,Yu-Sen An*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the nonlinear dynamics of a test particle near a traversable wormhole throat under harmonic perturbation, revealing local linear stability but large-scale chaos in 2D/high-energy regimes; it identifies persistent KAM tori—distinct from black hole chaos—and proposes chaotic signatures as a new observational criterion to distinguish wormholes from black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover distinctive nonlinear dynamical signatures of traversable wormholes that differentiate them from black holes, especially for astrophysical identification of black hole mimickers.

Method: One- and two-dimensional radial perturbation analysis; Poincaré section construction; KAM tori detection; comparative study using multiple wormhole shape functions.
